Monitors (`marathon-ls.sh`, `marathon-detail.sh`, and `marathon-tui.sh` by delegation) get a **dual-path lookup, not a swap**, keeping two populations visible — this repo's un-migrated pre-flip runs, and fleet repos whose vendored `.xyz/` has not re-synced and is still writing to `phases/` — with the newer file winning, an invariant separately falsified against a naive "check the new dir, return if found" variant. `test/marathon-root-audit.sh` now probes both names so a `.gitignore` rule on either is caught before it crashes a phase mid-record, and **it immediately flagged the new test's own `env`-array invocation as unscoped**, which was correct. GH-308 exception process followed for the one frozen twin edited. → [#484](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/484)

### Added
- **Release 0.2.0 Litmus reaches RELEASE CANDIDATE — the exit criterion is met by a command, not by a judgement.** `bash test/litmus-release.sh --release-gate` on `development` @ `263816c`: **`GOALPOST MET — all 6 manifest entries complete`** (6/6, 0 remaining, 0 false completion claims). The frozen manifest — #375, #390, #407, #417, #457, #461 — is a fixed denominator rather than a percentage, and it did not move: #457, #460, #461 and #472 were all *filed* while executing Litmus and none of them were admitted, which is what a frozen boundary looks like in practice. **The gate's own negative control was re-run on the same commit** and reports `negative control OBSERVED in both directions (6 pass, 0 fail)` — it detects a stripped declaration, an unregistered gate (the #461 defect, caught against the gate itself), and an invariant violated in either direction. Full `validate.sh` green on the RC commit. **Two of the six issues stay OPEN on purpose.** #390's gate is registered, green and control-observed, but a host free-memory floor and packet-driven per-phase overrides are deferred to a Phase 2 its own code names at `marathon_drive.py:1253`; #375's shipped three-state `unverifiable` verdict deliberately contradicts its criteria 1 and 5, because implementing them literally made a TTY error fatal and took `test/relay-self-sufficiency.sh` from 4/0 to **0/4** on a machine where agy was signed in and working. Both are audited per-criterion on the issues rather than closed quietly — closing them would have repeated the exact #401→#461 mistake this release exists to catch, in the release that exists to catch it. The exit criterion measures *gates and observed controls*; it has never claimed to be a proxy for issue closure, and this is that distinction doing its job.

### Fixed
- **The tier1 "flake" was never flaky — it was a SIGPIPE race against the pipe buffer, and it was blocking every open PR.** `test/pdda-local-checks.sh:127` did `printf '%s' "$real_out" | grep -Fq "SUMMARY [...]"`. `grep -q` exits the instant it matches, so once `$real_out` outgrew the 64KB pipe buffer `printf` was still writing when the reader vanished — SIGPIPE — and under `_setup.sh`'s `set -o pipefail` that became the *pipeline's* status, breaking the `&&` chain and failing an assertion **whose subject had not changed**. That is the whole of #460's pass/fail/fail/pass/fail across five consecutive runs on identical code: whether `printf` finished first was a race against a buffer every new `3-COMPLETED` doc pushed it further past, and it had finally tipped over into failing consistently. Proven rather than assumed — same string, same match, `set -o pipefail`: 43 bytes → rc `0`, 200,044 bytes → rc **`141`** (128+13). The CI log carried the confession directly (`printf: write error: Broken pipe`) with all three SUMMARY lines present in the very output the failure claimed a check had not produced. Fixed by grepping a file instead of a pipe, and the failure message now **names which summary is missing** — it previously dumped the whole run and named nothing, which is why this was twice misread as the unrelated GH-268 frontmatter WARN sitting at the top of its own dump. Scoped to the one site with evidence: the shape appears in **366 files** and is harmless below the buffer, so the latent class is filed as #472 rather than blind-swept or ignored. → [#460](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/460) · [#472](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/472)

### Changed
- **The Litmus wave-1 doc sweep dropped from three docs to two, and the ROADMAP's GH-417 entry turned out to be describing someone else's work.** `GH-343` and `GH-418` moved to `3-COMPLETED` (both issues CLOSED). **`GH-425` was pulled back out**: it shipped, closed on per-criterion verification, and was reopened the same day when salvage triage from the abandoned `agy/gh375-385-438-wip` worktree (#458) found a **second call site the fix never reached** — `marathon_drive.py`'s `--log-github` handler resolves `repo` and `issue` independently and compares no slug at all. Sweeping it would have been a false completion claim. Separately, the ROADMAP entry for **GH-417 carried GH-425's text verbatim** — it read `✅ BUILT` and then described `check_source_url` acting on the repo slug, claiming one lane was finished while describing another; it now describes #417's own resolution.

## 2026-08-08

### Added
- **Release 0.2.0 Litmus, first wave: all 4 lanes BUILT, agy-approved and gated — `marathon complete — all 4 phase(s) approved`.** 888 insertions across 14 files. Gate times 739s / 715s / 704s / 708s against a 900s wall cap, peak RSS ≤1002MB of 8192MB. Every lane was reviewed by agy (never the model that wrote it) and gated on the FULL suite, not a narrowed one. **`gh419`** — the instrument: `GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md` + `utils/py/gate_inventory.py` + `test/gh419-gate-inventory.sh`, a machine-readable per-gate inventory recording whether a negative control has been *observed* and in what form. **`gh343`** — a target-relative gate program now resolves against `target_root` rather than the process cwd, so an identical contract gives an identical verdict from any directory. **`gh418`** — the issue-state and GH-308 FROZEN-banner checks preflight never had, plus a third phase it grew from its own review (below). **`gh425`** — `check_source_url` acts on the repo slug it already computed, in both directions, with `wrong-repository`/`mismatch` classifications and remediation pointing at `related:`. **Three lanes' worth of findings came from running the thing rather than reading it**, which is the entire argument of #419 and is recorded lane by lane in ROADMAP.md.
- **GH-343's first two builds failed because the ACCEPTANCE CRITERION was wrong, not the builder.** Criterion 3 — *"a gate script **or** program that exists but is not executable sets NOT-READY, rather than passing readiness and failing at execution time"* — has a false premise for the interpreter branch: `bash foo.sh` runs a mode-644 file perfectly well, because the interpreter *reads* the script and never `exec`s it. Two independent codex builds implemented it literally, adding `os.access(script_path, os.X_OK)` to that branch, and both regressed the lane's own gate **`test/swarm-preflight.sh` 98/0 → 91/7** (`T15`/`T33`/`T36`, `expected exit 0, got 5`; `T15`'s fixture gate is `bash -x src/a.js` over a mode-644 file). **Satisfying it by `chmod +x`-ing the fixtures would have been worse than failing**: it ships the rule "an interpreter-invoked gate must carry the executable bit", which reports NOT-READY for `gate: "bash validate.sh"` against any repo whose `validate.sh` is 644 while that gate runs fine — a NEW false NOT-READY, the exact defect the issue exists to remove. Criterion 3 was narrowed **on the issue** (operator-approved) to programs that are *executed directly*; the third build then produced the correct split first try — `isfile` alone on the interpreter branch, `isfile` + `X_OK` on the separator-containing program branch. Same builder, same lane, same brief structure; the only variable that changed was the criterion. The criterion's false premise had already survived an adversarial codex+agy review *of the criteria* on 2026-08-06 — what caught it was building the lane and running its gate.

### Fixed
- **GH-457: the gate's caps came from bare constants that were smaller than the gate itself.** The wall/CPU caps the gate guard enforced were hardcoded and never checked against what a real gate actually costs — a full-suite gate that legitimately needs ~957s wall / ~533s CPU was being run under caps set for something much cheaper, so the guard's job became killing correct work. Caps now derive from a declared **tier** (`GATE_TIERS`: `fast` 300s/240s, `full` 1800s/1200s, default `full`), an unknown tier falls back loudly rather than silently, and a per-variable env override still wins over the tier. RSS is deliberately left untiered and unchanged. `test/gh457-gate-tiers.sh` **10/0**, asserting the default tier clears the *worst observed real* gate runtime rather than an invented number; the control mutates the registry, requires the resolved cap to follow, and restores it. → [#457](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/457)
- **GH-407: `pre-advance-failed` claimed a gate verdict for phases where the gate never ran.** Several unrelated failures reach the same relay exit 5 — a builder shim that failed to start, a builder that exhausted its turn cap, a reviewer turn discarded by containment — and every one of them was escalated with the label that means *the gate ran and rejected the change*. Observed three times in one 10-lane marathon on 2026-08-02, wrong all three times; in one case the relay file read `STATUS: Approved` while the phase was reported as though the gate had failed it. It costs real time because the reason is the operator's entry point: `pre-advance-failed` sends them to read a diff and test output that does not exist. Every escalation now records `gate: not-run|green|red` — **on every reason, not only the gate-related ones**, so the line resolves the ambiguity even where the reason taxonomy is incomplete — and exit 5 without a gate run escalates as `relay-failed-before-gate`. **Deliberately narrower than the issue asked:** it suggested three distinct reasons (builder-failed, cap-exhausted, reviewer-containment), and the driver has no channel telling it which cause occurred — #408 is that same missing channel from the other side — so a label that guessed would trade one confident wrong answer for another. `test/gh407-gate-ran-attribution.sh` **7/0**, including the assertion that protects the other direction: a gate that **did** run and failed is still `pre-advance-failed` with `gate: red`, because reserving the label is easy to over-apply and every other criterion would still pass while real gate failures stopped being reported as failures. Control: the pre-fix revision replayed inside the fixture reproduces the mislabel with no `gate:` line at all. → [#407](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/407)
- **GH-417: the tree asserted both that `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` is the right ROOT default and that it is the bug "caught live" — the answer is a commit nobody had connected, and Marathon Plan K was two days early.** `utils/py/rtl.py:resolve_turn_root` defaults ROOT to `--show-toplevel` (GH-296); `relay-turn-lib.sh`'s GH-160 collapse carried a comment calling that construct the bug. Nothing reconciled them and no test could tell them apart, so the failure mode — exit 6, a legitimate in-allowlist edit reverted as a containment violation — could not be attributed to anything. **What actually resolved it: `312a2c3` (2026-07-21, GH-261), whose own commit message names `test/marathon-drive.sh`'s GH-171/GH-172 cases** — the exact failures Plan K measured on **2026-07-19** and explained by blaming `--show-toplevel`. GH-261 canonicalizes **both** `RTL_ROOT` and each absolute allowlist entry to physical form before stripping, so from that commit on either symlink form resolves. Both statements were true of different things: the GH-160 collapse runs *before* that normalization and must not change `RTL_ROOT`'s form, while `resolve_turn_root` runs after it. Both files now say so and point at each other; **the "caught live" sentence was kept, not deleted** — it is a real constraint on the GH-160 collapse, and only its scope was ever ambiguous. New `test/gh417-turn-root-symlink-prefix.sh`, **13/0**: a Python turn shim with `AGY_TURN_ROOT` **unset**, driven from a repo reached through a symlinked prefix, is not reverted. **The control is what makes it evidence** — reverting GH-261's canonicalization in a fixture copy brings exit 6 straight back with the original symptom in words (`OFF-ALLOWLIST change: relay.md — reverting`), and the *same reverted tree* passes when ROOT is pinned to the logical form, which is what identifies ROOT's symlink form as the axis rather than the fixture. Two traps sidestepped: the fixture builds its **own** `link -> phys` symlink instead of trusting `$TMPDIR` (macOS gives `/var -> /private/var` free, Linux CI does not, and a sandbox can hand back an already-physical `$TMPDIR` — the shape would vanish and every assertion would pass for the wrong reason, so the test asserts the shape first), and the **Python lane is proven** by a `python3` wrapper on `PATH` that records it ran, never certifying the frozen Bash twins as a proxy. **Comments only** in both files; the five GH-308-frozen Bash shims untouched. `marathon-drive` 144/0, `agy-turn` 62/0, `worktree-isolation` 33/0, `relay-target-root` 12/0. → [#417](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/417)
- **`swarm-preflight` crashed outright on any binary file under `test/` — found by agy mid-marathon, in code it was not asked to review.** `expand_effective_artifacts`'s inner `read()` helper (`utils/py/swarm_preflight.py:366`) did `open(..., "r")` with `except OSError:` only, while walking the entire `test/` tree to infer tests. One binary fixture there raised an uncaught `UnicodeDecodeError` and killed preflight before it could render any verdict — reproduced with a single probe file: `'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0xff in position 3`, at `:1049 main → expand_effective_artifacts → :385 → :369`. **The tell was an asymmetry inside the same file:** codex's own new `find_frozen_artifacts` (`:631`), written two hundred lines away in the same turn, already used `encoding="utf-8"` + `except (OSError, UnicodeError)`; the pre-existing helper beside it did not. Now hardened and pinned by a binary fixture asserting both exit 0 **and** the absence of `UnicodeDecodeError` in the output — an exit-code assertion alone would pass if the error were merely swallowed. Weight: preflight is the tool that decides marathon-readiness, `/10days` Step 6 treats any non-zero exit as "drop this issue", and #343/#418/#425 are all about it returning *wrong* verdicts — a hard crash on a routine repo state outranks a wrong verdict. Folded into the gh418 lane by operator decision rather than deferred, because it is one line in a file already in that lane's write-set.
- **A reviewer had no sanctioned place to write, so proving a finding destroyed the turn that found it.** agy discovered the crash above and wrote `dummy.py` + `test/binary/` in-tree to demonstrate it — the same probe used to confirm it. Both are off-allowlist, so containment reverted them and failed the turn with exit 6 (correct per GH-410 case 3: a genuine off-lane *write* must still fail). The verdict survived only because `RELAY.md` happens to be on the reviewer's allowlist; written anywhere else it would have been lost, and the crash with it. **Containment is unchanged and not weakened** — instead the rendered reviewer prompt now carries instruction 4b: write probe files under `$TMPDIR`, never in the working tree, with the reason and this incident stated inline. Verified on the next two lanes: agy reviewed both with zero containment failures, and on `gh425` reported *"I executed `test/gh425-source-url-slug.sh` outside the test tree in an isolated `$TMPDIR` and all assertions passed"* — full verification, no violation. **Python lane only:** `relay-automation/marathon-drive.sh` is a GH-308 frozen twin and is byte-unchanged (`gh308-frozen-twin-guard` 32/0, `marathon-drive` 142/0).
- **GH-397 — the reviewer's containment boundary was about to be moved onto an honor system the repo had already proven does not hold (#456, `ed937d8`).** `NEXT:` carries two incompatible value domains: `/relay` writes a ROLE (`Producer`/`Reviewer`), marathon-drive writes an AGENT ID, and the shared reader `rtl_is_reviewer_turn` understood only the former — so on every marathon reviewer turn it returned false and the reviewer kept the full `ALLOW_PATHS` allowlist over the artifact it was reviewing, which is the 2026-06-20 agy over-reach. The first fix asked the acting model, in prompt prose, to rewrite the header itself. **That was rejected in review on evidence already in the tree:** `test/agy-turn.sh` (S2) exists specifically to pin *"KNOWN GAP reproduced — NEXT was NOT flipped and nothing caught it"*, and the failure direction is OPEN — one forgotten flip silently restores artifact write authority. The role is now DERIVED from marathon-drive's machine-readable role directive plus `RELAY_AGENT`, which relay-drive exports from the **tick token** — two pieces of state no agent authors. Plain `/relay` threads, hand-run turns, and pre-directive relays fall through to the historical `NEXT:` test byte-for-byte; a `builder == reviewer` self-review lane carries no role signal and deliberately falls through rather than guessing. **Both lanes fixed by one change** — `utils/py/rtl.py` shells out to the same function and `relay-turn-lib.sh` is not a frozen twin, so the Bash fleet gets it too. `test/gh397-reviewer-turn-role.sh` (11 asserts) was confirmed RED against the pre-fix tree before being trusted green, on the case the whole design exists for: the reviewer stays scoped when the builder never flipped `NEXT:` at all.
- **GH-401 — `--dry-run` mutated the repo, and the audit written to catch exactly that class reported PASS the entire time (#456, `ed937d8`).** The relay write and `os.makedirs(phase_dir)` both now sit below the dry-run exit, so a dry run touches the filesystem not at all; the render is emitted to stdout between explicit fences instead, so the flag still shows its work. **The more useful half was the guard:** `test/marathon-root-audit.sh` is the GH-209 guard for *"every test invocation of the marathon driver is `MARATHON_ROOT`-scoped"*, and its scope was the two hardcoded filenames `marathon.sh` and `marathon-drive.sh` — `test/gh268-relay-cue-and-target-checks.sh` was out of its reach because of its FILENAME, not because it was safe. Widened to audit every test script, it immediately surfaced a **third** unscoped invocation nobody had reported, `test/gh322-runlog-python-lane.sh:270`; coverage went 2 invocations → 40, all scoped. **Two existing tests had encoded the defect as the contract** and had to be corrected, not deleted: `marathon-drive.sh` case (1) asserted `[ -f phases/p1/RELAY.md ]` *after* a dry run — the suite would have failed if the bug were fixed — and `debug-mantra.sh` used the dry-run write as its render-inspection mechanism (a legitimate need: it must render without driving a phase or disturbing `.tick/attempts/<lane>`, the counter each of its cases hand-seeds), so it reads the fenced render from stdout instead. `test/gh401-dry-run-no-mutation.sh` carries a positive control so "nothing was written" cannot pass for the wrong reason, and aims at a throwaway `MARATHON_ROOT` so a future regression can never dirty the real repo just by running the suite — which is how the polluted file got committed in the first place. **The headline claim measured rather than asserted: `git status --porcelain` is now byte-identical before and after a full `validate.sh` run** (159 tests, exit 0; CI `tier1` pass).

- **GH-442 · fourth `/radar` run — operator-run on `rebalance-OS`, the first non-tooling repo — found the same defect class as this repo, independently, and exposed a real defect in reconciliation.** The convergence is the headline: rebalance-OS's #2 target is `RADAR-class-guard-cannot-report-red`, the same class as this repo's #419 `guards-cant-fail`, reached in a 548-commit product codebase (collectors, Swift app, web server) that shares no application code with xyz. Two of its three top clusters are the same *shapes* found in tooling repos with entirely domain-specific membership — memory ceilings, SQLite lock contention, 2.65 M orphaned vectors. Strongest evidence yet that the taxonomy generalizes rather than describing xyz's quirks, and the first concrete argument for the fleet roll-up GH-442 deliberately deferred (a class recurring independently in two repos is a fact no single-repo radar can observe). **Two of this session's fixes were independently confirmed:** the concentrated-authoring discriminator correctly excluded GH-257 (16 fix commits, one day, one issue — the repo's hottest seam by raw count), and the tally-must-sum guardrail proved under-specified when that run hit **RTK truncating `git log` to exactly 50 lines for both windows of a 548-commit repo** — a silent cap that would have bucketed 50 commits and reported a confidently wrong distribution with no visible symptom; the skill now also cross-checks `git rev-list --count`. **Five fixes folded in, one a genuine defect:** (1) **signal 6, operational evidence** — a product repo can prove a class is firing *now*, and that run's top target was confirmed by `refusing to start: memory compressor holds 21.1 GB` in 5 of the last 9 nightly logs, a signal neither tooling repo possessed; (2) **the defect — symptom-masking on strike-through**: its store-bloat class is a plausible cause of its unbounded-ceiling class, so shipping the reclaim work would have silenced the ceiling's symptom while its defect stayed put, and the next run would have struck the target through and reset its aging clock — strike-through now requires a commit that *names the seam*, and an unexplained quiet target is annotated as possible masking instead; (3) a **missing-colon prefix family** (`fix(GH-169) Phase 2: …`) dropped four correctly-typed commits, now checked by name and reported as a source-fixable measurement defect rather than silently compensated; (4) **Lens 3 fallback** when `Milestone:` is empty or no milestones exist — read claim status from `Description:` prose and report a 100% orphan share as a *missing binding*, not backlog drift; (5) **three-way signal-yield reporting** — parser failure vs. structurally unavailable vs. available-and-genuinely-empty (81 closed issues with zero doc-only closes is a real negative result, not a gap). → [GH-442-RADAR.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-442-RADAR.md) · [#442](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/442)
- **GH-442 · `/radar` installed system-wide, and its reconciliation path executed for the first time — the substantive finding is that 63 commits changed nothing the radar tracks.** Run 3 is the first dispatched through the **installed** skill (`~/.claude/skills/radar` symlink) rather than read by hand, and the first run from a **clean worktree** cut from `origin/development` @ `f95eefc`, so neither the main checkout's uncommitted work nor a concurrent operator session in another repo could contaminate the read. **Reconciliation works:** across a 63-commit delta (Litmus marathon, PR #443) both targets matched by ID and carried forward at `runs: 2`, no duplicate radar issue was opened, and a run-delta comment landed on [#444](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/444). **What it found is the aging signal doing its job:** the flow mix moved ~1 point (Run 71%→71%, Grow 4%→5%, denominator 260→276), `relay-automation/xyz-vendor.sh` was untouched by all 63 commits so `RADAR-ensure-gitignore` sits at 44 days and counting, #438/#439 are still assigned to no milestone, and the orphan share is identical at 67 — while a **third** release band (Plumbline 0.4.0) was added. Planning is extending forward faster than the existing bands absorb the field reports already in hand, which is run 1's Nightwatch-drift finding sharpened by a second data point rather than a new one. **Three fixes earned, each from a real failure:** (1) run 1 had already taken `RADAR-REPORT-2026-08-07.md`, so a same-day rerun collides — "never edit a prior report" outranks one-doc-per-date, and an Nth same-day run now appends `-runN`; (2) **a `tail` view of a piped commit tally returned counts contradicting the `head` view of the same pipeline** (shell-wrapper output mangling), which would have silently corrupted Lens 1 — the skill now writes subjects to a file via `/usr/bin/git` and asserts the bucket sum equals the raw line count before reporting anything, because an unproven tally is precisely the reads-as-authoritative-while-wrong failure this tool exists to catch; (3) `rgt:` adoption is **0 docs repo-wide**, so "Transform 0%" has always meant *nobody declared anything* rather than *no transformative work happened* — adoption is now printed beside the figure instead of left to the reader. **Still unexercised, and named rather than glossed:** the strike-through branch — no target has been fixed between any two runs, so "seam went quiet → strike through citing the fixing commit" has never executed. → [GH-442-RADAR.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-442-RADAR.md) · [#442](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/442) · [#444](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/444)
- **GH-442 · second `/radar` calibration run, on `giant-brains-claude-skills` — the no-targets guardrail fired and nothing was written, which is the outcome that most needed proving.** Phase 1's QA gate is now passed: two runs on structurally opposite repos (438 commits vs 21), **whose sharpest signals turned out to be exact inverses** — `related:` kinship decided run 1 and yielded nothing in run 2; per-commit seam heat was flat in run 1 and carried run 2. That inversion is the finding that most changes the design: signal precision is repo-dependent, so the skill now measures per-signal yield instead of trusting a fixed ranking. **One fix was a genuine defect in the radar itself:** signal 1 read 2 docs carrying `related:` and extracted **0 references in silence**, because that repo uses the scalar-filename shape (`related: GH-8-FOO.md`) rather than the block-array-of-`#refs` shape — the radar exhibited the exact *reads-as-active-while-nothing-runs* class it was built to detect (#419's family). Yield reporting is now mandatory: `M=0 while N>0` must be announced as a parser failure, never as an absent signal. **The most consequential fix is the recurrence discriminator.** 13 of 21 window commits were `fix(install-improve-audit)` — an overwhelming apparent top target until the dates are checked: all 13 landed on one day, all from PR #10. That is a skill being written, not a defect recurring, and the mirror image of run 1's #308 problem (citation count conflating kinship with context). A hot seam now qualifies only if its fixes span **≥2 calendar days AND ≥2 originating PRs**; otherwise it is labeled concentrated authoring and excluded. Also: Lens 3 skips installer-seed `RELEASES.md` blocks (that repo's only block is the literal EXAMPLE placeholder — correct output is silence, per §GH-381's do-not-treat-sparse-as-incomplete rule); Lens 1 now prints Unclassified subjects verbatim with an adjusted read beside the mechanical one, after component-as-type prefixes (`stay-focused: add session-anchor skill`) made inference report **5% Grow where the honest read was 19%**, a 4x undercount; signal 4 is declared structurally unavailable at zero closed issues rather than reported as a clean sweep; and the degradation table gained rows for zero-closed-issues and for repos younger than two windows. Read-only throughout — nothing was written into the audited repo. → [GH-442-RADAR.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-442-RADAR.md) · [#442](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/442)
- **GH-442 · first `/radar` calibration run on this repo — the skill survived contact with its own subject, at the price of four fixes it wrote into itself.** Full three-lens pass, window 2026-07-17..2026-08-07. **Flow:** Run 71% / Grow 4% / Transform 0% / Unclassified 25% over a 260-commit RGT denominator (178 harness commits excluded); trend vs the prior window is the headline — **Grow collapsed 15%→4%** while Run rose 54%→71%, read as the Litmus pivot executing, not drift. **Targets:** the #419 class (13 kinship citations) reported as *claimed by Litmus* — context, not recommendation; **RADAR-ensure-gitignore** is the top unclaimed target (#18 → #314 day 34 → #440 day 44, one function failing in both directions, 8-repo blast radius, score ≈24); **RADAR-class-foreign-repo-field-gaps** (#312/#438/#439) — 5 field reports in 10 days from 4 distinct vendored repos, none claimed by any band. **Lens 3:** Litmus aligned; **Nightwatch drifting** — the field is filing its theme early and the band claims none of it; orphan share 67/98 open issues (68%). **The four calibration fixes, each from a real divergence:** (1) `related:` citation counts conflate defect kinship with infrastructure context — #308 drew 11 citations, *all* FROZEN-twin contract references, and a naive tally would have ranked it the #2 defect cluster; kinship-language rule codified. (2) Harness prefixes must match as families — `relay-pkg:` leaked through the exact-match list. (3) Per-commit seam heat is flat in a squash-heavy repo (GH-432 fixed 5 shims in one commit = 1 count each) — group by issue. (4) Lens 3 gained the orphan-share line, the sharpest planning fact the run produced. **Bonus, on open question 5:** first confirmed instance of the doc-only-close recurrence predictor — #18 was created and closed doc-only within **2 hours** on 2026-06-24, and the same seam re-fired twice. Both sinks written on one confirmation: immutable [RADAR-REPORT-2026-08-07.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/RADAR-REPORT-2026-08-07.md) (evidence) + [#444](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/444) under the new `radar` label (live checklist).
