The method is validated by its own prior manual execution. Two Phase 0 findings changed the design before a line of the skill was written: **(1)** of 438 window commits, the top "prefixes" are `relay` (122) and `marathon` (43) — harness-generated turn/render commits that a naive Lens 1 tally would let swamp the signal, so the skill gained a fifth **Harness** bucket, counted but excluded from the RGT denominator (the remaining human work reads ≈94% Run / 6% Grow / 0 Transform, recognizable for a gate-repair period); **(2)** #419 is **class-shaped** (one defect class across many files) where `ensure_gitignore` is seam-shaped, settling the doc's open question 2 — clusters come in both shapes and the skill supports both ID forms (`RADAR-<seam-slug>` / `RADAR-class-<slug>`). Ships `skills/radar/SKILL.md` + `install.sh` (sibling symlink pattern): three lenses over a 21-day window, two-sink persistence (immutable dated `RADAR-REPORT-*.md` owns evidence; one `radar`-labeled issue owns the live checklist), never-re-slug alias rule for renamed seams, and a degradation table for repos missing `gh`/`PROJECT/**`/conventional commits. The doc-only-closure recurrence predictor (open question 5) is deliberately still untested — closed-issue archaeology, not rushed for the gate. Remaining Phase 1: end-to-end runs on this repo + a low-structure vendored repo. → [GH-442-RADAR.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-442-RADAR.md) · [#442](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/442)

### Fixed
- **GH-441 Phase 1 · the marathon's own pre-advance gate could not pass, because it inherited the state it was supposed to judge.** `validate.sh` is `marathon-drive`'s **default** `--pre-advance-cmd`, so it runs as a child of a live driver and inherits `RELAY_DRIVER_LOCKED=1` (`marathon-drive.sh:245`, the nested-driver re-entrancy guard — correct for its own purpose). Inherited, it tells every driver a test spawns that the lock is already held, so suites asserting real lock/heartbeat/cost-summary behaviour measured the **parent** instead of themselves: `gh284-runlog-heartbeat` 20/0 → **15/5**, `gh331-cost-summary` 8/0 → **5/3**. `oracle-guard` fails the same way on an inherited `ALLOW_PATHS`. **Every affected suite passes standalone, every time**, which is the expensive part — this halted the Litmus marathon **twice** and was misdiagnosed as flakiness both times, the second time after three clean standalone re-runs. **Both wrong turns are recorded, because they are the argument for Phase 2.** The first remedy attempted was *skipping* the two suites in the gate — narrowing a gate to make a marathon pass, in the release whose entire theme is that a check which cannot fail is not evidence. The second was a **global** `unset` in `validate.sh`, landed and then **reverted**: it was measured on a clean tree with no parent lock, the one state in which the flag is never set, and in the real nested state it merely traded 8 failing assertions for 6 (`gh322` 17/3, `gh268` 31/3) — nested drivers need the flag SET, lock assertions need it UNSET, and its commit message claimed it "removes the need to skip any suite", which was untested and false. **The fix is what a global scrub ruled out but a per-suite one did not.** Only **two** suites were ever wrong, and both drive against their own throwaway `$A` from `test/_setup.sh` — so the lock they would acquire is theirs, not the ambient repo's, and each can clear the flag *for itself* while the other ~40 driver-spawning suites keep inheriting it. That is not a new idea: `test/driver-lock.sh:11` already does exactly this, with a comment naming this failure mode ("if this test runs UNDER a marathon gate, it would inherit that export and skip the very logic it's testing"). `validate.sh` is **unchanged** and **no suite is skipped**. Measured with the flag SET **and** the driver lock HELD — the state a real marathon produces, not a clean tree: **`gh284` 20/0, `gh331` 8/0, `gh322` 29/0, `gh268` 34/0, `driver-lock` 4/0, `oracle-guard` 11/0, identical standalone — and `bash validate.sh` itself exits 0 with zero failing assertions.** That last one is the end-to-end assertion the reverted commit never made, and making it is precisely what would have caught that commit being wrong. **A second, independent defect surfaced and was deliberately not folded in:** the driver's lock block runs at `marathon-drive.sh:189` but `--help` is not parsed until `:664`, so **`--help` refuses to print whenever any driver is active** — it emits the lock-contention notice instead. It was masking one `gh284` assertion, a help-text grep that has no business touching the ambient lock; that assertion was given its own `MARATHON_ROOT` rather than weakened, and the ordering fix is filed separately because it is a driver change needing Bash+Python twin parity (GH-308). **Phase 2 is still open and is the part that lasts:** `GATE_SCRUBBED_ENV` denylists three names while the driver exports ten-plus, `_gate_env()` is the natural enforcement point but every gate defends itself instead, and a custom `--pre-advance-cmd` has nothing to source — so any hand-written gate is silently wrong, which was observed live on the same run. Phase 1 fixed two names; it did not make the boundary governed. → [GH-441-GATE-ENV-CONTRACT.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-441-GATE-ENV-CONTRACT.md) · [#441](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/441) · [#419](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/419)
- **GH-432 · a failed builder turn took the one exit that skips `rtl_enforce`, so its work was never committed and its token never handed off.** The generic-failure branch called `sys.exit(5)` two lines above the timeout branch that deliberately falls THROUGH to `enforce` — so the single case where persistence matters most was the single case that skipped the file-scoped commit, the allowlist containment check, the transcript archive, the GH-67 token handoff, and the drift signal, all at once. Nothing in the code explained the asymmetry. **Worktree isolation made the loss precise rather than partial:** `worktree_end()` has already copied the agent's allowlisted edits back into the real tree by that point, so they sit there correct and uncommitted while the exit discards the only path that would commit them — the reporter confirms the lost Round-3 patch "closely matched the fix I ended up applying by hand." **All five Python shims shared the shape** (`claude`, `codex`, `agy`, `pi`, `aider`), so the report was reachable through four builders the issue never named; fixing only the reported file would have left it live. Exit 5 is unchanged — only the side effects preceding it. **`aider-turn` needed a second change to stay correct:** its GH-278 empty-stub cleanup was gated on `bounded_rc == 7`, which was sufficient only because a generic failure could never reach a commit; widened to any non-zero exit, or the GH-432 fix would have started committing 0-byte stubs as progress — the exact regression that guard exists to prevent. **The agy review of the first fix filed one Blocker and it was correct** — three shims (`agy`, `pi`, `aider`) carry a THIRD early exit with the same defect, the "backend exited 0 but produced NO output" guard, which is the harness declaring the turn failed and which leaked exactly what the crash path leaked by a different route. That route matters more than the reported one: a sandboxed `agy` run does precisely this, so the most likely field encounter was the case the first fix left open. Corrected as agy proposed (`sys.exit(5)` → `bounded_rc = 5`, reusing the new fall-through), with a dedicated negative control confirming the two new assertions observe it (2/2 fail when only that finding is reverted). New `test/gh432-failed-turn-persist.sh`, **12/0 post-fix with a 5/4 pre-fix negative control**, asserting only side effects (commit landed, token handed off) — an exit-code assertion would have passed throughout the defect's life and proved nothing. **Two defects in the test itself were caught by running that control:** `git show HEAD` was matching the seed commit and reported PASS while nothing had been committed, and the happy-path guard, ordered last, failed on the pre-fix replay because a leaked claim from an earlier crash case starves the claim cap — a true failure with a false explanation. Both corrected; the happy-path guard now runs first, and the commit check is scoped to `$before..HEAD`. Largely subsumes **#409**; **#408** is adjacent and untouched. The issue's second suggestion — a possible `RELAY_PEER` interaction with crash handling — was checked and declined: unset `RELAY_PEER` only reaches a WARN branch that cannot fail a turn. → [GH-432-TURN-FAILURE-PERSIST.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-432-TURN-FAILURE-PERSIST.md) · [#432](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/432)

## 2026-08-06

### Added
- **Five more Litmus capture docs — #343, #358, #375, #388, #401 — all preflight READY, bringing the release to 12 gated lanes.** Each carries its issue's acceptance block verbatim (`match` at 6/7/7/7/5 criteria), a preflight contract whose probe polarity was verified against the tree, and a phase split. **#344 and #390 were deliberately excluded**: both have implementations in flight — #390's in PR #393, #344's root-resolution half already merged — and preparing them as fresh marathon lanes would queue a rebuild of existing work. **The pre-check was run first this time.** After #416 and #344 both turned out mostly-shipped, every one of these five was checked for an open PR, a branch, and a matching commit on `development` before a word was written; all five came back clean. That check costs a minute and is exactly what preflight cannot do, which is #418. **Each doc records what the codex+agy review changed about its criteria**, because the revision is often the most useful thing in it: #343's *"the two branches agree"* was satisfiable by making the **correct** branch wrong; #375's identity assertion named neither the field nor a valid response; #388's *"partial transcript"* was satisfiable by an empty pre-created file, and one of its criteria rested on a false premise about what the PDDA lint scans; #401's *"writes nothing into any git repository"* would have broken a legitimate existing test that renders into a caller-supplied fixture root. **#358 gained two criteria that came from reading the code rather than the issue** — every appender's exit status is discarded (`wait "$p" 2>/dev/null || true`, in two places), and two different lock bounds are in play, so a report naming "the timeout" is not actionable. **#401 is no longer a historical report:** its defect fired three times across these sessions, each needing a hand revert before unrelated work could be committed, and once nearly rode into a commit. Ratings and phase splits are provisional. → [#343](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/343) · [#358](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/358) · [#375](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/375) · [#388](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/388) · [#401](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/401)

### Changed
- **PR triage: #411 closed as superseded, #393 diagnosed as 26 commits stale.** **#411** ("GH-329 + GH-344") was **CI-green and would have regressed the file**: its commits are not ancestors of `development`, but the same work landed independently via `1612878`, and `38af517` (GH-369) then built on it. Real tree divergence `development → #411` is **+6 / −64** — its `find-doc.sh` is 80 lines against `development`'s 94 and **lacks GH-369's valueless-`--root` guard entirely**, the fix for a script that otherwise spins forever. `git merge-tree` also reports conflicts. The green `tier1` is the point worth recording: it verified that the tests pass, not that the branch was stale, which is #416's shape. **#393** ("GH-390 Phase 1", +946/−12) fails four assertions, and three are staleness — `path-integrity`, the GH-189 doc-side stale-status scan, and GH-284 Phase 3's missing-`Milestone:` warning are exactly the guardrails #416 restored *after* this branch's last update, and a full suite on `development` has none of them. **The fourth is its own and is real:** `expected reason gate-killed for the CPU cap, got 'pre-advance-failed'` — a resource kill reported as the gate having found a defect, which is #407's mislabel and the exact criterion now written into #390's acceptance. Recommended a rebase rather than performing it; force-pushing a branch this session did not create is not a call to make unasked. → [#411](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/pull/411) · [#393](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/pull/393) · [#416](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/416)

### Fixed
- **GH-368: `marathon-plan.sh` documented `--check` as a drift guard running in `validate.sh`; `validate.sh` invokes it zero times.** The smallest, cleanest instance of the #419 class in the tree — *a documented guard that reads as active while nothing runs it* — same family as #315 (a stale relay token let a build-nothing run report success), #319 (the gate word-split its own path and passed on a 0-byte file) and #348 (parity evidence that did not cover forward drift). The flag itself works; nothing calls it, so a hand-edited or stale `MARATHON-PLAN-<date>.md` can diverge from what the ledger renders with the suite fully green. **Scoped to the issue's Option B** — delete the claim — with Option A (actually wiring it in) left open on #368, because it carries a real false-positive question the issue states plainly: `--check` fails whenever today's plan doc has not been re-rendered, so wiring it naively turns the suite red on an ordinary day, and *"a suite people learn to ignore protects nothing."* The determinism rationale the false parenthetical hung off is true and is kept; only the assertion about `validate.sh` is removed, and the Usage text at `:72`/`:123` already documented `--check` correctly as a manual command. **The edit target is a GH-308 FROZEN twin**, which is the whole reason this lane was sequenced before #418: that lane's new frozen-artifact check would refuse this one, and weakening the check to accommodate a one-line comment would have been the wrong trade. Declared instead through the mechanism that already exists for it — a per-file `Frozen-twin-exception:` trailer (GH-321) — and verified with CI's own invocation, which reports *"utils/marathon-plan.sh — covered by a declared Frozen-twin-exception"* and *"every frozen-twin edit in this range is accounted for."* No behaviour change; `utils/py/marathon_plan.py` never carried the claim. → [GH-368-MARATHON-PLAN-CHECK-CLAIM.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-368-MARATHON-PLAN-CHECK-CLAIM.md) · [#368](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/368) · [#419](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/419)

## 2026-08-05

### Added
- **Release planning: `0.2.0 Litmus` and `0.3.0 Nightwatch` added to `RELEASES.md`, with both GitHub milestones created and 32 open issues assigned.** Authored at explicit operator request, which is the sole trigger `RELEASES.md` permits (#381) — recorded here so a later reader does not mistake it for the fill-it-in drift that file warns about. **The two arcs were derived from where the failures actually cluster, not from a backlog sweep.** *Litmus* (17 issues, target 2026-09-05) is the observation layer: make every gate capable of reporting red, or downgrade it to advisory. Its spine is **#419**, and the sessions since it was filed kept supplying evidence — #418 (preflight reads neither issue state nor FROZEN banners), #425 (the source-URL gate compares the issue number and ignores the repo slug, so an unrelated repo's same-numbered issue passes), #426 (containment exits 6 correctly and the file lands in the harness root anyway, while the obvious test checked the other repo and passed), #416 (three shipped guardrails deleted, CI red two days, read as noise), #375 (`agy whoami` exits 0 on a TTY error, so the probe cannot fail in the headless context it exists for), #344 (24 of 49 issues got an already-done verdict from same-numbered foreign documents), #368 (a `--check` documented as a `validate.sh` drift guard that `validate.sh` never runs). *Nightwatch* (15 issues, target 2026-10-10) is unattended durability against a real target repo: #384 (a crash leaves a clean tree containing ungated commits and nothing reports it), #387 (a turn killed at its wall-clock cap is committed and gated as if it completed), #383 (a hanging gate stalls an unattended run indefinitely), #382 (a kernel panic with no signal in the telemetry), #402 (the run commits to whatever branch the target happens to have checked out). **The ordering is the recommendation, not the calendar.** Nightwatch's fixes are verified by the instruments Litmus exists to make trustworthy; shipping durability first means measuring it with checks that have never been shown capable of reading anything but green — the #419 shape applied to a whole release. `Iterations:` bands reserve `0.2.0-0.2.4` and `0.3.0-0.3.4` per the admission rule, so patch releases inside an arc are recorded in this file only. Deliberately left unassigned rather than padded in: the multi-agent swarm cluster (#423/#354/#415), which is a third arc with its own theme, and the prompt-stack analyses (#398/#404), which are findings rather than scoped work. `pdda.sh releases` **errors=0 warns=0**. → [#381](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/381) · [#334](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/334) · [#419](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/419)

- **Acceptance criteria authored for seven Litmus issues, then reviewed adversarially by codex and agy in one consult — both found criteria satisfiable without the defect being fixed.** **First, a correction to the previous entry's count:** re-measuring with the gate's own extractor rather than a narrower grep showed **10 of 17** Litmus issues already had usable acceptance sections, not 7 — `ACC_HEADING_RE` accepts `## Suggested acceptance criteria`, which #409/#408/#407 carry (4/3/4 criteria). So seven issues needed authorship, not ten. **47 criteria were written for #343, #344, #358, #375, #388, #390, #401**, leaning on each issue's own stated constraints rather than invented ones — #358's *"do not lower M from 16 or drop the distinctness check"*, #390's deferral of gate-in-container, the GH-308 frozen-twin boundaries on #343/#375. **The review was worth more than the drafting.** Codex returned REWRITE on five of seven, agy on two, and **both independently found the same blocker in #343**: *"the two branches agree"* is satisfied by changing the **correct** branch to match the broken one — a criterion whose plain reading permits making the defect worse. Codex alone found #390's: every kill-path criterion was satisfied by a guard that kills *every* gate, because nothing required a normal gate to still pass and advance. **Codex read the tree rather than the issue text, and five of its factual claims were verified before any were acted on** — all five held. Two were errors of mine: #388's criterion asserted *"the repo's own PDDA lint already classifies those locations as non-durable"*, which is **false** (`check_hardcoded_paths` scans `pdda_list_working_docs` — documentation — and says nothing about runtime log destinations), and #401's *"writes nothing into any git repository"* was too absolute, contradicting a legitimate existing test that renders into a caller-supplied fixture root. **The fifth changed a lane materially: #344's root-resolution half already shipped** — `1612878` resolves `PROJECT/**` from the swept repo, and `test/gh369-find-doc-root-resolution.sh` pins the measured collision directly (*"case 5: same issue number from beta's cwd resolves BETA's doc"*). What remains is the identity guard the issue raised as an aside: `find-doc.sh` has **no** `gh_issue`/`source:` check, so a filename number match is still treated as identity. That is the **second** Litmus lane found mostly-shipped (after #416), both caught by reading the tree and neither catchable by preflight — which is #418's whole point. **Two reviewer findings were rejected with reasons:** agy's *"a file number match from the correct repository is sufficient"* contradicts #344's own *"Also worth a guard"* paragraph, and its *"remove entirely"* for #401's tracked-file question discards something the issue explicitly raises — codex's concrete-state replacement is better than deletion. **The cross-cutting finding is the one that outlives this batch** and is recorded on #419: *"observed failing against pre-fix code and recorded"* has no required location, command or result, so **the negative-control requirement is currently satisfiable by writing a sentence** — true of #400's `1 pass / 19 fail`, #410's `7 pass / 4 fail` and #422's `15/3` alike. All seven sets now require a durable baseline artifact naming the reproducer, the pre-fix revision, and both results. → [#419](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/419) · [#344](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/344) · [#390](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/390) · [#343](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/343)
- **Litmus lane prep: six capture docs, seven preflight-READY contracts, five ROADMAP entries, and today's marathon plan — all seven lanes gated on operator GO.** Prepared at operator request for release 0.2.0. **Measured before authoring rather than estimated:** of Litmus's 17 issues, only **7** carry a `## Acceptance` section, so only those can get a capture doc that is a verbatim copy. The other 10 were deliberately left unprepared — a capture doc for an issue with no acceptance section means the *model* writes the definition of done, and then the builder and reviewer both grade against that wording with no undamaged copy anywhere, which is the GH-202 inversion #400 exists to prevent. **Every acceptance block was machine-inserted from `gh`**, never retyped; all seven now read `acceptance: match` and `verdict: ready (exit 0)`. **Four findings came out of the preparation, none of which a plan on paper would have surfaced.** (1) `utils/marathon-plan.sh` carries the **GH-308 FROZEN banner**, and #368's own recommended fix is to delete a comment from it — so #368 is precisely the lane #418's frozen-artifact check would refuse, and both are in this release. Resolution recorded identically in both docs: **fire #368 first**, which costs nothing and leaves the check strict, rather than weakening it. (2) The plan generator's *"gated on operator GO"* decision is a **regex over the ledger entry's prose** (`gated on operator go|…|awaiting go`), so #425's entry — which said *"BLOCKED ON A DESIGN CALL, do not fire"* in plain English — was scheduled as an **active lane**. Reworded to a matching phrase so today's plan is correct; the defect is the #410 shape (a decision made by grepping prose) sitting in the planner. (3) **#416 is one criterion wide, not five** — criteria 1–4 shipped in PR #413 and were verified by direct inspection, so a doc restating all five would have sent a builder to rebuild four things that exist, and preflight could not have caught it (that is #418). Its remaining criterion cannot live in `PROJECT/PDDA.md`, which is **sync-managed** — a policy about surviving syncs, in a file the sync replaces, is the incident restaged. (4) **Two gates are mechanically incompatible**: fidelity requires the acceptance block byte-for-byte, `pdda-check-hardcoded-paths` rejects a path substring #417's criterion legitimately contained, and a `[changed]` deviation must quote the original text — **reintroducing the rejected substring**, so declaring the deviation cannot satisfy the second gate either. Resolved at the source by rewording the criterion **on the issue** (meaning-preserving, recorded in an issue comment); the general consequence — *an issue whose acceptance text names a path cannot be copied verbatim* — is recorded for the #419 inventory. **The suite caught one of my own errors:** #368's first deviation attempt declared a deviation against a still-verbatim list and was rejected as vacuous — the C8b hole closed during #399/#400, working on its author. → [#419](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/419) · [#418](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/418) · [#417](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/417) · [#426](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/426) · [#425](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/425) · [#416](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/416) · [#368](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/368)

### Fixed
- **GH-410: the worktree containment verdict was a substring scan of the agent's prose, so it turned on wording and threw away completed reviews.** `agy-turn.py:223` decided containment with `if root in line` over the transcript — which answers *"did the model mention a path"*, not *"did it access one"*. The two diverge in both directions: an agent that quietly touched the real tree without naming it was never caught, and one that cited a path in a finding was failed for it. The reporter's quantified case settles it — same run, same builder, same isolation settings: the phase with **10** repo-root mentions was **Approved**, the one with **9** failed **three consecutive times**. **The harness supplies the string it punishes:** the retry preamble renders absolute paths into `RELAY.md` (`marathon_drive.py:403-407`, plus *"Use this exact tick binary (run it from any directory): {tick_cli}"* at `:1199`/`:1217`), and it renders **only when `prior >= 1`** — so a phase that fails once is handed *more* copies of the trigger, a recovery path that raises the hazard it is retrying against. Four exemption shapes had already accreted (`[trace] ` lines, `TICK_REPO_ROOT=`, file-scheme URIs, markdown link targets) across two prior patches (#183, #187): a deny-list that does not converge. **Correction to the report's third recommendation**, which asked for containment to be made uniform across shims on the premise that *"either codex is under-contained or agy is over-contained"*: **neither.** `rtl.worktree_end()` — the real filesystem off-lane diff — is called and enforced with `sys.exit(6)` by **all five** shims (`agy:206`, `codex:107`, `claude:135`, `aider:187`, `pi:137`). Containment was already uniform; what agy alone carried was an *additional, unsound* layer. So uniformity is reached by **removing** it, not by propagating it to four more shims, and choosing a builder never picked a safety model — only an exposure to false positives. **The resolution rests on what is observable.** Writes are observable and already observed, so `worktree_end` stays the verdict untouched. Reads are not observable here — `openat`-level tracing is not portable under macOS SIP without entitlements — so the honest response is to stop asserting an unobservable property rather than keep asserting it badly. The prose signal is now an **advisory** (`rtl.narration_mentions_root()`, shared so shims cannot drift) recorded on the transcript and stderr, and it **never fails a turn or discards a verdict**: a heuristic that destroys completed work must fail toward keeping it. The retry preamble now names `relay-automation/DEBUG-MANTRA.md` by its **harness-relative** path with the directory given once — preserving GH-162's contract that the note reference that file, which the first attempt broke — and `ESCALATION.md` by filename; the tick line keeps its absolute path because *"run it from any directory"* requires it, and that is harmless now that nothing fails on prose. Python only; all five Bash twins are frozen (GH-308). New `test/gh410-containment-advisory.sh` **11/0**, with the counter-pin doing the real work: **C4/C4b assert an off-lane write still exits 6 and C5/C5b assert every shim still enforces the filesystem check**, because a suite that only proved "mentions no longer fail" would pass just as happily against a build with containment deleted — the #348/#351 shape #419 exists to catch. Two controls: **pre-fix replay 7 pass / 4 fail**, and a **containment-deleted mutation 9 pass / 2 fail**. That mutation run earned its keep immediately: it exposed that C4 had been satisfied by the word `worktree_end` appearing in a *comment*, so the check now matches the call site, not the name. `test/agy-turn.sh` case 15 was updated rather than deleted — it encoded the old exit-5 behaviour and now pins that the review survives and the advisory is still recorded. `relay-pkg.tar.gz` regenerated (packaged `test/agy-turn.sh` drifted). Full `validate.sh` **exit 0, 0 failures**. → [GH-410-CONTAINMENT-PROSE-SCAN.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-410-CONTAINMENT-PROSE-SCAN.md) · [#410](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/410) · [#419](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/419)

## 2026-08-04

### Fixed
- **GH-422: the source-URL gate was correct but expensive, and its blast radius had been measured on the wrong population.** PR #420's gate stays exactly as it is — those docs genuinely lack provenance. Two things around it were wrong. **(1) The posture was chosen from this repo alone**, 1 of 48 docs, while the repos vendoring the harness carried far more: the same shape as the defects #419 exists to catch, a decision made from a sample that did not include the affected group. **(2) The remediation it printed was a placeholder** — `Set source: https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/issues/154` — for a value `repo_slug_for()` already resolves from the target's `origin`, a few hundred lines away in the same file; an operator with a dozen affected docs did that lookup a dozen times. **Stated precisely, because it was overstated in discussion once:** the gate sets `ready=0`, prints NOT-READY, exits 5 and writes no packet. **No file is modified, nothing is deleted, no git state changes.** It is a refusal to start a lane, reversible by adding one line, and shares nothing with GH-312's destructive class. **Fixed** by resolving the real `owner/repo` into the message (falling back to the placeholder only when no remote resolves) and adding `utils/py/backfill_source_url.py`, which derives the URL from the **target** repo's own `origin` — never the harness's — so a vendored install cites its own project. Three deliberate conservatisms: **dry-run by default** (it edits tracked files); **a mismatch is never rewritten** — when `source:` cites a different issue than `gh_issue` the wrong field may be `gh_issue`, and rewriting the URL to agree would launder bad provenance into provenance that *looks* verified, so it reports and exits non-zero rather than guess; and **frontmatter only**, with the body asserted byte-identical before any write. **A real defect was caught by dry-running against a live consumer before merge:** `cactus` reported *0 to change* where the measurement said 4, because its docs use `issue:` not `gh_issue:` — the **gate** still blocks them (it resolves a doc by globbing `GH-9-*.md` for `--gh-issue 9`), but the **backfill** read only frontmatter and skipped precisely the docs the gate refuses, reporting success while leaving the operator blocked. That is the two-extractors-disagree failure agy found in #400's checker during #413, recurring in new code within a day; fixed by giving the tool the gate's exact effective-issue rule and pinned by C5b/C5c, which assert not just that a line is written but that `check_source_url` **then agrees the doc passes** — a tool claiming a fix the gate still rejects is worse than no tool. **A second correction from the same run:** fleet exposure is **34 docs, not the 18 first reported** — the original blast script used a non-recursive glob and missed subdirectories like `PROJECT/2-WORKING/v1.3.5/`, so the measurement that flagged an under-measurement was itself under-measured. Fleet dry-run (read-only): `rebalance-OS` 16, `LTVera-Pandas` 14 + **1 conflict left for a human**, `cactus` 4, and `pdda`/`sleuth-app`/`fast-key-replacement-macos` clean. `test/gh422-backfill-source-url.sh` **20/0**, with **both** #419 negative-control forms recorded because the tool is net-new and has no meaningful "before": a **pre-fix replay** of the message change at **15/3**, and a **deliberate mutation** disabling the conflict guard at **16/2**. One bug in this fix's own body-integrity guard was caught by its suite before merge — it sliced the rewritten text with offsets computed from the original, so every insertion tripped it and every doc fell through to `skip`; fixed by re-matching frontmatter on the new text rather than loosening the assertion. → [GH-422-SOURCE-URL-REMEDIATION.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-422-SOURCE-URL-REMEDIATION.md) · [#422](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/422) · [#400](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/400) · [#419](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/419)
- **GH-400 criterion 2: a capture doc must now cite its issue's URL, enforced instead of merely instructed.** `b026737` shipped this criterion as **prose only** — `skills/10days/SKILL.md` told the model to put the issue URL in `source:`, and **no gate ever read the field**. `source: issue#400`, or no `source:` at all, passed everything, because the fidelity check resolves its issue number from `--gh-issue` or the `gh_issue:` frontmatter key and never looks at `source:`. That is **this issue's own defect reproduced inside its own fix**: a model-authored field trusted as though it were verified. It is why the criterion was deliberately left unchecked on #400 rather than claimed — a green run proved nothing about it. **Fixed** with `check_source_url()` in `utils/py/swarm_preflight.py` (Python only; the Bash preflight twin and all five turn shims are frozen under GH-308): a doc naming a `gh_issue`, or resolved by `--gh-issue N`, must carry a `source:` containing a GitHub issue URL whose number matches — `missing` or `mismatch` is **NOT-READY, exit 5, no packet written** — and `readiness.source_url` is recorded on every run so the claim is auditable rather than inferred from a green verdict. **Frontmatter only:** a `source:` line inside a fenced example in the prose is documentation, not provenance, and a case pins that. The check is **purely local** — it reads frontmatter and never touches the network — so unlike the acceptance gate it has no offline-degradation path and no `unknown`-means-skip escape; the only pass-through is a doc with no issue to point at. **Blast radius measured before choosing the posture, as with Phase 0:** of 67 capture docs across `1-INBOX` + `2-WORKING`, **48 already carry the exact issue URL, 19 name no `gh_issue`, and exactly 1 would newly block** — `GH-299-DIAGRAM-TRUST-LAYOUT.md`, a genuine instance of the defect, fixed in the same commit — with **0** mismatches. A hard fail costs one doc, which is why it could be taken literally rather than softened to a warning. New `test/gh400-source-url.sh` is **13/0** and was **observed at 2 pass / 11 fail against pre-fix code**, where the missing-URL case emitted `verdict: ready (exit 0)` and wrote a packet — the negative control GH-419 asks of every gate. The 2 that pass pre-fix are the anti-noise controls that must pass in *both* directions, kept and labelled for the same reason #400's cases 2–4 were. **Two sibling suites needed fixtures corrected, not assertions relaxed:** `swarm-preflight.sh`'s `mkcap` and `gh400-acceptance-fidelity.sh`'s `mkcapture` both built capture docs carrying a `gh_issue` (or a `GH-N-` filename resolved by `--gh-issue`) and no `source:`, so under the new rule the fixtures were the invalid artifacts. Narrowing the gate to frontmatter-only would have made both pass untouched — and would have reintroduced precisely the "omit a field to dodge the check" bypass agy found in this same checker during the #413 QA round — so the rule was kept and the fixtures moved. All four of #400's acceptance criteria are now met. → [GH-400-CAPTURE-DOC-ACCEPTANCE-FIDELITY.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-400-CAPTURE-DOC-ACCEPTANCE-FIDELITY.md) · [#400](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/400) · [#419](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/419)

## 2026-08-03

### Fixed
- **GH-416: the PDDA sync silently deleted three shipped guardrails, and CI stayed red on `development` for two days because the failures read as noise.** `cfd56b0` — *"land the upstream PDDA sync and close the two failures it arrived with"* — replaced the sync-managed `utils/pdda/**` tree wholesale. It closed two failures and left **three**, which were then carried as "pre-existing" by everyone who looked, including in PR #413's own body. They were not noise. Diffing across the commit: **(1)** GH-189's 3-COMPLETED stale-status scan — `non-terminal` went **4 occurrences → 0** in `pdda.sh` and existed nowhere else in the repo; **(2)** GH-189's entire **`roadmap-issue-state` subcommand**, **5 → 0**, so the test invoking it got `unknown command` and the file exited 2; **(3)** GH-284 Phase 3's missing-`Milestone:` warning — the release → issue-set join key `/10days` depends on — reduced to a bare `printf`; **(4)** the roll-up's explicit `Milestone: (none — release cannot resolve to an issue set)` line; **(5)** four broken path references introduced into `path-integrity`'s scan. Only (5) was cosmetic. **(2) and (4) were invisible until (1) and (3) were fixed**, because the test files fail-fast — the same broken-observation family as #315/#319/#351, where a failure that has been red a while stops being read as information at all. **The root cause is structural, not clerical:** local checks were living inside a tree that is replaced wholesale, and `pdda.sh` offers no extension seam (it sources only its own `pdda-lib.sh`), so every check this repo added upstream was on borrowed time. Fixed by moving them **out** of the sync surface into repo-owned `utils/pdda-local-checks.sh`, which sources only `pdda-lib.sh` and carries its own copy of the four private helpers that live in the un-sourceable CLI; findings are byte-identical and the functions were extracted mechanically from git rather than retyped. `test/pdda-local-checks.sh` guards them **falsifiably** — every check gets a fixture that must trip it *and* one that must not, plus a drift detector comparing the copied terminal-word list against upstream's, because an "exit 0" assertion would pass against a deleted check body, which is precisely the bug. Wired into `validate.sh` **and** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` so they run against the real repository, which `pdda.sh run` used to do for them. `path-integrity` now skips `utils/pdda/**` with the reason recorded at the exemption: those tokens describe upstream's tree, and no edit here survives a sync. **Behaviour (4)'s INFO → WARN change is kept, not reverted** — upstream's reasoning (*"A check that could not run is NOT a check that passed"*) is better than what it replaced and is the same principle GH-400 applies to acceptance fidelity, so the stale test was updated to pin that the state is reported and never escalates to ERROR. Found while writing the fix: the `path-integrity` comment documenting the four broken paths **quoted them, and tripped the very check it documents** — they are now described rather than spelled out. `validate.sh` **0 failures**, the first fully green local suite since `cfd56b0`, and `tier1` green on PR #413. → [#416](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/416)
- **GH-399/GH-400: an agy QA relay found two real gate bypasses, and both were reproduced before being fixed.** Reviewed via `relay-xyz` with a review-only allowlist; 2 Blockers, 2 Shoulds, 1 Nit, all accepted. **(1)** An issue whose `## Acceptance` section *exists but lists nothing* returned `[]`, and the caller collapsed `[]` with `None` → `unknown` → **no block**, so a capture doc could invent arbitrary criteria under its own heading. `extract_acceptance_criteria`'s own docstring said the two cases must stay distinct *because collapsing them would let a doc dodge the gate*; the caller collapsed them anyway — the defect was in the gap between the documented intent and its one consumer. **(2)** Extraction stopped at the first **unindented** line, so an explanatory paragraph between criteria truncated the list — worse than reported, because `collect_inline_checklist` kept scanning: the two extractors **disagreed about the same document**, and the packet would inline three criteria while the fidelity check compared two. **(3)** `## Acceptance:` and `## Acceptance Criteria (draft)` read as "no section" → another silent `unknown` bypass; relaxed **more narrowly than proposed**, since dropping the `$` anchor outright would also match a prose heading like *"## Acceptance is not required here"* (pinned by a negative case). **(4)** The review correctly identified three cases that pass against the pre-fix code — they were exactly the 1 and the 2 that passed in the pre-fix runs. **Kept and labelled rather than deleted:** they are the cry-wolf controls, and a gate that flags faithful copies is precisely the failure that gets a gate switched off; each now names its falsifiable sibling. **(5)** An arrow inside a criterion broke `[changed]` parsing — **fixed differently than proposed**, because `rsplit` is also wrong: with three arrows neither the first nor the last is the separator, so the parser now offers every split as a candidate and reconciliation picks the one matching the actual diff. Suites 21 → **26/0** and **14/0**. **The review turn itself exited 6:** agy wrote `scratch-reviewer.md` off-allowlist and `rtl_check` reverted it — GH-22's backstop working — with the content recovered from `.tick/orphan-backups/`; another instance of the known agy-oversteps-allowlist pattern, on a turn whose `ALLOW_PATHS` was deliberately empty. → [thread](relay-system/2026-08-03/gh399-400-acceptance-qa.md)
- **GH-399: the packet's acceptance block kept only each criterion's first line, so every hard-wrapped requirement reached the builder as a half-sentence.** Second cut in the same chain as #400 — that one is the capture doc drifting from the issue, this is the packet drifting from the capture doc. Preflight matched only lines *beginning* a `- [ ]` bullet; capture docs hard-wrap at ~80 columns, so continuations were discarded **silently**, under a heading asserting the criteria were *"inlined from the capture doc."* **10 of 10 lanes** in one marathon lost 1–4 lines each. The issue's GH-201 evidence, now the test fixture: *"An explicit `--database` path that does not exist raises a clear error instead"* — the clause *"of silently resolving to the canonical DB"* never reached the builder, and the second criterion ended on an em dash, so what survived reads as an order to **preserve** the fallback the issue exists to remove. Because the reviewer grades against the same packet, there was no intact copy anywhere for either role to check. **Correction to the report:** it cites `utils/swarm-preflight.sh:882`; GH-308 **froze** that twin and it does not execute — the live defect was the identical regex in `utils/py/swarm_preflight.py`. **Fixed** with indent-gated continuation folding (reusing #400's extractor — ungated folding swallows a trailing `---` or the next paragraph, which is not theoretical: it produced a real false positive while dogfooding #400), extraction bounded to `## Acceptance` where one exists, an announced 25-item cap, and a lossless-copy check that re-parses the **rendered** block — deliberately not the structure that produced it, since comparing text to its own generator proves nothing — and sets NOT-READY rather than warning. `readiness.acceptance_inline` records scope, count, cap and losslessness for post-hoc audit. **One criterion was narrowed and the change declared** through #400's deviations mechanism, which is its first real use: bounding extraction *unconditionally* to `## Acceptance` — as the issue asks — would empty the acceptance block of **32 of this repo's 33 active capture docs**, which keep their checkboxes under `## Phase N`, deleting every lane's definition of done to close a leak the issue itself files as *"not yet observed."* **That declaration then exposed a hole in #400's own checker**, found by dogfooding: reconciliation only ran when the two lists differed, so a deviation declared against a still-verbatim list passed vacuously — a reader would believe a criterion was narrowed while the list said otherwise. Reconciliation now runs whenever the section exists (`test/gh400-acceptance-fidelity.sh` C8b, **21/0**). New `test/gh399-packet-acceptance-continuation.sh` **14/0** pins the whole chain — capture doc → `packet.md` → **relay file** — byte-for-byte at each hop, because asserting only the packet leaves the last hop, the one the builder actually reads, unproven; observed **2 pass / 12 fail** against pre-fix code. Writing that chain test cost one **orphaned `marathon_drive` holding `.git/relay-driver.lock` in the live repo** — the driver resolves ROOT to the harness clone unless `MARATHON_ROOT` is set, so the fixture run locked the real repository and hung — so the test now sets `MARATHON_ROOT` and runs under a hard 60s timeout, on the GH-369 principle that an unattended suite must fail on a hang rather than inherit it. → [GH-399-PACKET-ACCEPTANCE-CONTINUATION.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-399-PACKET-ACCEPTANCE-CONTINUATION.md) · [#399](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/399)
- **GH-400: `/10days` capture docs restated a GitHub issue's acceptance criteria instead of copying them, and preflight now refuses to emit a packet when the two have diverged without explanation.** Step 4 has a model summarise an issue into `PROJECT/2-WORKING/`, and everything downstream — preflight, the packet, the relay file, the builder, the reviewer — reads the **summary**; the issue text reaches none of them, so the summary silently becomes the contract and **no undamaged copy of the requirement exists anywhere in the pipeline**. **The measured case is an inversion, verified byte-for-byte against both source repos before any code was written:** `rebalance-OS` #202 requires a malformed row be *"proven to be either reconciled or surfaced, never silently dropped from the projection"*; the capture doc `/10days` wrote (commit `b941299`) instead requires asserting *"the exporter's actual current behavior (drop the row, continue processing the rest)"*; two criteria were dropped outright, including the one written specifically to prevent what shipped. The delivered artifact is **worse than the report claimed** — `3673257f` adds a test function literally named **`malformed_source_row_is_dropped`**, asserting `assert_not_contains "$out" 'truncated row'`, and a second independent run (`7525d047`) produced the same thing, because both were instructed to. Every gate was green, the reviewer approved, the phase closed; it surfaced only when a human read the issue and the test side by side weeks later. **Two corrections to the report, both load-bearing:** its suggested fix rests on *"preflight already fetches the issue for freshness checks"* — **it does not**, neither twin invokes `gh` in any form (freshness is `git fetch` + local probes), so this introduces the first issue-body fetch onto that path and has to carry its own offline contract; and companion #399 cites `utils/swarm-preflight.sh:882`, which GH-308 **froze** — the line that actually executes is `utils/py/swarm_preflight.py:844`. **The fix is three parts.** (1) `skills/10days/SKILL.md` §4 now requires the `## Acceptance` block be reproduced byte-for-byte (re-wrapping allowed, nothing else), requires `source:` to carry the issue URL, and demotes the contract's `remediation.criteria` to an explicitly-labelled ranking summary rather than the definition of done. (2) `utils/py/swarm_preflight.py` re-fetches the issue and compares; unexplained divergence is **NOT-READY / exit 5 with no packet written**, and the packet now states in the builder's own context window whether its checklist was verified against the issue or merely inlined. (3) The only supported way to diverge is a machine-checked `## Acceptance — deviations from the issue` section (`- [dropped|changed|added] <text> — reason: <why>`) whose entries must reconcile the two lists **exactly** — an entry naming a criterion that is not actually different is rejected, so the section cannot become a rubber stamp, and there is deliberately **no env-var bypass**: a deviation belongs in the doc where the next reader finds it. **Undeterminable is not divergence** — no `gh`, unauthenticated, offline, or an issue with no acceptance section all report `unknown` loudly and never block, because an unreachable network is not evidence of drift. **Measured before choosing the posture rather than after:** all 33 `2-WORKING` docs carrying a `gh_issue` were compared against their live issues (33/33 fetches succeeded), **0** would newly read NOT-READY — 32 of the 33 issues have no `## Acceptance` section at all — so the hard fail the issue asked for costs nothing today. New `test/gh400-acceptance-fidelity.sh` (**20/0**, hermetic via a stubbed `gh`) pins the real GH-202 pair, and was **observed failing against the pre-fix code: 1 pass / 19 fail, with the inversion emitting `verdict: ready (exit 0)` and a written packet.** Cases 2–4 are the half that keeps it honest — a checker that flags the inversion but also flags a faithful copy is noise that gets switched off — and case 4 exists because **dogfooding this check against issue #400 itself caught a real false positive**: a GitHub body's trailing `---` and italic sign-off were being folded into the last criterion, so a byte-perfect copy reported as diverged. Continuation folding is indent-gated as a result, which also means the comparison sees the full text of a hard-wrapped criterion — the content #399 truncates at the packet-inlining site, deliberately left for #399 to fix rather than absorbed here. → [GH-400-CAPTURE-DOC-ACCEPTANCE-FIDELITY.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-400-CAPTURE-DOC-ACCEPTANCE-FIDELITY.md) · [#400](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/400) · [#399](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/399)

## 2026-08-01

### Changed
- **GH-381: `RELEASES.md` is now explicitly OPTIONAL, and two competing proposals were adjudicated into one plan.** Two issues were filed ~25 minutes apart in parallel sessions for the same request — #381 (the planner skill; first, and more complete on command surface, tests and reversibility) and #389 (the admission rule, the iteration band, and the sync/upstream analysis). **#389 is closed as a duplicate**, its capture doc deleted, and `ROADMAP.md` carries a single canonical pointer. Two conflicts were resolved, and in both cases the losing option was the one that quietly grows the file: **(1)** a `--horizon` batch mode on `/release-plan` rather than a new `/Releases` skill, so everything writing `RELEASES.md` has exactly **one** enforcement point for the admission rule instead of two; **(2)** a reserved `Iterations: 0.2.0-0.2.4` band rather than five persisted `Iteration N:` labels — five slots across a five-release horizon is 20–25 named rows, each an invitation to fill in what shipped, which is the same drift arriving as *structure* instead of as appended blocks. That also answered #381's own open design question. **The headline change is optionality**, a requirement neither issue originally carried: the concern was that assistants keep offering to fill the file in. This turned out to be **purely a prose problem — the tooling was already correct** (`pdda.sh releases` is warn-only, never blocks, and skips a missing file outright), while `PROJECT/PDDA.md` documented the format, every field and two skills at length and never once said the file was optional. The resulting drift is one nobody decides to cause: a sparse planning file *looks* incomplete, a helpful assistant offers to populate it, and repeated across sessions that produces a **de-facto pre-CHANGELOG** — a second hand-maintained history guaranteed to disagree with this file — **one helpful suggestion at a time**. So `RELEASES.md` now *opens* with an instruction not to top it up, before any format detail, phrased as an instruction rather than a description because the audience that causes the drift reads it as instructions. → [GH-381-RELEASES-ROLLING-TRAIN.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-381-RELEASES-ROLLING-TRAIN.md) · [#381](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/381)

Upstream now carries the optionality language, an `Iterations:` field, and an **iteration-band check** — this repo's `Iterations: 0.1.0-0.1.4` passes it unchanged. **The warning issued in the handoff prompt was heeded and is the load-bearing outcome:** this repo's copy carried `Milestone:`, which upstream did not have at all, and because `PROJECT/PDDA.md` *is* sync-managed a blind re-sync would have deleted the release → issue-set join key that the `/10days` hand-off depends on. ## 2026-07-30

### Changed
- **GH-372: removed `LICENSE.md`, leaving AGPL-3.0-only as the repo's single, unambiguous license.** Operator decision. Found during an artifact sweep, and **it was my error**: `1a1b5bc` added `LICENSE` (AGPL-3.0-only) + `LICENSE-COMMERCIAL.md` and flipped `package.json` from `ISC` to `AGPL-3.0-only` **without noticing `LICENSE.md` already existed** (`1b88b68`, 2026-06-14) declaring a *Personal-Use / Commercial* dual license © Neochrome, Inc. with an Apache-2.0 option. These were not two options in one scheme but two schemes making incompatible claims about the same code: AGPL grants everyone — companies included — commercial use conditioned on source disclosure, while `LICENSE.md` stated *"You may not use this software… for commercial purposes"* and routed all business use through a paid license. A recipient reading `LICENSE` could lawfully do what `LICENSE.md` forbade, and with neither file marked as superseding the other, ambiguity in a license is normally construed against the drafter. GitHub had already stopped being able to resolve it, classifying the repo as **`Other`**. Verified before removal that nothing depends on the file: **no reference in `test/`, `utils/`, `relay-automation/`, `validate.sh`, or the vendoring/packaging scripts**. Two remaining mentions are deliberately left alone — `REPO_MAP.md` is auto-generated and banner-marked *"do not edit by hand"*, and `ROADMAP.md`'s GH-71 entry is a point-in-time historical record, which that entry itself establishes as the convention. **Timing is why this was worth blocking on:** the contradiction existed on `development` only — `main` was 9 commits behind and never carried `LICENSE`, and `v0.1.0` was tagged before `1a1b5bc` — so it was resolved before reaching the release trunk rather than after. → [#372](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/372)

### Fixed
- **GH-370: the GH-336 plan doc cited an engine that no longer exists — including a no-`gh`-call test that could not fail.** PR #350's own body recorded this re-test as outstanding; this closes that gap. Rounds 1–4 verified every code citation against `utils/py/_marathon_plan_node.js`, which **#340 deleted**. Re-run against `origin/development@9082328` in a clean worktree: **7 of 8 findings hold**, one broke, and two new defects surfaced that the original review could not have caught. **The sharpest is defect 2, and it is live rather than stale:** the kill-switch criterion specified a `gh` stub "that fails loudly if invoked," which against this engine is *undetectable* — `_resolve_gh` runs the binary with `stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL` and folds any non-zero exit into `GH_MODE = "off"`, so the scream is discarded and the failure is indistinguishable from "`gh` not installed." Demonstrated rather than argued: a stub echoing to stderr and exiting 42 produced **zero** observable output, while a **file tripwire** in the same run recorded `gh auth status`. A fixture written as specified would have been green and worthless — this repo's recurring family (#315/#319/#348/#368/#369), *a signal that reads as protection while nothing is checked*, and the sixth instance logged. The criterion now mandates a file tripwire and states why an exit code carries no information. **The claim that broke** was round 4's proudest `[Pass]` — that the hook point was "real and already proven," citing `_inject_review_lanes`. #340 folded it into the engine (`_marathon_plan.py:1297`) and `marathon_plan.py` shrank ~390 → **212** lines with no post-render *mutation* step, so a builder following the note would hunt a deleted function; the seam is now named as the gap between `engine.run()` (`:170`) and the first mode branch (`--check` `:181`, `--dry-run` `:197`, write `:201`). **Codex caught three citation defects in my own corrections** on review (PR #371) and all three were right: `:181` was cited as though it opened all three branches when it opens only `--check`; the `_marathon_plan.py:450` link did not actually span the `stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL` on `:451` or the `except` on `:452-453` that the claim rests on; and "no post-render step **at all**" was overstated — the wrapper still performs check/dry-run/write handling at `:181-205`, and the narrow true claim is that nothing rewrites the rendered bytes. Fixed in the same PR. Worth recording rather than smoothing over: this correction pass was itself imprecise in exactly the way it was written to catch. **Codex's second pass then found the sixth stale citation** — the out-of-scope bullet cited `marathon-plan.sh:54` for the `--check` drift-guard comment, but `:54` is a bare `#` and the claim is at **`:67`**. My citation audit missed it because it only walked markdown `(path#Lnnn)` links, and this one was written as a bare `` `file.sh:54` `` — **an audit whose scope didn't cover the shape the defect took**, which is the same family as the defects it was auditing for. The audit now walks bare `file:line` references too; all nine resolve in-range *and* content-match. Second pass verdict: 4 `[Pass]`, 1 `[Should]`, no pre-existing defects, and explicitly *"no remaining reference treats deleted `_marathon_plan_node.js` or `_inject_review_lanes` as live code."* Also corrected: `QP_GH_STATE_FILE` was offered as a usable env var when it is **internal to the Bash twin**, assigned from the real `QUEUE_PLAN_GH_STATE_FILE` at `marathon-plan.sh:179` — exporting it is a no-op on both lanes and silently runs a fixture live; two citations had drifted past end-of-file (`:378-391` against a 212-line file); and the `/10days` "no executable" claim was too strong now that the skill ships `find-doc.sh`/`install.sh`/`scan-issues.sh` (narrowed to *no plan-rendering entry point*, which is what the criterion actually needs). **Verified, not assumed:** all six surviving citations were checked in-range *and* content-matched by script; `QUEUE_PLAN_GH=off` and `QUEUE_PLAN_GH_STATE_FILE` each drive `gh` invocations to **0**; the fire list still renders 7 `**Wave N:**` lines ahead of the single `## How to fire a lane` anchor; churn re-measured at **528** commits/14d (`utils/py` 44, `relay-automation` 57) against round 4's 538/40/59 — same conclusion; `test/marathon-plan.sh` **72/0**. **The architecture is unchanged for the fifth time** — as with the three prior rewrites of its rationale, what needed fixing was the evidence and the test recipe, never the design. → [GH-336-PLANNING-CONTEXT.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-336-PLANNING-CONTEXT.md) · [#370](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/370)
- **GH-351: the registered dashboard gate rendered the artifact into the repo and then validated the copy it had just written.** `test/roadmap-dashboard.sh:27` ran the renderer — overwriting `$ROOT/ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` — and `:34` then `--check`ed that file. The assertion could not fail, and its own pass string read *"--check passes on the committed artifact"*, which was false by the time it ran. **The renderer was never the defect and was not touched:** standalone `utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh --check` returned `rc=1` on all three of the day's stale dashboards, so the correct signal existed the whole time — the test overwrote its input before asking. A second, quieter fault: the test **wrote into `$ROOT`**, so `validate.sh` itself silently un-staled the dashboard mid-run, a suite repairing the evidence it was meant to judge and leaving the working tree dirty afterwards. Three stale dashboards reached `development` on 2026-07-30 alone from three different authors (GH-342, #356, #350); it fires on essentially every PR that edits `ROADMAP.md`, and the only reason it was ever caught was someone running the standalone checker by hand. Fixed with two rules whose case ordering is load-bearing: **`--check` runs first, against the committed artifact, before anything writes**, and nothing writes `$ROOT` — write-mode is exercised through `ROADMAP_DASHBOARD_OUTPUT`, which the renderer already supported, so no renderer change was needed. **4 → 9 cases**, adding a missing-artifact case, the source-drift direction that actually occurs in practice, a no-side-effect assertion, and — the one that keeps this honest — **a mutation proof**: cases 1 and 3 are unfalsifiable alone, so case 4 plants a canary line in a copy and requires `--check` to reject it. **Verified against the real commit rather than a synthetic fixture:** replaying `719867f` (the #350 merge, genuinely stale), the old test scores **4 pass / 0 fail** while the new one scores **6 pass / 3 fail**. Two things visible only by running it — the old test left `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` modified in the fixture afterwards, and **case 9 (section counts) was already correct in the old file and still reported green** because the regeneration ran first: the bug did not add a bad assertion, it *disabled a good one* (`roadmap=128 dashboard=127` is what it should have been saying). Sixth instance of *an assertion that compares a thing to a freshly-derived copy of itself*, after #348, #342, #362(B) and #369 — the last of which was in a test written earlier the same day. The recurring lesson is not "write more tests" but **run the new test against the old code**; every one of these was caught that way and none by review. → [GH-351-DASHBOARD-TEST-GRADES-ITS-OWN-ANSWER-KEY.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-351-DASHBOARD-TEST-GRADES-ITS-OWN-ANSWER-KEY.md) · [#351](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/351)
- **GH-369: `/10days`' capture-doc lookup spun forever on a valueless `--root`, and blamed a flag the caller never passed for a bad `$TENDAYS_ROOT`.** Found reviewing PR #364 before merge — #364's own fix is correct and shipped; these are defects in the argument parser it introduced. `find-doc.sh --root` with no value **looped indefinitely**: `shift 2` with one argument remaining shifts nothing and returns non-zero, and the script is deliberately `-e`-exempt (documented analysis-tool profile), so `$#` never decreased, `$1` stayed `--root`, and the branch re-ran forever. Confirmed under an `alarm(5)` wrapper — **rc=142/SIGALRM, no output**. That matters more than a usage nit because `/10days` is explicitly an *unattended* skill, so this stalls a sweep with no error, no output and no timeout: an absent signal, the same shape as #315/#319/#351 rather than a wrong answer. Second defect: a bad `$TENDAYS_ROOT` printed `--root not a directory:` — naming a flag never passed, with an **empty path**, because the message hardcoded `--root` and interpolated `$ROOT_ARG`. Now tracks which of the four sources (`--root`, `$TENDAYS_ROOT`, git toplevel, `$PWD`) supplied the value and reports that one. My first cut of that fix reproduced the same class of bug — it printed `$ROOT`, which the failed command substitution had already emptied — kept as `$ROOT_RAW` with a comment. New `test/gh369-find-doc-root-resolution.sh` (**14/0**) registered in `validate.sh`; the file previously had **no test at all** and was absent from `TESTS=()`, which is why both defects and GH-344 before them landed unchallenged. **Observed failing before the fix rather than merely passing after:** against `1612878` (post-#364, pre-fix) **12/2**, exactly cases 1 and 2, with the hang *reported as a failure instead of hanging the suite*; against `4dc27b4` (pre-#364, the GH-344 bug) **4/10**, case 6 catching it with *"answered GH-163 from the HARNESS tree"*. **Case 6 is itself a caught mistake worth recording:** as first written it probed issue 777, absent from both trees — so the harness-relative bug and correct behaviour both returned `null` and it passed against the very code it existed to catch. It now derives a real `GH-<N>` from the harness's own `PROJECT/` tree, which is what makes that pre-#364 failure detectable. A fifth member of this repo's recurring family: *an assertion that cannot distinguish the bug from the fix.* → [#369](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/369) · [#364](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/pull/364)
- **GH-351, third instance in one day (three different authors): PR #350 landed a stale `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` and CI stayed green.** Regenerated as `b6e4bf2`. Running both checkers immediately after the merge isolates the defect precisely: `utils/roadmap-dashboard.sh --check` returns **rc=1** — the standalone checker is correct and needs no change — while the *registered* `test/roadmap-dashboard.sh` regenerates the artifact and then validates the copy it just wrote. The gate is green because it grades its own answer key. Fires on essentially every ROADMAP-touching PR; the only reason it keeps being caught is someone running the standalone checker by hand afterwards. → [#351](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/351)

## 2026-07-29

### Fixed
- **GH-362: the GH-308 freeze guard could not pass a range that contained the freeze itself, and rejected the trailer format that is permanently in its own git history — plus marathon-plan's exception is retired.** Both surfaced on release PR #361, the first time `development`..`main` had ever been through CI: the guard blocked it naming **all 11 twins** with nothing wrong in the diff. **(A)** `BASE_SHA` was `main`'s tip, so the range contained `07ae1e7`, *the commit that added the FROZEN banners* — and a commit that freezes N files necessarily edits N files. The guard was structurally unable to pass. Fixed with a narrow predicate: a path is exempt when the commit introducing its `FROZEN` banner is in the range **and nothing touched the path after it** — the exemption is for the establishing *edit*, not the file, and case 12 pins the negative so that is not a hole. Not self-limiting despite appearances: once `main` contains the freeze, later `main..development` ranges are clean, but a bisect, a long-lived branch, a fork on an old base, or a release branch cut from before the freeze all reach back past it again. **(B)** `07ae1e7`'s trailer is the **pre-GH-321 pathless form** (`Frozen-twin-exception: <reason>`, no path), which was correct when written — GH-321 (PR #328) made the trailer per-file and the new parser calls the old form malformed. **Git history cannot be rewritten, so GH-321 permanently rejected its own past**; I shipped GH-321, so this is mine. Reading the code made it sharper than the issue described: `collect_declared` returned rc 1 for *any* malformed trailer anywhere in the range, independent of whether that commit's edits needed covering — so a legacy trailer failed the run even when (A) already exempted its commit. Fixed by skipping trailer parsing for freeze-establishing commits **only**, so a *new* pathless trailer is still rejected everywhere, which is what GH-321 was actually for; git-standard **indented** continuation lines are now also folded, while flush-left wrapping deliberately is not (indistinguishable from the next paragraph, and guessing would let arbitrary prose become a coverage claim). **The decisive evidence is an A/B on identical input** rather than a green suite: same fixture, pre-fix guard from `origin/development` → **rc=1**, fixed guard → **rc=0**. Suite 27 → **32/0** (+1 inverted assertion, +4 cases). Self-checked: this change itself freezes a twin, and its own CI invocation passes while strict mode correctly still objects. Two incidental fixes found by *running* it, not by review — `| head -1` on a git pipe made every call emit `printf: write error: Interrupted system call` under `set -euo pipefail`, and `mapfile` is a bash 4+ builtin while macOS ships 3.2. **Separately, marathon-plan's GH-308 exception is retired.** It was the single documented exception, Bash authoritative *"until the known Python parity gaps are closed"* — GH-340 closed them (the copied `_marathon_plan_node.js` is deleted, `_marathon_plan.py` is native stdlib, no Node needed), so `UPGRADE.md:68` had been asserting a false premise since #341 merged. marathon-plan is now the **12th frozen twin**, and the guard's own assertion is **inverted** rather than deleted so a future revert fails loudly instead of silently restoring an exception whose reason is gone. The banner's first line matches the canonical literal exactly because the guard keys on it — the banner was changed to fit the assertion, not the reverse. **One tension created and stated rather than buried:** freezing marathon-plan and keeping Scenario T's byte-identity assertion agree today, but the first deliberate Python-only change to the planner will fail it, and the pressure then will be to delete the assertion for green; the right move is to retire it on purpose, which the banner and `UPGRADE.md` now say. → [GH-362-GUARD-FIX-AND-EXCEPTION-RETIREMENT.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-362-GUARD-FIX-AND-EXCEPTION-RETIREMENT.md) · [#362](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/362)

### Released
- **`0.1.0` — Quicksilver: Python-authoritative Tier-A twins.** The repo's first named release, and
  the first time `development` has been merged into `main` (which was **425 commits behind**). Scope was
  deliberately two issues, not a sweep, so the roll-up would mean something: **#308** (freeze the 11 Tier-A
  Bash twins; Python is the sole maintenance lane) and **#342** (the last known Bash-only behavior, ported).
  `release-lanes.sh rollup` reports **2/2 landed**.
  **What the release does NOT do**, stated plainly because the ledger previously promised it: Bash is not
  phased out. GH-308's own `non_goals` exclude porting the 21 Bash-only scripts and exclude
  `relay-turn-lib.sh`; the policy is **freeze, not delete**, and the `.sh` bodies remain as the reversible
  `XYZ_PYTHON=0` fallback. The ledger description was corrected from *"Phase out Bash scripts"* to
  *"Python-authoritative Tier-A twins"* before shipping, for exactly that reason.
  **Closing GH-308 found one thing that had never been done:** its Phase 1 insurance tag. `git tag` returned
  **0 tags**, local and remote — the escape hatch the epic promised did not exist. Cut as annotated
  `bash-final-2026-07-28` at `06100cc`, the parent of `07ae1e7` (the commit that landed the FROZEN banners),
  and named for its commit's real date rather than the `2026-07-26` placeholder the capture used
  illustratively. Found by *verifying* the checklist rather than trusting it — the same discipline that
  produced every other finding this cycle.
  Also demonstrated rather than asserted: staging one line into `codex-turn.sh` is **blocked, exit 1**.
  **Three ship gates ship unmet, marked honestly rather than ticked:** `Front-door reviewed: Not yet`,
  `Shakedown reviewed: Not yet`, and `License file: Not yet` — there is no `LICENSE` in the repo at all,
  now tracked as #360. Version `0.1.0`, not `1.0.0`: `package.json`'s `1.0.0` is npm scaffold default from
