  → `RELEASES.md` · [#308](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/308)

### Added
- **GH-284 Phase 4: `utils/release-lanes.sh` closes the loop — a release becomes marathon input, and what landed is computed from git ancestry.** Two verbs on one script, both keyed on Phase 3's `Milestone:` join key. **`seed`** resolves a milestone (`--milestone`, else `--release NAME` matched against `Release:`/`Codename:`, else the single *in-progress* block carrying one — `Shipped` history excluded) and emits its open issues as JSON-lines with the same six keys and sort as `skills/10days/scan-issues.sh`, so release-driven selection is that pipeline with a different seed set rather than a new pipeline. A release with a blank `Milestone:` **exits 3**, never an empty list: an empty list is indistinguishable from a milestone with no open issues, which is the exact ambiguity the join key exists to remove. **`rollup`** classifies each issue against the derived trunk and prints `N/M landed`. **The obvious data source did not exist:** GitHub's `closedByPullRequestsReferences` is **empty on every issue probed in this repo** (#308, #319, #292, #274, #51, #261 — open and closed alike), because issues here are closed by hand; building on it would have reported `0/N landed` forever and looked like a fact. So evidence comes from the commit log, and three matchers were tested against issues of known state before one was chosen: *any `GH-N`/`#N` anywhere* is too loose (`#308` matched a commit that merely mentions it); *`GH-N` anywhere in the subject* is still too loose (`#308` matched `fix(RELEASES): correct the GH-308 release codename spelling`, `#284` matched `fix(GH-322): … port GH-284 Phase 2 …`); **`GH-N` as the commit's conventional scope** (`type(GH-N…):` or leading `GH-N`) rejects both and resolves every real implementing commit. A bare `#N` is deliberately **not** evidence — in a subject it is the squash-merge PR number, a different namespace, so it would land issue 326 off PR #326. **Three states, not two:** the matcher under-reports (#319/#320 were really fixed, inside a marathon commit whose subject claims no issue), so the report distinguishes `landed` / `mentioned` / `absent`, where `mentioned` means *"trunk activity exists but nothing claims to implement it"* — a flat binary would report those as a clean "not done" and be believed. **Trunk is derived and it matters:** `origin/HEAD` resolves to `origin/main` here, so Quicksilver is honestly **0/1 landed** on the release trunk and **1/1** on `origin/development` where work lands first; `--trunk REF` selects, and the report always names what it measured. Exit 4 for an empty milestone, because `gh` returns an empty list for an unknown milestone exactly as for an empty one and "nothing found" must not read as a successful selection. `RELEASES.md` is parsed directly rather than through `pdda.sh releases-current` — that is a human-facing report whose wording is free to change, and parsing a report to drive automation is how you get a silent break. Bash only, no Python twin: a new non-Tier-A script does not need a second copy to drift (#320/#322). New `test/gh284-p4-release-lanes.sh` **33/0**, registered in `validate.sh`, hermetic, using this repo's real false-positive subjects verbatim — and **mutation-tested**: loosening the matcher back to a subject-mention fails exactly the discriminating assertions (29/4), so the green run is not green by construction. Three defects were caught in this implementation by inspection rather than by any exit code: the rollup fed its issue JSON to a `python3` heredoc that already owned stdin (the same SC2259 collision that broke the P2 run log for a release), `fail()` used `"$*"` and printed its own exit code into the operator's message, and the empty-milestone path rendered `0/0 landed` *before* exiting 4. → [GH-284-MARATHON-CLOSEOUT-PR.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-284-MARATHON-CLOSEOUT-PR.md) · [#284](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/284)
- **GH-284 Phase 3: `RELEASES.md` gains `Milestone:`, the release → issue-set join key.** A release could not drive marathon selection because it had no way to resolve to a *set* of issues — `GH_URL:` holds exactly one link (Quicksilver → GH 308), which cannot express scope. The linkage now lives in **GitHub**, where it maintains itself as issues open and close, and the ledger carries only a milestone **title**. Deliberately *not* a hand-maintained issue list: `PROJECT/PDDA.md` records that this light ledger already replaced a heavier design with "linked marathons/issues, a GitHub release-tag cache" that was "too much data to keep current," and re-adding one would walk straight back into that. The zero-field alternative — repointing `GH_URL:` at the milestone — was rejected because it silently changes an existing field's meaning. `pdda_releases_list` parses `Milestone:` as a 7th `\037`-delimited field; `pdda.sh releases` warns when a block has a `Target Date`, isn't `Shipped`, and has no milestone (scoped to *dated* blocks so the example entry stays quiet; `Shipped` releases exempt, since backfilling history buys nothing; still never gates the exit code); `pdda.sh releases-current` prints the milestone and prints `(none — release cannot resolve to an issue set)` when absent, because a roll-up that silently omits the join key is the same quiet gap this program keeps finding. GitHub milestone **Quicksilver** created (#1, due 2026-08-01 matching the ledger) with #308 assigned; broader membership is a scoping call left to the operator. New `test/gh284-p3-release-milestone.sh` is the **first test the `releases` check has ever had**, so it pins the pre-existing behaviors (empty version → error, bad date → warn, overdue → warn, never gates) alongside the new field, plus a field-ordering case — appending to a `\037` record could silently hand `line_no` to `milestone` and every other assertion would still pass by accident. Observed **10 pass / 5 fail** against pre-Phase-3 code, **15 / 0** after. Phase 4 can now ask `gh issue list --milestone "Quicksilver" --state open` for a release's scope with no new cache and no new file format. → [GH-284-MARATHON-CLOSEOUT-PR.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-284-MARATHON-CLOSEOUT-PR.md) · [#284](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/284)

### Added
- **GH-347: `pi` was installed inside another application's private Node runtime — working, and invisible to every shell. The convention that would have prevented it did not exist, so it does now.** Surfaced while driving GH-336's round-4 review: `find-harness.sh --check` reported `pi` absent, yet `@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent@0.81.1` was installed and functional at `~/.hermes/node/lib/node_modules`. **The cause is ambient rather than a mistake anyone made twice.** `~/.hermes` is a separate agent application (own `SOUL.md`, `sessions/`, `memories/`, `state.db`, `.env`, `skills/`, `cron/`, `hooks/`) shipping a bundled Node runtime, and on 2026-06-03 it symlinked that runtime onto PATH (`~/.local/bin/npm -> ~/.hermes/node/bin/npm`). npm derives its global prefix from its own node install, so `npm config get prefix` returns `~/.hermes/node` — **with no `~/.npmrc` and no explicit prefix setting anywhere** — and a plain `npm install -g` deposits our tools into that app's folder silently, exit 0. The break completes because only `node`/`npm` were symlinked back out; nothing symlinks `pi`, and `~/.hermes/node/bin` is not itself on PATH. The two lanes that never had this problem show the correct shape: `~/.local/bin/agy` (real binary) and `~/.local/bin/codex -> ~/.codex/packages/standalone/current/bin/codex` (own app dir, own symlink). **Why it matters more than a papercut:** `~/.hermes/.install_method` reads `git` and the folder carries a 10.8 MB `hermes-setup` plus an `.update_check`, so a Hermes update that rebuilds `node/` deletes `pi` on that app's schedule — and the only signal would be `find-harness.sh --check` reporting `pi` not on PATH, which is *precisely what it reports today*. Wiped and never-installed are byte-identical to the observation layer: the same disease as GH-315 and GH-319, where the guard and the ground truth agree on the wrong answer. **A guidance audit found nothing wrong — it found nothing at all.** `relay-automation/README.md:29` names the package but not its location; `GH-295-PI-BUILDER-INTEGRATION.md` covers the config surface and containment posture but never where the binary belongs; `skills/relay-xyz/SKILL.md` requires workers *on PATH* and ships `--check` for exactly that, which is the right check for a different question; `GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md` had no tool-install-path convention whatsoever. So the fix is a new convention, not a correction: **"Tool install paths — never inside another app's folder (GH-347)"** now sits under Conventions, carrying the rule, the invisible-failure mode, the `npm install -g` trap with an explicit instruction to run `npm config get prefix` **before** any global install, the positive pattern, and a deliberate scope note that this is a convention and a warning — **never a gate**, since where a working binary lives is the operator's call and a false positive that blocks a relay is worse than the papercut it prevents. **Blast radius is small, and that is load-bearing for the scoping:** `~/.hermes/node/lib/node_modules` holds only `@earendil-works/`, `corepack/`, and `npm/`, so `pi` is the *sole* misplaced third-party package — one isolated instance, not a systemic cleanup. Phase 2 (the convention) is landed here; **Phase 1 — actually relocating `pi` — remains open**, as does an optional warn-only detection phase in `find-harness.sh --check` that is explicitly droppable if it cannot be done cleanly. Deliberately out of scope: repointing Hermes's own `node`/`npm` symlinks or the machine's global npm prefix (that app's business, and riskier than the problem). → [GH-347-TOOL-INSTALL-PATHS.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-347-TOOL-INSTALL-PATHS.md) · [#347](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/347)

### Changed
- **GH-340: marathon-plan's Python path is now a native Python engine — the copied Node renderer is deleted and Node is no longer required to run it.** `utils/py/marathon_plan.py` used to `exec node utils/py/_marathon_plan_node.js` — a copy of the planner embedded in `utils/marathon-plan.sh` that GH-112 duplicated to preserve behavior quickly. That copy drifted (GH-154 found it missing the GH-48 zone model) and GH-255 papered over two more gaps by pre/post-processing *around* the copied program in `marathon_plan.py`: `_normalize_roadmap` mutated the ROADMAP input so the copy's older `docOf` would resolve the bash-correct doc (shim `S`), and `_inject_review_lanes` appended the GH-86 "Review lanes" overlay section after the copy rendered (shim `N`). Both were symptoms of the copy being a second, worse source of truth — the reason GH-308 carved marathon-plan out as the exception to the Python-authoritative policy. This ports the **canonical** engine (the one embedded in `utils/marathon-plan.sh`, which already has native `docOf(item, gh)` own-doc precedence and native `parseLanesTable`/review-lanes rendering) into a new `utils/py/_marathon_plan.py`, stdlib-only. The two shims are **folded into the native logic** — `doc_of()` implements the correct precedence directly (no ROADMAP input mutation) and the review-lanes overlay renders in place (no post-render patch) — and `_marathon_plan_node.js` is deleted. `marathon_plan.py` drops the `node --version` hard-require and the `QP_*` env plumbing; a production Python run now needs neither Node nor the JS file (verified by running the Python path with **no `node` on `PATH`**, exit 4, full report + JSON emitted). **The bet:** the native engine must be byte-identical to the Bash/node engine, since a superficially similar plan with changed scheduling or safety semantics is the real risk (issue's own reversibility note) — proven by fixture-level output comparison, not a smoke run. Against the live 160-item ROADMAP, `XYZ_PYTHON=0` (Bash/node) vs `XYZ_PYTHON=1` (native Python) are **byte-identical** across the text report, `--format json`, `--policy derisk-first`, `--require-gh`, and the full 285-line rendered doc; a synthetic corpus proves the two folded shims (docOf divergence + review-lanes overlay) match byte-for-byte too. JS→Python fidelity traps handled explicitly: `JSON.stringify` parity (`separators=(",",":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`, insertion-order keys), JS `String(number)` integer rendering, a ready-but-unrated lane's `null` score coercing to `0` in the sort (JS `a.score - b.score`), and `readdirSync`/`os.listdir` sharing OS order for coverage-drift finding order. `test/marathon-plan.sh` **60/0** (Scenario T's `XYZ_PYTHON=1` parity assertions included). **GH-308's marathon-plan exception is left as documented** — this does not flip authority: `utils/marathon-plan.sh` stays the authoritative, dual-maintained twin and the `XYZ_PYTHON=0` fallback is untouched (the rollback path). The frozen-twin guard still asserts marathon-plan is the Bash-authoritative exception (27/0). **Correction on merge review:** the citation of Scenario T as parity evidence does **not** hold — `run_qp()` never sets `XYZ_PYTHON` and `utils/marathon-plan.sh:6` defaults `${XYZ_PYTHON-1}` to `1`, so T compares the Python lane against itself. Mutating the Bash engine's rendered output at `utils/marathon-plan.sh:956` leaves the suite at **60/0** with both T assertions green. The port is still genuinely proven — the live-ROADMAP comparison sets `XYZ_PYTHON=0` explicitly and was independently reproduced at merge time (text × both policies × `--format json`, byte-identical once the clock is pinned; `timestamp` fields make an unpinned comparison differ on every line) — but *forward* drift protection for the one file GH-308 deliberately exempted does not exist. Filed as [#348](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/348). Also filed from the same review: [#349](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/349) — an unknown flag exits **0** on the default lane where Bash exits 2 (`marathon_plan.py:91-94`, `usage()` ends in `sys.exit(0)` so the following `die()` is unreachable; pre-existing, same class as #322), carrying the three now-stale `UPGRADE.md` references to the deleted `_marathon_plan_node.js`. → [GH-340-NATIVE-PYTHON-MARATHON-PLAN.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-340-NATIVE-PYTHON-MARATHON-PLAN.md) · [#340](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/340)
- **Quicksilver's scope is now stated as what GH-308 actually commits to: "Python-authoritative Tier-A twins."** The ledger said *"Phase out Bash scripts,"* which the epic explicitly does not do — `GH-308-BASH-TWIN-RETIREMENT.md:19` (`non_goals`) rules out porting the 21 Bash-only scripts and `:263` puts `relay-turn-lib.sh` out of scope, and the shipped policy is **freeze, not delete**, with the Bash twins retained as the rollback path. A release description that promises removal it isn't doing makes the milestone unfalsifiable. `RELEASES.md:30` and the GitHub milestone description now both name the Tier-A twins and spell out the two non-goals. **Membership set to #308 + #342** (the last known Bash-only behavior, the `XYZ_DEBUG_LOG` Sentinel Tier-1 capture) — deliberately two issues rather than a wider sweep, so `release-lanes.sh rollup` reports a ratio that means something. It currently reports **0/2 landed on `origin/development`**: #308 `IN FLIGHT` (claimed by `f664dae` but the epic is still open) and #342 `ABSENT` (no trunk reference). #340/#341 is **not** in the milestone — it removed marathon-plan's Node dependency, but marathon-plan is GH-308's carve-out, so it advances the codebase without advancing this release's stated goal.
- **GH-268 Phase 2: the beta report's four onboarding findings closed, plus two dead anchors the report didn't know about.** A walk-in reader hit ~120 lines of beta logistics and the four-mode taxonomy before learning what XYZ *is* or that there was anything to run. `README.md` now opens with the one-line what-it-is, the full **What XYZ is**, the **Quickstart**, and **Then pick your path**; the beta onboarding guide moves below them and its banner is reworded (it no longer claims to "lead this README"). The CLI prerequisites — previously the sixth bullet of a *safety and reversibility* list, i.e. below the fast path that shells out to them — are now their own **Prerequisites** section directly above the fast path, carrying the actual install URLs from #123 (Codex app, Antigravity **CLI**) rather than only asserting the CLIs must be present. `relay-automation/README.md`'s prerequisite section is retitled **"Set up Codex, agy, and Pi (headless bring-up)"** and its `### 1. Prerequisites` becomes `### 1. Install and authenticate the CLIs`, leading with an install/auth table — the report's specific complaint was that the section a newcomer is *routed to for prerequisites* told them how to **test** the CLIs, never how to **install** them; the existing verification commands are kept, after the install step. The Quickstart gains a sandbox callout naming the real failure mode (`mktemp -d` blocked → several minutes of zero output, then failure — reads as a hang, not a permissions error). **Two dead links found while verifying, not in the report:** `README.md:70` and `:156` both pointed at `relay-automation/README.md#headless-bring-up-codex--agy`, which `a595c6f` had already broken by renaming that heading to add "+ Pi" — so both of the README's prerequisite pointers were 404-to-top since 2026-07-23. Fixed here along with the third stale copy in `skills/relay-xyz/SKILL.md`; the retitle would otherwise have broken them a second time. Historical references in `CHANGELOG.md` and `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/` are deliberately left as-is. **Also corrected:** the Quickstart claimed the suite runs "in about a minute" — a fresh clone of `development` on macOS measured **~8 minutes** (133/133, exit 0), so the text now says 5–10 minutes. That overstated claim was half of the report's finding #1; the other half (the run ending with **7 failed tests**) no longer reproduces, so #268's two blocking items are both closed. `skills/relay-automation/relay-pkg.tar.gz` rebuilt via `make-pkg.sh` — editing `relay-automation/README.md` drifts the bundled tarball, which `relay-pkg-freshness.sh` caught as the only red in the first full run. **Deliberately not in this pass:** findings #7 and #8 (relay turn-boundary handoff cues; the review loop checking the diff rather than the file it lands in) are Phase 3, and the cross-model re-test is Phase 4 — neither started. → [GH-268-BETA-ONBOARDING-BUILD-QUALITY-REMEDIATION.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-268-BETA-ONBOARDING-BUILD-QUALITY-REMEDIATION.md) · [#268](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/268)
- **GH-336: the planning-context proposal survived a three-round cross-model relay review and came out materially different — one Blocker was a source that does not exist, and the reversibility claim was prose.** Doc-only; no code shipped. Round 1 (**codex**, `--review-once`, `ALLOW_PATHS=""`) returned *Changes requested* with 2 Blockers: the proposal claimed to consume an existing `utils/release-lanes.sh seed|rollup` foundation — **no such file exists anywhere in the tree**, and the cited companion issue GH-334 has neither a `PROJECT/**` doc nor a ROADMAP line — and Phase 2's "remains opt-in and must retain that escape hatch" was an aspiration with no mechanism behind it. The false premise was **deleted rather than replaced with a prerequisite build**: `RELEASES.md` already carries `Milestone:` as the release → issue-set join key (GH-284 Phase 3, above), so Phase 1 reads it directly and needs no release helper. **The architecture decision neither reviewer raised is the load-bearing one.** `AGENTS.md` names `utils/marathon-plan.sh` as GH-308's one *deliberate* dual-maintained exception, but auditing what that costs found it is **triple**-maintained: `utils/py/marathon_plan.py:10-21` states it delegates rendering to `utils/py/_marathon_plan_node.js`, a hand-maintained 46 KB **copy** of the Bash JS engine with **no sync mechanism**, already "drifted on two points," carrying two standing parity shims, and already repaired once by a dedicated parity marathon (GH-154). New logic in that render path would be maintained in three places, so the overlay is now specified as a standalone Python-only `utils/py/planning_context.py` that appends a section to an already-rendered plan — touching neither engine, needing no `Frozen-twin-exception:` trailer, and leaving the untouched Bash lane as a **free permanent parity oracle for "feature off."** Rounds 2–3 (**agy**, independent QA per P12 separated grading, `GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md` as the stated tie-breaker) confirmed that architecture with citations and caught the revision's own worst defect: the Producer had defaulted the feature to `auto`, which still must *ask* whether a release resolves and whether `gh` is reachable and therefore puts an outward network reach on **every ordinary planning run** — the doc cited **P1** (*a coordination primitive that reaches out is a coordination primitive that can fail or leak*) to justify its `gh`-failure handling while defaulting to a mode that breaches it. Default is now **`off`**, and the kill-switch criterion asserts the **no-flag** invocation is byte-identical *and* issues zero `gh` calls, verified with a `gh` stub that fails loudly if invoked. Also landed: **four signals → three** (issue themes deferred out of Phase 1 on the *Relevant* pillar — no taxonomy exists and creating one is a standing non-goal, so clustering without one is low-confidence by construction); the stickiness closure table's *"no other tool may read the shape"* row became an **enforced CI grep** over `utils/` and `relay-automation/` rather than an assertion; the unauditable *"three real planning runs"* Phase 2 start condition became a **dated in-doc evidence table, empty by construction** (P9 doc-as-runtime-state, not a cache — a point agy explicitly checked against the doc's own non-goals); and every acceptance criterion was rewritten to name a runnable check, with `./validate.sh` / `pdda.sh run` **explicitly demoted to verification-only** so a phase can no longer pass on green gates that never exercised the new behavior. A doc-only **Phase 0** was added for the record-correction work. Net: `phases` 2 → 3, `risk` 3 → 2 on the strength of a structural rather than argued kill switch, and three operator questions reduced to **zero**. The last of them dissolved rather than being decided: Phase 0 had proposed deleting the live `Release: 0.1.0` EXAMPLE block on the grounds that release selection "would otherwise have to special-case" it — but the stated selection rule already skips any block with an empty `Milestone:`, which that block has, so **nothing in GH-336 needs it gone** and the item was removed. GH-336 stays read-only against `RELEASES.md`; the placeholder is genuinely expired (its own `Description` says to delete it "once real entries exist below," and `Quicksilver` now exists) but that is unrelated hygiene for a separate trivial doc fix. agy Approved at round 3/4 — **and a fourth round with a third model then proved that Approved was premature.** Driven with **pi + `qwen3.8-max-preview`** (direct Alibaba `qwen-token-plan` MaaS endpoint, the pairing GH-303 validated at 91 trials / 0 failures), charged specifically with finding what two models that had already agreed **both missed**: 5 `[Should]`, 2 `[Nit]`, 2 verified `[Pass]`, escalated at the round cap. It found **two factually false assertions in the Approved version**, both since verified independently at source. **(1)** The load-bearing kill-switch criterion claimed `off` mode "issues zero `gh` calls, asserted with a `gh` stub that fails loudly if invoked" — but the *base planner* shells out to `gh` in its ordinary render path, so a loud-fail stub fires on the base planner before the overlay is ever reached and the test fails in `off` mode for reasons unrelated to the feature. The criterion conflated "zero *overlay* `gh` calls" with "zero `gh` calls"; agy had `[Pass]`'d it. Fixtures now run under the hermetic `QP_GH_STATE_FILE` seam as a stated precondition, with the loud-fail stub scoped to the overlay's own `gh issue list --milestone`. **(2)** The operator-set churn threshold of "3 or more" was uncalibrated **by an order of magnitude**: measured over the trailing 14 days this repo shows **538 commits**, with `utils/py/` at **40** distinct commits and `relay-automation/` at **59** — so a ladder topping out at 8+ would park the primary code directories permanently at "consider bounded refactor," a signal that fires every run forever. Sharper still, `utils/py/`'s churn *is* the GH-308 Bash phase-out under the `Quicksilver` milestone, so an absolute rule would flag precisely the directed, release-aligned work the release-alignment signal already reports — **double-reporting known work as a discovery**. Replaced with a trailing-baseline ratio (window ÷ trailing-90-day median, laddered 2×/3×/5×), a 3-commit floor so 0→1 cannot spike, the baseline printed beside every signal, release-aware suppression labelling directed work as such, and the issue-linkage parse pinned to the **full commit message body** — round 4 showed the rung swings on that alone (subject-only gives 6 linked commits where whole-message gives 40+). Three further defects: `/10days` is a **prose skill with no executable**, so a criterion promising a "`/10days` dry-run … asserted with a stubbed `gh` state file" described a binary that does not exist (recast as an assertion about `skills/10days/SKILL.md` step ordering); that same hook made `skills/10days/SKILL.md` a **fourth file** absent from the three-file removal seam *and* outside the no-parser CI grep, which covered only `utils/` and `relay-automation/` (both widened); and the advisory **row format was undefined**, so two builders would have emitted incompatible output (pinned as `Claim | Kind | Source | Window`, with `Kind ∈ {fact, inference}` making the *Attested* pillar test-failable rather than stylistic). Round 4 also **confirmed the architecture rather than echoing it**, tracing evidence the earlier rounds asserted: the append seam genuinely existed, and the three-way byte-identical claims can all hold at once — verified through the real firing path, which closed the one risk flagged as unproven. Acting on its latent finding that `marathon_plan.py --check` re-renders with the default mode and `cmp`s against the committed doc (so a plan written under `auto`/`on` then checked under `off` reports **false drift**), the output moved to a **sidecar** `MARATHON-PLAN-<date>.context.md` that never touches the plan doc — which kills the drift hazard, makes "plan doc byte-identical in every mode" true *by construction* instead of by test, and sharpens the removal seam, all at once. **The architecture survived all four rounds unchanged; every defect was in the criteria that were supposed to prove the safety claims** — which is the reusable lesson here, and an argument for a third model over a second. Two out-of-scope pre-existing findings recorded rather than fixed: `marathon-plan.sh:54` documents `--check` as "a drift guard in `validate.sh`" that `validate.sh` never wires (why the drift hazard is latent, not active), and `relay-drive.sh:353-356` rejects a bold-backticked artifact path in a relay Setup line (hit live while scaffolding this review). `utils/pdda/pdda.sh run` green throughout. **Pi reported `cost.total: 0` for the round-4 turn — treat the spend as unmeasured, not free**, given GH-303's history of token-capture bugs in exactly this path. **Correction on rebase (2026-07-30): two of the findings above were true against `c6b98c2` and are no longer true on `development`, which advanced 6 commits during the review.** (i) `utils/release-lanes.sh` **exists** — #330 (GH-284 Phase 4) landed it with exactly the `seed`/`rollup` verbs the original draft claimed to consume, so round 1's Blocker was **premature rather than false**. Re-verified here: `test/gh284-p4-release-lanes.sh` **38/0**, and `release-lanes.sh rollup --milestone Quicksilver` runs live reporting `2/2 landed on origin/main` with per-issue evidence. The claim is restored and Phase 1 now **calls** the script instead of re-deriving release scope, which removes most of that signal's implementation. (ii) The "**triple**-maintained" argument for the standalone module is retired — #340 deleted the copied Node renderer (`utils/py/_marathon_plan_node.js`) and ported the engine natively to `utils/py/_marathon_plan.py`. The decision is unchanged but now rests on narrower, still-true ground: the planner remains **dual**-maintained (the JS engine embedded in `utils/marathon-plan.sh` plus the native Python engine) with **no forward drift guard** — a gap #340's own merge review found and filed as [#348](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/348). **The reusable lesson is the one this repo keeps re-learning:** every verification across four rounds was honestly derived and correctly cited against a tree that had moved. Re-check the base before trusting a long-running review's citations. **Deliberately still open:** no code has been written, so every criterion above is a claim about a build that has not happened — and `relay-drive.sh:353-356`'s Setup preflight was found to reject a `**`bold-backticked`**` artifact path (it strips `**` but leaves the backticks, failing a valid path as "not found in worktree"), which is unfiled. → [GH-336-PLANNING-CONTEXT.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-336-PLANNING-CONTEXT.md) · [#336](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/336)

### Fixed
- **GH-349 + GH-346: marathon-plan accepted a typo'd flag as success, and stamped every generated plan with a hardcoded trunk.** Two front-door defects found while merge-reviewing #341, fixed together because they touch the same entry point. **(a) An unknown flag exited 0 on the lane that runs.** `utils/py/marathon_plan.py`'s `usage()` ended in an unconditional `sys.exit(0)`, which made the `die(f"unknown argument: {arg}")` on the very next line **unreachable** — so `utils/marathon-plan.sh --bogus` returned **0** on the default Python lane while the Bash twin returned 2. The practical bite is the drift gate: `utils/marathon-plan.sh --check --requre-gh || alert "plan drifted"` printed usage, compared nothing, and reported **success**. Same class as #322. Fixed by splitting printing from exiting — `print_usage()` prints and returns, `--help` calls it then exits 0, the unknown-argument branch calls it then `die()`s — which is exactly the Bash shape (`:102` prints only; `:139` is `usage; die "unknown argument: $1"`), so usage lands on stdout and the error on stderr on both lanes. **(b) `branch:` was the string literal `"main"`.** Reported against `rebalance-OS`, whose trunk is `development`, so every generated plan asserted a branch that exists but is not the trunk and is not where any lane would land — and of 41 docs in that repo's `PROJECT/2-WORKING`, only the two *generated* plans said `main`, so a reader had good reason to trust the field. **#346's own fix note was already stale when filed:** it said the Python lane had no `branch:` string of its own so fixing `:945` covered both, which was true only while `marathon_plan.py` shelled out to the copied JS — GH-340 (merged `1d06862`) replaced that with a native engine carrying its own `o.append("branch: main")` at `_marathon_plan.py:1151`. Both engines now derive it, and #348's newly-working parity gate is what makes "both" verifiable rather than hopeful. Deliberately the **trunk, not the current branch**: deriving `HEAD` would make `--check` report drift merely because someone cut a feature branch. Order is `QUEUE_PLAN_BRANCH` (override + hermetic test seam, since the suite's fixture roots are plain directories) → `git symbolic-ref --short refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` with the prefix stripped (the same source `release-lanes.sh` uses) → `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` → `unknown`. The floor is `unknown` rather than `main` because PDDA does not require this key (`pdda.sh:39`) and no consumer was found — a wrong value is worse than an absent one, which is the whole complaint. New Scenarios **U** and **V** in `test/marathon-plan.sh`: **72 passed / 0 failed**, observed **65/7 against pre-fix sources**. The three pre-fix passes are each a thing that was already correct — usage already went to stdout, `--help` already exited 0, and the Bash lane already exited 2, that last being the entire premise of #349. V asserts neither engine still holds the literal, that a non-git root renders `branch: unknown`, that `QUEUE_PLAN_BRANCH` overrides on both lanes, and that **the whole rendered doc stays byte-identical across the two engines** — a derived value computed independently in two places is precisely the drift class #348 exists to catch. Checked by hand on the live 160-item ROADMAP too: both lanes emit the same 233-line doc. **Also corrected:** three `UPGRADE.md` spots (`:164`, the `:246` interpreter matrix, and `:251`'s "the 10 non-marathon-plan twins") still described `_marathon_plan_node.js`, which GH-340 deleted. Low severity — Node remains a baseline requirement via `bin/tick`, so the document's operative conclusion was never wrong — but two pointed at a path that no longer exists, and the carve-out had inverted: marathon-plan's Python lane is now the one entry point that does *not* need Node for its own work. → [GH-349-346-MARATHON-PLAN-CLEANUP.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-349-346-MARATHON-PLAN-CLEANUP.md) · [#349](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/349) · [#346](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/346)
- **GH-348: the repo's only Bash↔Python parity assertion compared the Python lane to the Python lane, and so could not fail.** `test/marathon-plan.sh`'s `run_qp()` invoked `bash "$QP"` and never set `XYZ_PYTHON`, while `utils/marathon-plan.sh:6` reads `${XYZ_PYTHON-1}` — **default 1**. So Scenario T, labelled *"matches between shell and `XYZ_PYTHON=1`"*, compared Python against Python. Proven by mutation, not by argument: corrupting the Bash engine's rendered banner at `utils/marathon-plan.sh:956` left the file at **60 passed / 0 failed**, both T assertions green. **Why this one mattered more than a generic coverage gap:** `marathon-plan` is the *deliberate* exception to GH-308's Python-authoritative policy — `utils/marathon-plan.sh` stays dual-maintained — so cross-lane drift here is a live risk that has already materialised twice, in **GH-154** (the copy missing the GH-48 zone model) and **GH-255** (two further gaps papered over by pre/post-processing *around* the copied program). Scenario T was added by GH-154 to prevent the third instance and never could. Surfaced while merge-reviewing #341, which cited Scenario T as its parity evidence; the port itself was still genuinely proven, by a live-ROADMAP comparison that sets `XYZ_PYTHON=0` explicitly. Fixed with three things, not one: a new **`run_qp_sh()`** forcing `XYZ_PYTHON=0` for T's left side (the other 58 cases deliberately keep `run_qp` — unset is what production runs, so they are correctly exercising the live lane); a **loud counted `SKIP`** when `node` is absent, since the Bash engine hard-requires it at `:146` and falling back to `run_qp` there would restore the exact tautology while looking like a pass; and an **in-run mutation self-check** that renders with a mutated copy of the Bash engine and asserts the difference *is* detected, so a future refactor cannot quietly point both sides at one lane again — with a failure message that names both possible causes instead of guessing one. **61/0** on a clean tree, and the mutation that previously left 60/0 now yields **59 passed / 2 failed**, which is the acceptance criterion the issue set. The dry-run half of T still passes under that particular mutation, correctly: the banner appears only in the rendered doc, which is why the self-check compares the doc. On a no-node PATH: 57/1/**2 skipped**, the one failure (`F: json lines not all valid`) reproducing identically pre-fix and left alone — Node is a hard baseline requirement (UPGRADE.md §2), so a no-node run is not a supported configuration. **Third instance this week of one shape:** an assertion comparing a thing to a freshly-derived copy of itself — this, #342 (GH-281's test `sed`-extracted the helpers from the *Bash* file, passing for a year while the shipped lane had zero references), and #351 (the dashboard test regenerates the artifact it then validates). In all three the output reads as coverage and the gap is invisible from the pass line. → [GH-348-MARATHON-PLAN-PARITY-GATE.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-348-MARATHON-PLAN-PARITY-GATE.md) · [#348](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/348)
- **GH-342: arming `XYZ_DEBUG_LOG=1` captured nothing, because the Sentinel Tier-1 hooks existed only on the lane that stopped running.** GH-281 shipped the Tier-1 debug capture into `relay-automation/marathon-drive.sh`; that file `exec`s `utils/py/marathon_drive.py` near the top and Python is the default lane since GH-264, so `grep -c 'XYZ_DEBUG_LOG' utils/py/marathon_drive.py` was **0** and an operator arming the capture to diagnose a bad marathon got an empty `debug.log` — reasonably concluding there was nothing to capture. The last known item in the Bash-only class (#322 `--log-github`, #331 cost summary, #289 build-turn guard, and the 16 ported in #338); deliberately deferred by that audit and filed so the deferral would not evaporate. **Why it hid is the sharper finding:** GH-281's own acceptance test, `test/sentinel-driver-hooks.sh`, `sed`-extracts the helper functions out of the **Bash** file and sources them into a controlled shell — so it has always passed while asserting a lane nobody runs. A feature that is off by default and silently does nothing when switched on emits no signal in either state, and its test was pointed at the wrong lane. All six Bash sites are now ported into `utils/py/marathon_drive.py` at **module scope** (the GH-322 `runlog_find_comment_id` precedent — a helper reachable only through a full driven run has its format contract asserted by nothing): `xyz_debug_log_append` + `_json_esc`, the escalation hook, the lane-park hook (moved *inside* `lane_attempt_gate` before its `sys.exit(8)`, since the Python gate exits directly where the Bash caller emits on `rc==8`), the inline stale-lock record (emitted **before** the reclaim, so the finding survives a failed reclaim), and both `harvest-findings.sh` spawns in `escalate()` / `save_transcript()`. Preserved exactly: opt-in **default OFF** (only the literal `1` arms it), `DEBUG_LOG_FILE` honored including the empty-value fallback, the JSONL field order with its empty `line`, C0/DEL normalisation, and best-effort appends that can never change the driven run's exit code. **Two things reproduced rather than improved:** the stale-lock record's *shorter* 6-field shape with an unescaped `repo` (an artifact of the Bash helper not being defined that early in the file) is carried as-is — fixing it on one lane only would manufacture the very divergence this issue removes — and `json.dumps` was avoided because it is free to differ on separators and key order while the two lanes must emit identical bytes. New `test/gh342-sentinel-debug-log-python.sh` **29/0**, observed **2 pass / 11 fail against pre-fix code**, registered in `validate.sh`. Every case drives the **default** lane (`XYZ_PYTHON` unset) or the module directly; a case pinned to `XYZ_PYTHON=0` would have passed on day one for the wrong reason. Coverage includes default-off asserting the file's **absence** rather than its emptiness, cross-lane byte parity against the real extracted Bash helper (3 cases, incl. tab/quote/backslash/newline/NUL/DEL input and a `--target-root` run), **end-to-end** on the default lane via a real `--dry-run` over a stale driver lock, and a cross-lane end-to-end comparison — the case that would have caught this on day one. The read-only-directory case **skips loudly under root** instead of reporting a pass it did not earn. What is *not* claimed: the escalation and lane-park hooks sit past the `--dry-run` exit, so they carry an **AST assertion** that they are called from their real call sites plus record-level parity, and the test says so in place rather than implying end-to-end coverage. The Bash twin is untouched — **no `Frozen-twin-exception` spent.** One defect was caught in the test before it shipped: its Python block's tallies were not folded into the file's counters, so an in-heredoc failure would have printed in red and still left the file reporting `0 fail` — the same green-signal shape the test exists to close. → [GH-342-SENTINEL-DEBUG-LOG-PYTHON-LANE.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-342-SENTINEL-DEBUG-LOG-PYTHON-LANE.md) · [#342](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/342)
- **GH-332: the Phase 4 rollup reported a release as finished when it had barely started — it fetched each issue's open/closed state and threw it away.** The tool's very first real output was `Quicksilver: 1/1 landed`, off a milestone whose single issue is **#308, an OPEN multi-phase epic**, whose only claiming commit was `GH-308: re-scope Phase 0/1 after #278 was fixed dual-lane` — planning, not delivery. The scope matcher was doing exactly what it was designed to do; the bug was in what that answer was taken to mean. *"Has any commit claimed this issue?"* is a different question from *"is this issue finished?"*, and the second one already had an authoritative answer sitting unused in the JSON: **a human closed the issue.** Landing now requires both a claiming commit **and** a closed issue. A fourth bucket, `claimed-but-open` (rendered `IN FLIGHT`), carries the rest and is excluded from the `N/M landed` headline — Quicksilver now reads `0/1 landed` with one in flight, which is true. **Commit type is deliberately still not filtered:** #332 raised whether `docs(GH-N):`/`chore(GH-N):` should count as delivery at all, and the answer is no-change — it adds false negatives for genuinely doc-only issues and is the weaker signal, since a human closing an issue outranks a commit-message prefix; the type stays visible anyway because the evidence line prints the whole subject. `test/gh284-p4-release-lanes.sh` **34 pass / 4 fail against the shipped code → 38 / 0**, on a fixture carrying #308's real subject shape. Two of the new assertions exist only to stop the *tally* from lying: the `IN FLIGHT` count must be named in the summary, and the four buckets must sum to the total, so no state can silently vanish from the denominator. A bug in the test itself surfaced the same way — `state_of()` read the label with `awk '{print $1}'`, which the new two-word `IN FLIGHT` would have truncated to `IN`, and the `grep` feeding it would not have matched the row at all. → [GH-284-MARATHON-CLOSEOUT-PR.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-284-MARATHON-CLOSEOUT-PR.md) · [#332](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/332)
- **GH-333: the run log's `driver still running` field could only ever say `running` — dropped, and the surviving exit code now says what it means.** The exit hook asked `driver_heartbeat_status()` *before* clearing the heartbeat, and the PID it tested belonged to the process doing the asking, which is alive by definition. The field was a constant wearing the clothes of a measurement — the exact shape this program keeps finding (#315, #319, #320, #322, #331). It was also in the wrong place: the comment is posted *at exit*, so it can never tell a reader whether a driver is alive now. The thing that answers that is the heartbeat **file**, which works and is untouched — this lane still writes and clears it, and the reader observers actually use (`rtl_driver_heartbeat_status`) lives in `relay-turn-lib.sh`, a shared runtime dependency rather than a frozen twin. Writer here, reader there, so removing the Python-side status function cost nothing. **Nothing was lost, because nothing was ever posted:** `--log-github` was Bash-only and inert on the default lane until #322's port landed the day before, and a scan of the likely lane issues found **zero** run-log comments in existence — so no reader breaks and no history is invalidated. The half worth keeping was folded into the neighbouring line, which was already carrying the useful signal as a bare number: `- driver exit: \`3\` (no-progress escalation)`. `_exit_meaning()` is at module scope, total (an unmapped code renders `unrecognised exit code` rather than an empty parenthetical that would read like a meaning), and maps 1/2/8 defensively even though lock contention, `die()` and the lane-attempt cap all exit before the run log arms and so can never appear in one. **Python only — the Bash twin keeps the old field, on purpose.** GH-308 froze the eleven Tier-A twins and AGENTS.md directs behavior fixes to the Python twin; editing `marathon-drive.sh` would have required a `Frozen-twin-exception:` trailer, which #321 just narrowed to per-file declarations and which exists for *safety* defects (the GH-319 precedent), not cosmetic ones. Divergence in a frozen fallback slated for deletion is the intended outcome of that policy, not a new parity defect. `test/gh322-runlog-python-lane.sh` 26/0 → **29/0**, asserting the gloss, the field's **absence** (re-adding it fails), and that the heartbeat writer survived the removal. `test/gh308-frozen-twin-guard.sh --check` confirms no frozen twin was touched. → [#333](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/333)
- **GH-322 (the port): GH-284 Phase 2 shipped in July and had never once executed — *both* halves lived only in the Bash twin.** #324 stopped `--log-github` being silently discarded; this makes it do something. `relay-automation/marathon-drive.sh` `exec`s `utils/py/marathon_drive.py` at its own **line 18**, while the `EXIT` trap that runs the run log and stops the heartbeat is installed at **line 244** — so under the default lane (`XYZ_PYTHON` unset since GH-264) the process was replaced before either could ever be wired up. Every marathon since Phase 2 merged ran, exited 0, reported success, and observed nothing. **#322's own scope statement was wrong, and the correction is the substantive finding here:** it deferred only the run log on the grounds that "the driver heartbeat *is* already in the Python twin (12 references)" — those 12 matches are `xyz_marathon_heartbeat_*`, the **GH-75 `XYZ.heartbeat.json` session record**, an unrelated file and feature; `grep -c driver_heartbeat utils/py/marathon_drive.py` returned **0**. Two different features named "heartbeat" in one file, told apart only by prefix, is what let a grep agree with the wrong conclusion. Both halves are now ported: the heartbeat (write, refresh thread, clear, `RTL_DRIVER_HEARTBEAT_FILE` honored) writes a record byte-compatible with `rtl_driver_heartbeat_write` — same six fields, same atomic `mkstemp`+`os.replace` — so the Bash reader and the Python writer agree; and `--log-github` is a real flag with `run_gate_result` tracking behind it. Exit handling mirrors the Bash trap's contract exactly (code resolved first, hooks in a `finally`, original code re-exited), so reporting can never change the driven run's result. `runlog_find_comment_id` sits at **module scope**: in Bash the same parser could only be reached through a `python3` subprocess, and the invocation shape itself (SC2259) silently broke it for a release — in-process kills the failure class, and importable means the test drives the real function instead of a copy of its logic. New `test/gh322-runlog-python-lane.sh` (**5 pass / 19 fail against pre-change code, 26 / 0 after**) is the counterpart to `test/gh284-runlog-heartbeat.sh`, every driver invocation in which is pinned to `XYZ_PYTHON=0` — which is exactly why that suite could not see any of this; the new file `unset XYZ_PYTHON`s deliberately. `test/gh322-unknown-arg-rejection.sh`'s `--log-github` block was **inverted**: "refuse loudly and name the `XYZ_PYTHON=0` workaround" was an interim contract, and a lane that still refused a flag it now implements would be the bug. **Deliberately left as-is:** the run log's `driver still running` field can only ever read `running` — the hook asks for status *before* clearing the heartbeat, and the PID it checks is the process asking. The Bash twin has the identical ordering (`:238` then `:239`), so it is inherited, not introduced, and porting it faithfully beats opening a silent twin divergence — the exact disease #320 and #322 are. → [GH-284-MARATHON-CLOSEOUT-PR.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-284-MARATHON-CLOSEOUT-PR.md) · [#322](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/322)
- **GH-320: the executing Python lane capped turns at a third of the documented budget.** An agy review turn died with `agy -p exceeded 300s wall-clock cap` on a shim whose header *and* code both read 900. XYZ has been Python-default since GH-264, so `relay-automation/agy-turn.sh` `exec`s `utils/py/agy-turn.py` — which defaulted to **300**. Same split in `codex-turn` (900↔300) and `claude-turn` (600↔300); `aider-turn` and `pi-turn` were consistent. Because Python is the lane that runs, 300 s was the **real** ceiling for three of five turn-takers while every operator-facing document said otherwise, and the failure surfaces as `exit 7` — *turn timeout / hang* — which reads as a hung model rather than a misconfigured ceiling. The natural response to that, retrying, burns another turn at the same wrong cap. **GH-308's freeze does not cover this class:** freezing the Bash twins stops them drifting *forward* and says nothing about values that had already diverged before the banners landed; nothing in the suite compared the two numbers. Fixed by aligning the three Python defaults with their twins. New `test/gh320-twin-timeout-parity.sh` reads the default out of **both** files for all five shims and compares them — **no expected value is hardcoded**, since a third copy of the number would just be a third thing to drift — and separately asserts that each Bash header's documented `(default: N)` matches that file's own code, because the header is what misled. 10 pass / 0 fail. **Deliberately still open:** nothing establishes the turn timeout was the only constant that diverged pre-freeze; a general Bash↔Python constant-parity sweep belongs to GH-308 Phase 2. → [GH-320-TWIN-TURN-TIMEOUT-DRIFT.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-320-TWIN-TURN-TIMEOUT-DRIFT.md) · [#320](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/320)
- **GH-321: the frozen-twin escape hatch was range-scoped — one trailer excused every twin edit in the PR.** The `Frozen-twin-exception:` hatch shipped in PR #318 passed the guard for **every** frozen twin touched in `BASE..HEAD` as soon as *any* commit in the range carried the trailer — including files nobody reasoned about, in commits that declared nothing, possibly by a different author. CI then printed `1 commit(s) declare Frozen-twin-exception — allowing:` and listed the **declaring** commit, so the log read like a narrow reviewed exception while the undeclared edit was **never named**. The trailer's whole value is making an exception auditable, and range scoping broke exactly that property in the case where it counts. Marathon PRs are the common case and the worst shape for it: 4+ autonomous lanes on one branch make an undeclared twin edit riding behind someone else's declared exception the expected accident, not an exotic one. Same class as #319 and #315 — *a green signal that means less than it appears, with nothing in the output revealing the gap* — and it was disclosed at merge time rather than found later. A trailer must now **name the twin(s) it covers** (`Frozen-twin-exception: relay-automation/marathon-drive.sh — <reason>`), and every changed twin in the range must be named by some trailer; failures name the uncovered file. **The rule moved out of inline `ci.yml` shell and into the guard script as `--allow-exceptions`** — inline YAML is unreachable from the suite, which is part of why the looseness shipped at all; `ci.yml` is now a single call. Per-file *across the range*, deliberately not "the trailer must be on the same commit as the edit": a later fixup correcting an earlier commit is ordinary and should not need re-declaration, and what the looseness actually cost was attribution, which naming the file recovers. A typo'd path fails loudly rather than silently covering nothing; the bare path-less form no longer covers anything (that form *is* the looseness); reason text is not scanned for paths, so a reason may cite `validate.sh` without being read as a coverage claim; strict mode (what `validate.sh` and `--staged` use) is unchanged. `test/gh308-frozen-twin-guard.sh` **15/0 → 27/0**, with a fixture built on **two** frozen twins because with one the range-scoped and file-scoped rules are indistinguishable — which is how this shipped. One case **reproduces** the defect rather than only asserting its absence: it evaluates #318's exact predicate on the same fixture and asserts it *would* have allowed the PR, so the suite says so if the fixture ever stops exercising the bug. The single historical trailer (`07ae1e7`) is the bare form and would fail this rule; it is already merged and CI only judges an open PR's own range, so no existing history is re-evaluated. → [#321](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/321)
- **PR #318 agy review: two Blockers closed, one Should implemented differently, one declined.** The review of the marathon PR was itself driven through the harness (`--review-once`, `ALLOW_PATHS=""`, exit 5 = changes requested). Both Blockers were re-verified against source before being acted on. **(1)** `AGENTS.md` asserted that "CI supplies `GH308_FROZEN_TWIN_BASE`" — `grep -rn GH308_FROZEN_TWIN_BASE .github/` returned **0 matches**, so the frozen-twin guard had no enforcement anywhere. Added the `Frozen Bash twin guard (GH-308)` step to `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, with a `Frozen-twin-exception: <reason>` commit trailer as the documented escape hatch so a legitimate exception is auditable in `git log` rather than invisible. **(2)** The reviewer argued a silently-fake safety gate outranks the freeze policy, so `relay-automation/marathon-drive.sh:493` is patched too — but the obvious shape was wrong: `printf '%q'` is eval-safe yet backslash-escapes the space rather than removing it, so the preflight regex at `:511` still captured `.../GH\` and would have `die`d "script file does not exist", trading a false pass for a hard failure. Single-quoting is eval-safe *and* regex-tractable, and is byte-identical to `shlex.quote()`'s output for the same path; the preflight regex now matches a fully quoted token. `test/gh319-gate-path-with-space.sh` drives **both** lanes — the `XYZ_PYTHON=0` cases fail against pre-fix Bash (7/2) and pass after (9/0). **(3)** The proposed fix for the GH-293 guard-marker fragility required editing `relay-drive.sh`, a frozen twin, to satisfy a `[Should]` — the wrong trade; the manifest now matches the guard's user-facing **diagnostic** (`turn cannot report: relay file`), which does not get reflowed. The underlying point was sharper than the example: `test/gh293-vendored-guard-drift.sh` was itself restating the old literal, so a manifest change would have left the test green while the guard check was dead — the fixture now **derives** the pattern from `SAFETY_GUARD_PATTERNS` and asserts it stripped something (15/0). **(4)** Declined the suggestion to isolate `test/pdda-repo-contract.sh` from `validate.sh`: CI already fails the PR on those errors, so the coupling predates the change — what changed is finding out locally in 8 minutes instead of after a push, which is precisely what GH-311 exists to fix.
- **GH-319: every marathon phase reported "gate passed" without the gate ever running — the default gate word-split on the space in the repo's own path and executed an unrelated 0-byte file.** `utils/py/marathon_drive.py` built its default gate as an unquoted string (`f"bash {root}/validate.sh"`) and handed it to `subprocess.run(..., shell=True)`. At a clone under `.../Documents/GH Repos/xyz-3-agents-swarm` the shell split on the space and ran `bash $HOME/Documents/GH`, passing `Repos/.../validate.sh` as a positional argument. **`$HOME/Documents/GH` exists on this machine as a 0-byte file**, so bash executed an empty script: exit 0, no output, 0.0 s. Reproduced in isolation before anything was changed. **All four lanes of the 2026-07-27 gate-and-fleet-integrity marathon were approved with no gate**, while `bash validate.sh` on that exact tree was RED with two genuine failures (`relay-pkg-freshness`, `skill-extract` — GH-308's freeze banners had drifted `relay-pkg.tar.gz`). It was caught by timing, not by any signal the harness emits: `.tick/events/` showed **6.6 s and 7.5 s** between `agy-done` and `marathon.phase.approved` against a **483 s** real suite. **GH-238's gate-runnable preflight — which exists for exactly this — did not fire**, because its `^\s*(bash|/\S*/bash)\s+(\S+)` regex captured the same wrong fragment and `os.path.isfile()` on the decoy returned `True`; the guard and the gate agreed on the wrong file, and the preflight even carried quote-stripping logic for a quoted argument the default never produced. This is the third instance of one failure class in this program, after GH-315 (a stale relay token let a build-nothing run report success) and the sandboxed-`Monitor` liveness gap folded into GH-284 Phase 2: **a broken observation layer where failure is invisible and every available signal agrees.** Fixed with `shlex.quote` on the default path and `shlex.split` in the preflight, so the runnability check inspects the file the shell would actually execute. **Regression coverage was written to demonstrate the bug, not merely assert the fix** — `test/gh319-gate-path-with-space.sh` builds its fixture at a spaced path *and plants the same 0-byte decoy at the split point*, so a regression cannot hide behind a split prefix that happens not to exist; against pre-fix code the driver prints `phase p1 complete — STATUS: Approved, gate passed` and exits 0 while the fixture's gate exits 1 (4 pass / 2 fail), and post-fix it is 6/0 with the driver exiting 5. Registered in `validate.sh`. **Deliberately not fixed:** `relay-automation/marathon-drive.sh:493` has the identical construction, but GH-308 froze that file in this same PR and AGENTS.md now directs behavior fixes to the Python twin — so an `XYZ_PYTHON=0` run at a spaced path still has a fake gate until GH-308 Phase 2 retires the twin. Historical marathon runs on this machine that reported "gate passed" are retroactively unverified; no audit of them is planned.
