- **[GH-198](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/198) Bug 1 — `rtl_enforce`'s "file-scoped" commit had no pathspec, so pre-existing staged content silently rode along into the relay's commit.** `relay-automation/relay-turn-lib.sh`'s commit step staged each allowlisted path independently (`git add -A -- <path>`, tolerant of a non-matching entry) but then committed with a bare `git commit` — no trailing pathspec — which commits the ENTIRE staged index, not just what the turn just staged. Reproduced live twice (2026-07-10/11) driving `relay-drive.sh --target-root` against a repo with pre-existing staged WIP: it rode into the relay's auto-commit under a message that only named the relay file. Fixed by scoping the commit to the allowlist — but a naive `git commit -- "${RTL_ALLOW[@]}"` reintroduces the GH-29 class of bug (unlike `git add -A --`, `git commit --` is NOT per-path independent: one pathspec entry that matches nothing aborts the WHOLE commit), caught by `test/relay-target-root-newfile.sh` regressing during my own fix. Corrected: cross-check each allowlist entry against what's actually staged (`git diff --cached --name-only`) before including it in the commit pathspec, and treat an empty result (only pre-existing unrelated content was staged, the turn changed nothing) as the existing "no tracked changes" no-op rather than either an aborted commit or a commit-everything fallback. Kernel-path change (`relay-turn-lib.sh` is orchestrator-only per this repo's own zone model) — implemented directly, not via a headless marathon builder turn. New `test/relay-commit-pathspec.sh` (9/9): proves pre-existing staged content is excluded and remains staged/untouched, plus a control case confirming a real allowlisted artifact still commits normally. Full regression sweep across every test touching `rtl_enforce` (`archive-commit.sh`, `relay-self-sufficiency.sh`, `relay-concurrent-commit.sh`, `relay-turn-trace.sh`, `relay-dep-drift.sh`, `shim-worktree.sh`, `relay-turn-handoff.sh`, `relay-target-root-newfile.sh`) green. Rebuilt `skills/relay-automation/relay-pkg.tar.gz`. Full `validate.sh` green except the pre-existing, tracked [#208](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/208) environment red. GH-198's Bug 2 (doc/UX gap around reviewing an uncommitted same-repo artifact) remains open.
- **Raised the `claude-turn.sh`/`aider-turn.sh` default per-turn timeout from 300s to 600s.** `codex-turn.sh` and `agy-turn.sh` were already raised to 900s by GH-205 (2026-07-15); these two remaining shims (used for `--builder claude` and the Aider/OpenRouter lane) were still at the original 300s default, too short for a real build turn. Also corrected a stale doc table in `skills/relay-xyz/SKILL.md` that still advertised codex/agy's timeout as 300s post-GH-205. `RELAY_TURN_TIMEOUT_S` remains overridable per-call; no test pinned the old value. `test/claude-turn.sh` (34/34), `test/aider-turn.sh` (44/44) green; full `validate.sh` green except the pre-existing, tracked [#208](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/208) environment red. Trivial doc/default-constant change, no GH issue per the ≤2–3 line exemption.
- **[GH-212](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/212) — marathon builder-default (no billed CLI) and plan-location (`PROJECT/2-WORKING`) are now explicit, enforced defaults in the vendored harness.** Two previously-implicit conventions, made first-class: (1) `marathon.sh`/`marathon-drive.sh` (and the `XYZ_PYTHON=1` port, `utils/py/marathon_drive.py`) now default `--builder` to `codex` instead of `claude` — `codex`/`agy` bill via subscription, not a per-call Anthropic API charge like the headless `claude` CLI subprocess; `--builder claude` remains fully supported as a documented, cost-acknowledged opt-in (`--help`/usage text on both scripts, plus `MARATHON.example.yaml`'s header). This also reconciles `marathon.sh`'s default with what `swarm-preflight.sh` already suggested (`--builder codex`). (2) `marathon.sh --plan PATH` now refuses (exit 2) a plan that resolves outside `PROJECT/2-WORKING/` in the target repo — exempt: paths under the harness's own home (`MARATHON_HOME`, e.g. shipped examples) or an explicit `MARATHON_ALLOW_PLAN_OUTSIDE_WORKING=1` override — so an agent authoring a new marathon from only the vendored bundle can't drift into a standalone top-level folder (a real, tracked instance of exactly this drift already exists in this repo at `marathon-plans/2026-07-15-gh205-207/`, left as historical/out of scope here). Both conventions documented in `GUIDING-PRINCIPLES.md` → Conventions. Operator chose the simple builder-default swap over a more invasive `--builder self`/orchestrator-hands-back-the-turn design (recorded in the capture doc as a non-goal, revisitable later). Fixed two macOS `/var`→`/private/var` symlink canonicization gaps in the new plan-location guard (surfaced by `test/marathon.sh`'s vendored-install case) by resolving both the plan path and `ROOT`/`MARATHON_HOME` with `pwd -P` before comparing, mirroring `swarm-preflight.sh`'s existing pattern. Updated `test/marathon.sh` (fixtures moved under `PROJECT/2-WORKING/`, 3 new guard cases: refuse-by-default, override, harness-home-exempt), `test/marathon-drive.sh` (pinned `--builder claude` in its shared `run_driver` helper so existing claude-identity assertions keep testing what they always tested, plus a new dedicated case proving the unset default is now `codex`), and `test/xyz-harness-hooks.sh` (moved its two real-marathon.sh fixtures under `PROJECT/2-WORKING/`, changed a `reviewer: codex` phase to `reviewer: agy` to avoid a builder==reviewer collision now that the default builder is `codex`, added a `CODEX_BIN` stub alongside the existing `CLAUDE_BIN`/`AGY_BIN` ones). Full `validate.sh`: only the pre-existing, tracked [#208](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/208) environment-dependent red remained. Capture doc: [GH-212-MARATHON-BUILDER-PLAN-DEFAULTS.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-212-MARATHON-BUILDER-PLAN-DEFAULTS.md).

Documented when to choose all three layouts and generated a third real-repo example at `ARCHITECTURE/system-diagram-top-down.json`/`.html` (31 nodes, 43 edges). Verification: focused renderer fixtures 19/19, Node syntax check, skill validation, zero dangling endpoints, headless-Chrome visual inspection, and an isolated 6-node/6-edge forward test. - **[GH-197](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/197) — spike: two-axis (disclosure/steering) diagnostic tag pass on AGENTS.md, closed same day, Codex-reviewed via `relay-xyz`.** Ran the Phase 0 diagnostic proposed in `PROJECT/1-INBOX/AGENT-MD-PYDANTIC.md` (analysis of pydantic.dev's "What makes a good agent harness"): tagged all 8 current AGENTS.md Operating Principles as disclosure/steering/both. Result contradicts the source doc's prediction of a disclosure-heavy skew — the actual split is steering-heavy or balanced (6 steering-leaning vs. 4 disclosure-leaning); this count held up under review. Ran a 3-round Codex relay review ([relay-system/2026-07-10/gh-197-spike-review.md](relay-system/2026-07-10/gh-197-spike-review.md)) against the doc's own 5 self-checks: r1 `Changes requested` — caught that the original "ROUTER.md owns disclosure / AGENTS.md owns steering" explanation overstated a strict file-level split (ROUTER.md's startup sequence has real steering-shaped verify gates — "run `./validate.sh`"/"run `pdda.sh run`" before claiming success, `ROUTER.md:24-25,35` — and AGENTS.md opens with disclosure-shaped routing pointers, `AGENTS.md:3-9`) and that "no further work needed"/"repo already runs the split" overreached a two-file read; fixed by narrowing to "ROUTER leans disclosure, AGENTS leans steering" and retracting the repo-wide sufficiency claim. r2 `Changes requested` — caught the fix only touched the doc body, leaving the YAML frontmatter `status:` line still asserting the retracted repo-wide claim; fixed. r3 `Approved`. The core count verdict (AGENTS.md's 8 principles aren't disclosure-heavy) was `[Pass]` throughout and never changed. One candidate missing-steering-rule flagged (a standing "stop and ask when ambiguous" rule lives only in system-level Auto Mode instructions, not in either repo-owned file) but not actioned — out of spike scope. Findings written back into [GH-197-AGENTS-MD-TWO-AXIS-SPIKE.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-197-AGENTS-MD-TWO-AXIS-SPIKE.md).
- **[GH-192](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/192) built — `rollup.sh` now bridges `marathon-scan.sh`'s preflight readiness into the Obsidian daily rollup.** `utils/hq/rollup.sh` shells out to `utils/hq/marathon-scan.sh` and appends its aggregate report **verbatim** (never through `agy`) as a new `## Marathon Readiness (cross-repo preflight)` section beneath the existing agy-synthesized ROADMAP queue/parked summary in the same `HQ-Daily-Rollup.md` — deliberately not LLM-paraphrased, since the ready/blocked-not-promoted/blocked-other/stale-already-landed/ambiguous verdicts are the one deterministic, structured signal in the pipeline. One behavior change: `rollup.sh` now always writes `OUT_FILE` (with a placeholder if the ROADMAP scrape found nothing), instead of exiting without writing anything, so the marathon section still reaches Obsidian on a quiet ROADMAP day. New `AGY_BIN` test seam (matches `consult.sh`'s `CODEX_BIN`/`GEMINI_BIN`/`AIDER_BIN` convention). New `test/hq-rollup.sh` (12/12: stubs `agy`, reuses `marathon-scan.sh`'s real fixture pattern so the marathon section is a genuine classification, not a stub). Along the way found `test/hq-marathon-scan.sh` (GH-158, 11/11 green) had never been registered in `validate.sh`'s `TESTS` array — fixed alongside registering the new test. Full `bash validate.sh`: 109/111 (only the two pre-existing, unrelated reds — `acorn-extract.sh`, `python:test_python_layer.py`). `pdda.sh run` clean. Promoted the capture doc to [PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-192-HQ-MARATHON-OBSIDIAN-ROLLUP.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-192-HQ-MARATHON-OBSIDIAN-ROLLUP.md), moved its `ROADMAP.md` pointer from Queue to In progress. **Phase 1 (same day):** dogfooded the real `rollup.sh` end to end — real `agy`, real `hq_known_repos`/`hq_repo_resolve`, real `marathon-scan.sh` — first into a scratch vault (found 3 real repos, 6 marathon docs, 2 active lanes), then, with explicit operator go-ahead, live against the operator's actual `~/Documents/Noel Saw/Dashboards/HQ-Daily-Rollup.md`, confirmed both sections present. One cosmetic nit accepted as-is: the embedded marathon-scan report's own H1 title sits under the merged note's H2 section heading — renders fine in Obsidian, not fixed. **Phase 2 (same day):** operator chose to also fix `hq_repo_resolve()`'s pre-existing double-candidate bug — before writing a fix, checked whether it was actually still live and found it wasn't: already fixed by GH-159 (PR #179, merged 2026-07-08), confirmed via a live synthetic reproduction (two XYZ registry rows resolving to the same coord path now correctly collapse, preferring the vendored `.xyz` install) plus an existing passing regression test (`test/hq.sh:41`) — no code change made there. Redirected the remaining scope to the Phase 1 heading nit: `rollup.sh`'s marathon-section awk now demotes every heading in the embedded report by 2 levels (fence-aware) so it nests properly under `## Marathon Readiness`. `test/hq-rollup.sh` extended to 14/14; re-ran full `validate.sh` (still 109/111, same 2 pre-existing reds) and re-dogfooded live against the operator's real vault to confirm the fix landed in the actual file. **Phase 3 (next day, 2026-07-10):** opened [PR #196](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/pull/196), then ran `/consult` (Codex + agy) on it. agy's answer was mechanically stamped `FAIL` by the isolation-breach detector for citing the real repo root in its citations instead of the isolation worktree (a known false-positive pattern, GH-183/GH-187) — read the content anyway, it was legitimate. Codex caught a real `[Blocker]` neither the author nor agy had: `command -v "$AGY_BIN"` was still an unconditional startup check, so a machine with no `agy` on `PATH` at all would exit before ever reaching the marathon-scan section, undermining the "always reaches Obsidian" claim — independently re-verified against the actual code before agreeing with it. Fixed: the `agy` check is now lazy, only enforced inside the `if [ -s "$RAW_FILE" ]` branch (`node`'s check stays unconditional, correctly, since both the ROADMAP loop and `marathon-scan.sh` need it regardless). Also fixed two `[Should]` findings both models converged on independently: `MARATHON_TMP` cleanup now uses `trap ... EXIT` instead of one `rm -f` at the end (survives an early abort), and added a `MARATHON_SCAN_BIN` test seam (mirrors `AGY_BIN`) so the `marathon-scan.sh` failure path is actually testable. `test/hq-rollup.sh` extended to 21/21 (added Case C: marathon-scan.sh failing, and Case D: agy entirely absent from PATH — the direct regression test for the Blocker fix). Re-ran full `validate.sh` (still 109/111, same 2 pre-existing reds) and `pdda.sh run` (clean), and re-dogfooded live against the operator's real vault once more post-fix.
- **`ARCHITECTURE/system-diagram.json`/`.html` — suite-wide Safety & Trust boundary markers, ATE folded in as a subsystem.** Added a `trust` field to every node (31 total, up from 24) and a top-level `trust_boundaries` array naming 8 cross-cutting boundaries: agent-CLI writes are contained by `relay-turn-lib.sh` not the agent itself, a dispatch shim only acts on `RELAY_AGENT` identity match, where real external credentials/side effects cross (OpenRouter/Google search/GitHub), `trust-audit`'s post-hoc scan is partial not full containment (GH-183 gap), ATE's `control.json` is Claude-write-only, **ATE's scratch-repo boundary is unenforced in code (docs-only)** — the one boundary in the whole suite that exists only as an operational instruction, ATE's single external side effect (`gh issue create`) per run, and governance gates run outside the live-turn path. Folded ATE in as a first-class subsystem (`run_variations.py`, Gemma/LM Studio, `control.json`/`error_log.jsonl`, `checkin.py`, `compile_issue.py`) rather than a separate diagram, plus a new shared `github-api` external node (justified by both ATE's `gh issue create` and `pdda-gh-refresh.sh`'s `gh issue list`). An initial standalone `ARCHITECTURE/ate-diagram.json`/`.html` was built first, then deleted once the suite-wide version superseded it, per operator direction to avoid two diagrams telling overlapping stories.

Ran `marathon-scan.sh` live to confirm it still works: on this device it resolves only `sleuth-app` (other registries unpopulated here), found 5 active lanes, all `blocked-other`. - **ATE (Automated Testing Environment) skill built and hardened, `utils/ate/`**: renamed from `gemma-aider-variation-tester` (frontmatter `name: ate`, install destination, all doc paths). A local Gemma worker (via LM Studio) fuzzes Aider CLI-flag combinations against an OpenRouter/GLM-5.2 target for hours unattended while a frontier model supervises via `checkin.py`; the full cycle — run Aider, capture the error, document it, file it — is now chained end to end, verified with real subprocess execution (a stub failing `aider` + a stub `gh`), not just read: `run_variations.py` auto-invokes `compile_issue.py` when it stops (time limit/abort/safety cap), which files **one** GitHub issue titled `ATE - [test-name] yyyy-mm-dd` containing every finding from that run as a single severity-ranked checklist — no manual second step. A `/consult` with Codex + agy (agy's transcript flagged `FAIL` by the isolation-grounding guard for citing real-repo paths, but its findings still cross-checked cleanly) surfaced and fixed ~10 unattended-run robustness bugs beyond the three found solo: crash-on-malformed-LM-Studio-response, `compile_issue.py` `IndexError` on empty stderr, `AttributeError` on a JSON-`null` classification, stale `control.json` abort persisting into a fresh run, non-process-tree-safe subprocess timeouts (orphaned `aider` children), `UnicodeDecodeError` on non-UTF8 subprocess output, an `install.sh` self-deletion risk, and a `dict | None` (PEP 604) annotation that crashed every script outright on this machine's Python 3.9 — found only by actually executing the scripts, not `py_compile`. Confirmed via direct code trace + `grep` that the implementation is hardcoded to Aider specifically (binary name, `--edit-format`/`--map-tokens`/`--no-auto-commits` flags, the variation-grid schema, the classifier prompt's pipeline framing) despite the skill's generic description; generalizing it to other harnesses is deliberately deferred, not urgent — captured as [#191](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/191), parked in `ROADMAP.md`'s queue, and given a `ROUTER.md` routing hint.

Added one sentence requiring a quote or `file:line` citation on any `[Pass]`/"verified"/"confirmed" finding, backstopped by a new mechanical check (`rtl_check_uncited_findings()` in `relay-turn-lib.sh`, called from `rtl_enforce()` on Reviewer turns only) that rewrites an uncited claim to `[Unverified — no citation]` in place — catches the absence of a citation, not whether a present one is accurate. **A1** (advisor registry, #178): `consult.sh`'s `case "$m" in codex|agy|gemini|aider)` dispatch is now a data table (`ADV_NAMES`/`ADV_RUNFNS` parallel arrays), so adding a 5th advisor is a data addition, not a new case arm; wrote the "add vendor N+1" recipe into `relay-automation/README.md`. **A4** (verdict provenance, #178 — deliberately scoped down, NOT the full firsthand-vs-asserted taxonomy): reused A2's exact stdout+prepended-transcript+sidecar mechanism to stamp `NO FIRSTHAND VERIFICATION CITED — treat conclusions as conditional` on any answered advisor whose transcript has zero explicit citations anywhere; the fuller provenance distinction stays future-scoped. New tests: `test/relay-uncited-findings.sh` (8 assertions), plus 6 new assertions in `test/consult.sh` (A1: 3, A4: 3). Targeted regression sweep green across `test/consult.sh`, `test/agy-turn.sh`, `test/codex-turn.sh`, `test/claude-turn.sh`, `test/aider-turn.sh`, `test/shim-worktree.sh`, `test/relay-turn-trace.sh`, `test/new-relay.sh`, `test/relay-target-root.sh`, `test/relay-case-insensitive.sh`, `test/test-agy-isolation.sh`.

Closed the discoverability gap: a doc pointer in `aider-turn.sh`'s `AIDER_MODEL` comment, a pointer in `AGENTS.md`, and a new `skills/open-router/SKILL.md` that checks the alias table first, falls back to the live catalog only on a miss, and writes the resolved slug back so the next lookup is instant. ### Fixed
- **PR #184 code-review follow-up (B3/A4 rigor)**: a review of PR #184 flagged that B3's mechanical backstop only recognized a 3-word trigger list (`[Pass]`/verified/confirmed), and that A4's citeless check asked "is there a citation ANYWHERE in the whole transcript" rather than "is THIS claim cited" — letting one incidental citation excuse every later uncited claim. Fixed both: `relay-turn-lib.sh` gained `RTL_CLAIM_WORD_RE` (widened vocabulary — LGTM/looks good/checks out/all good/works as expected/no issues, alongside verified/confirmed) and a new read-only predicate `rtl_has_uncited_claim()`, shared by B3's per-line downgrade and a rewritten `consult.sh` A4 check that now flags a transcript with any uncited claim near itself, not just a transcript with zero citations anywhere. **Caught mid-fix, by the same review pass**: passing `\[Pass\]` through `awk -v` (rather than an inline `/regex/` literal) silently corrupts it under macOS's default `/usr/bin/awk` — the -v value's backslash gets stripped before the string is compiled as a regex, turning the literal tag into the bracket expression `[Pass]` ("any single P, a, or s character"), matching almost every line. Fixed by keeping the `[Pass]` tag check as an inline literal in both awk scripts and only passing the backslash-free prose-word list through `-v`; pinned with a dedicated regression test (a P/a/s-heavy non-claim line asserted byte-for-byte untouched) in both `test/relay-uncited-findings.sh` and via `rtl_has_uncited_claim`. New/extended tests: `test/relay-uncited-findings.sh` (+6 assertions, 16 total), `test/consult.sh` (+2 assertions, updated the A4 non-regression fixture to cite each claim individually rather than once anywhere). Full `./validate.sh` run (item 4 of the follow-up): also rebuilt `skills/relay-automation/relay-pkg.tar.gz` via `make-pkg.sh` — it had already drifted from `new-relay.sh`/`agy-turn.sh`/`README.md`/`relay-turn-lib.sh` since the original PR #184 landed without a rebuild, failing `relay-pkg-freshness.sh`; now green. The only remaining `./validate.sh` reds are two confirmed pre-existing environment gaps unrelated to this change — `acorn-extract.sh` (missing the `acorn` npm module) and `python:test_python_layer.py` (missing `pytest`) — both reproduced identically on the PR's original head commit via `git stash`.

### Docs
- **Marathon Plan C status reconciliation — the plan shipped 2026-07-04; the docs kept advertising it as unfired for four days.** Asked to extract Plan C's `#107` kernel track (Opus-serial) and build it standalone, the first step — verifying the item was actually unbuilt — found the opposite: **all six Plan C lanes plus the kernel track were already built and merged.** `rtl_is_containment_ignored()` is live at `relay-automation/relay-turn-lib.sh:345-364`, its kernel-required [decisions record](decisions/2026-07-04-containment-ignore-toolcache.md) exists, `test/worktree-isolation.sh` is 31/31 green (incl. the control asserting a non-built-in path *without* `CONTAINMENT_IGNORE` still exits 6), and all 7 commits (`742c230`, `691848c`, `53c8dce`, `b4e73df`, `6daaff5`, `524d345`, `d5a1681`) are ancestors of `main`. **Root cause of the drift:** Plan C's own post-merge sweep moved the six capture docs and the plan into `3-COMPLETED` but never performed the "flips ROADMAP/CHANGELOG" half of the step it had written down for itself — so `ROADMAP.md`'s Status cell still read *"Ready to fire: Marathon Plan C's Wave 1 … + its kernel track (#107, Opus-serial)"*, and all eight per-item entries (#106, #107, #108, #116, #117, #124, #126, #127) still carried 🆕 `captured`. **Nothing was rebuilt.** Corrected the prose only: flipped the eight ROADMAP entries to ✅ `SHIPPED` with their commit hashes and test counts, rewrote the Status cell, updated the Plan C doc's frontmatter/status/waves section, and marked GH-107's Definition-of-Done boxes with per-line evidence pointers. Regenerated `ROADMAP-DASHBOARD.md` (✅ 23 → 31; also picked up GH-187, which the previous commit added to `ROADMAP.md` without regenerating the derived view). All eight issues verified **CLOSED** on GitHub. The PDDA gap that let this drift persist silently is filed as **[#189](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/189)** (below).

### Found
- **[GH-189](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/189) — PDDA's issue↔doc sync has a blind spot created by its own recommended remediation.** `check_issue_doc_sync()` (`utils/pdda/pdda.sh:507-542`) scans only `pdda_list_working_docs()`, scoped to `2-WORKING` (`pdda-lib.sh:178`). Its direction (a) fires on *issue CLOSED + doc still in `2-WORKING`* and prints a `git mv … 3-COMPLETED` remediation — and **performing that remediation removes the doc from the check's scan set permanently.** `PDDA_COMPLETED_DIR` (`pdda-lib.sh:10`) is referenced *only* to construct that recommendation string; no check ever reads a doc out of `3-COMPLETED`. Direction (b) is likewise `2-WORKING`-only, and nothing at all validates a `ROADMAP.md` ledger entry's status marker against issue state — `check_roadmap()` (`pdda.sh:227-267`) lints structure only (task-list items, execution-detail headings, line/heading caps). Net: the sweep half of the workflow is enforced, the ROADMAP/CHANGELOG flip half is not, and completing the enforced half silences the only watcher. Demonstrated by the Marathon Plan C drift above — 8 CLOSED issues, a `3-COMPLETED` doc still reading `status: captured`, a ROADMAP cell reading "Ready to fire", and `pdda.sh run` reporting "all checks passed" for four days. It nearly caused a duplicate rebuild of the risk-4 containment kernel (`relay-turn-lib.sh`). Proposed fix (both warn-only, both degrading to the existing offline INFO path): a new `pdda-check-roadmap-issue-state` (CLOSED issue + non-terminal ledger marker → warn, and the inverse), plus extending `check_issue_doc_sync` to flag a `3-COMPLETED` doc whose frontmatter `status` lead word is non-terminal. Capture doc: [GH-189-PDDA-COMPLETED-STATE-BLINDSPOT.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-189-PDDA-COMPLETED-STATE-BLINDSPOT.md). *(PDDA is not a standalone repo — it lives at `utils/pdda/` here, so the issue and ledger entry both land in `xyz-3-agents-swarm`.)*
- **GH-178 B1's isolation-breach detector false-positives on legitimate agy turns**: reviewing PR #182 (which lands B1's agy-isolation-breach fix alongside the `/open-router` work above), reproduced a false "isolation breach" (exit 5) on a fully in-bounds agy turn — the check flags any transcript containing the real `$ROOT` path, but the harness's own prompt mandates a `TICK_REPO_ROOT="$ROOT"` tick command, so an agy response that narrates having run it (ordinary LLM behavior) trips the same detector. Filed as [#183](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/183); shipped as-is per operator call rather than blocking the merge.

The cleanup skill audits active marathon plans and bundles lane-by-lane across canonical task docs, terminal frontmatter/status, GitHub issues and PRs, target-branch commit reachability, verification evidence, and CHANGELOG provenance. ## 2026-07-10

### Fixed
- **[GH-183](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/183) + [GH-187](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/187) — agy isolation-breach detector false positives (PR #194, merged).** GH-178 B1's post-hoc transcript scan (`relay-automation/agy-turn.sh`, `consult.sh`) failed a fully in-bounds agy turn whenever the transcript contained the real `$ROOT` path in a benign context: a mandatory `TICK_REPO_ROOT="$ROOT"` tick-command narration (GH-183) or a markdown `file://` citation of an edited file (GH-187, confirmed live-blocking a real marathon lane on GH-186). Fixed by broadening the pre-scan filter (strip `TICK_REPO_ROOT=`, `file://`, and `](` citation shapes) in **both** shims — `consult.sh` previously had no filtering at all — with 3 new regression cases in `test/agy-turn.sh` (both false-positive shapes + a genuine breach that must still fail). **Built by a real dogfood marathon relay** (Aider/OpenRouter GLM-5.2 builder + agy reviewer, `marathon-drive.sh`), Approved round 2. Two operational gaps found while driving it are recorded in `PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-178-EPISTEMIC-RECONCILIATION-HARDENING.md` (Lessons learned) and `skills/relay-xyz/SKILL.md`: a killed foreground driver can orphan a hand-rolled background process (recover via `git worktree remove` + `tick reap`), and any `relay-automation/*.sh` edit needs `make-pkg.sh` re-run before `validate.sh` is truly green. Also documented the GitHub branch-rename-orphans-open-PR anti-pattern in `AGENTS.md` (hit renaming the branch backing PR #193 → #194).

### Added
- **ATE LM Studio driver target + repo-safety hardening ([#195](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/pull/195), merged).** The ATE variation fuzzer (`utils/ate/scripts/run_variations.py`) can now drive Aider against an OpenAI-compatible endpoint (LM Studio's `deepseek-coder-v2-lite-instruct-mlx`, proven 361/361 clean on the full flag grid) via the GH-147 `AIDER_OPENAI_API_BASE`/`AIDER_OPENAI_API_KEY` contract, without changing the OpenRouter default path. An independent agy+codex consult (via the installed `consult` skill; transcripts in `relay-system/2026-07-10/pr195-review-093811/`) returned "not safe to merge as-is" with two `[Blocker]`s, both fixed before merge: (1) a fresh 0-commit `git init` scratch repo crashed `git rev-parse HEAD` → `ensure_base_commit()` auto-creates a base; (2) the per-variation `git reset --hard` + `git clean -fdx` had no disposable-repo guard → `assert_disposable_repo()` refuses the harness repo (unconditional) and a remote-having repo (unless `--allow-destructive-reset`). Plus a deterministic `edited`/`EDIT_APPLIED` signal so an exit-0/no-edit run is a `fail` not a false pass, stale `.git/index.lock` cleanup, and a new `test/ate-run-variations.sh` (18 checks, in `validate.sh`).

### GH-178 epistemic/reconciliation hardening — B2 + A2 shipped, B1 root-caused, A1 inventoried; GH-173 reopened with a status update
Cut [fix/gh-178-reconciliation-hardening](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/pull/181) from `main` and shipped the two items with enough root-cause evidence to fix directly. **B2**: `warn_if_relay_file_untracked` (`relay-automation/relay-drive.sh`) was generating a false "will find nothing" warning for the common same-repo case — `rtl_worktree_begin`'s seeding step already covers it — because the warning never distinguished same-repo from archive-routed (genuinely cross-repo) relay files; fix compares the relay file's repo toplevel against the turn-taker's effective root and only keeps the strong warning where seeding provably can't reach. **A2**: `relay-automation/consult.sh` now mechanically stamps `SINGLE-MODEL — NOT RECONCILED` into stdout, the surviving transcript, and a sidecar file whenever more than one advisor was requested but exactly one answered, so the degrade caveat survives with the data instead of living only in a live operator's stdout; scoped to not fire on an intentional single-model request. New/extended tests: `test/relay-file-seeding-visibility.sh` (new), `test/relay-untracked-file-warn.sh`, `test/consult.sh` (+8 assertions).

Also did the Phase 2 exploration on the other two items, doc-only. **B1** (agy worktree grounding): a decisive live repro — gitignoring a marker file so it never entered `consult.sh`'s throwaway isolation worktree — proved agy doesn't confine its grounding to the CWD it's given; it self-searches the filesystem and can land on the real repo root regardless. Reframes the original "sometimes zero visibility" report as one symptom of a broader defect (unscoped grounding), not a seeding bug; fix directions written up in [GH-178](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/178) but not yet implemented. **A1** (advisor pluggability): inventoried `consult.sh` and all 4 relay turn shims — the safety core (`relay-turn-lib.sh`) is already vendor-agnostic; the real gap is `consult.sh`'s `--models` dispatch being a fixed `case` instead of data, narrower than the issue's original "hardwired" framing.

### GH-165 Codex shim now owns the relay token before launch
Captured [GH-165](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-165-CODEX-TOKEN-OWNERSHIP.md) on the repo's PDDA rails and fixed the narrowed root cause in both implementations: Bash [relay-automation/codex-turn.sh](relay-automation/codex-turn.sh) and Python [utils/py/codex-turn.py](utils/py/codex-turn.py) no longer rely entirely on prompt-following for the **claim** half of the relay protocol. Before launching Codex they now build the exact `claim --paths` set, best-effort claim the handed-off task, prove `claimer == codex`, and ping the token; if ownership is missing they fail fast with exit 5 before any mutation, rather than allowing a commit with the token still open under the old owner. This complements GH-67's post-commit `release/done` backstop: GH-67 handled "Codex forgot to release", while GH-165 closes "Codex never owned the token in the first place." Added two regressions in [test/codex-turn.sh](test/codex-turn.sh): a **no-tick** Codex stub that edits the relay file but runs zero token commands (the shim must still hand off to the peer), and an **unowned-token refusal** case that proves no edit/commit happens when another agent still owns the task. Verification: `bash test/codex-turn.sh` **37/0** green, `XYZ_PYTHON=1 bash test/codex-turn.sh` **37/0** green, `python3 -m py_compile utils/py/codex-turn.py` green, and `bash test/relay-pkg-freshness.sh` green after rebuilding `skills/relay-automation/relay-pkg.tar.gz`. A full `./validate.sh` run reached **101/104** with the remaining red gates outside this change surface: `worktree-isolation.sh` (moved-ROOT-HEAD preserve case) and `python:test_python_layer.py` (environment lacks `pytest`).

Codex found a real `[Blocker]`: `utils/py/swarm_preflight.py`'s inferred test/helper-path widening only checked `.startswith("test/")`, which a `test/../...` string satisfies despite resolving outside the test tree — reopening exactly the vulnerability class GH-137 closed in the Bash original (`sanitizeInferredPath`, which also rejects generated `__pycache__`/`.pyc` paths). Independently re-verified the finding by direct code inspection before acting on it (confirmed the bypass string really does pass the old check). No live exposure — `XYZ_PYTHON` still defaults off — but a real, silent gap. Fixed: ported `sanitize_inferred_path()`/`is_generated_inferred_path()` 1:1 from Bash, gated both widening call sites through it; added regression tests mirroring the Bash `T35b` fixture plus direct unit coverage of the named bypass string (`pytest test/test_python_layer.py` 15/15). The two `[Pass]` findings (binary-probe ordering; GH-107's exemption logic genuinely inherited via `rtl.py`, not reimplemented) and the `[Should]` (a receipt proving `relay-turn-lib.sh` itself was untouched by `cd4c215`) were also independently confirmed rather than taken on trust. Codex's own Round-2 confirmatory pass re-ran the tests itself and returned **Approved**. ### Loose-ends sweep: GH-140/GH-141 paper trail backfilled
A `/relay-xyz` review of the GH-96/GH-140 work above actually completed successfully (Approved, 5/5 `[Pass]`) but surfaced a second containment hazard along the way: **[#141](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/141)** — `rtl_enforce`'s pre-turn dirty-snapshot protection (`rtl_before`/`rtl_was_dirty_before`) only exempts WIP that existed *before* a turn starts; it can't see a second, independent session's edit landing *during* the turn's execution window, so `rtl_check()` reverts it directly in `$RTL_ROOT` as if it were an off-lane agent edit. Live-hit: a review-only agy turn reverted a concurrent Claude Code session's in-progress edit to `PROJECT/1-INBOX/PEER-RESEARCH.md`; recovered by hand (the content was still visible in recent scrollback), not recoverable in general. Filed, not fixed — genuinely a multi-session concurrency question, not a quick patch; captured as [GH-141-CONCURRENT-PEER-EDIT-RACE.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-141-CONCURRENT-PEER-EDIT-RACE.md) with three candidate fix directions, none chosen. A subsequent `/loose-ends` sweep also found GH-140 itself had no dedicated capture doc (only inline ROADMAP/CHANGELOG prose) despite being a real kernel fix — backfilled as [GH-140-CONTAINMENT-ATOMIC-COPYBACK.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-140-CONTAINMENT-ATOMIC-COPYBACK.md), and posted the missing "Seam #1 shipped" comment on issue #96 (Seam #2 got one; Seam #1 hadn't).

### GH-96 Seam #1 shipped + GH-140 containment-kernel fix (dogfood found a real repo-wiping bug)
Shipped Seam #1 of the XYZ⇄Rebalance integration (`XYZ.json` schema/cadence doc + a `XYZ.heartbeat.json` companion so a hung/dead run goes stale instead of presenting the last completed record as current) — but getting there surfaced a serious containment-kernel bug. The first marathon attempt (codex build → agy Approved, 2 turns, clean relay lifecycle) crashed `marathon-drive.sh` itself immediately after approval with a garbled `syntax error`, then wiped this repo's local working tree and `.git` internals down to a handful of surviving dot-directories (`origin/main` was completely unaffected — confirmed via `git ls-remote`, recovered by re-cloning). Root cause, found by reading `rtl_worktree_end()`: it copies a turn's allowlisted artifacts back from the isolated worktree via a **non-atomic in-place `cp -R`**, which truncates-and-rewrites an existing destination at the same inode; since this lane's own artifacts included the *actively-executing* `marathon-drive.sh` and its `relay-drive.sh` subprocess, the live bash interpreter — which reads a script incrementally, not fully buffered upfront — read a half-old/half-new file mid-parse. Filed and fixed as its own kernel patch, **[#140](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/140)** (`f64a051`): copy to a temp path beside the destination, then atomically rename into place — the same `os.replace()` pattern `append-xyz-completion.sh` already uses, so an fd already open on the old file keeps reading the old inode until it closes, never observing a half-written script. Regression: `test/worktree-isolation.sh` 31/31, `test/shim-worktree.sh` 32/32, `test/agy-turn.sh` 27/27, full `validate.sh` green. Re-fired the identical GH-96 lane on a temp branch (`marathon/gh-96-xyz-json-emit-contract-heartbeat-2026-07-06`) with close, active monitoring this time — completed cleanly end-to-end (gate `test/xyz-completion.sh` 44/0, agy Approved after 1 round), confirming the fix; verified independently (gate re-run 44/0, full `validate.sh` green — the one flake was `test/relay-self-sufficiency.sh`, a live-agy-API test that reproduced clean 4/4 in isolation, pre-existing and unrelated), fast-forward merged to `main` (`f1e5223`), pushed. Marked [GH-96-XYZ-JSON-EMIT-CONTRACT-HEARTBEAT.md](PROJECT/3-COMPLETED/GH-96-XYZ-JSON-EMIT-CONTRACT-HEARTBEAT.md) shipped and moved to `3-COMPLETED`; issue #96 stays open for Seam #3 (tick-lane consume), still explicitly gated behind Seam #1 proving out per its own text.

### GH-137 swarm-preflight containment fix (marathon lane) + ledger drift reconciliation

Built by a `marathon-drive` lane (codex builder → agy **Approved**, gate 87/0): a new `sanitizeInferredPath` in [utils/swarm-preflight.sh](utils/swarm-preflight.sh) drops any inferred path with a `..` segment, resolving outside the repo `test/` subtree, or generated, applied to both inferred tests and the helper walk; declared `artifacts[]` pass through untouched (narrowing only). `test/swarm-preflight.sh` regression (T35b); `GH-137` marker; `validate.sh` **102/102**. **Ledger reconciliation:** closed the three genuinely-landed-but-open issues — **#137** (this fix), **#112** (Python port scope complete; default-flip deferred by the decision record), **#30** (transcript archive, already shipped) — moved the two shipped capture docs to `3-COMPLETED`, marked their ROADMAP entries ✅, and regenerated the dashboard. Ran the newly-landed opt-in Python layer against itself: an `XYZ_PYTHON=1` headless **Codex** relay reviewing the port's own FFI wrapper `utils/py/rtl.py`, driving the full chain through `relay_drive.py` + `codex-turn.py` + `rtl.py` (worktree isolation ON, containment held, file-scoped commits, no push). Round 1 returned **Changes requested** with three real findings; the Producer turn fixed all three and Round 2 returned **Approved** (independent grader ≠ producer — GUIDING-PRINCIPLES §12). Fixes in [utils/py/rtl.py](utils/py/rtl.py): **[Blocker]** `_run_rtl()` no longer `sys.exit()`s on a non-zero shell status — it returns the `CompletedProcess`, so the failure branches in `before()`/`enforce()`/`worktree_begin()` are reachable (`worktree_begin()→None` drives `codex-turn`'s exit 5; `enforce()` non-zero is containment exit 6); must-succeed derivation calls keep fail-fast via a new `_run_checked()`. **[Should]** shell-injection closed — every interpolated path is `shlex.quote()`d and `TICK_REPO_ROOT` is passed via the child `env=` rather than embedded in the sourced script (adversarial check: a `xyz_root` with a `"; touch …; echo "` payload no longer executes). **[Nit]** `__del__` guarded with `hasattr(state_file)` against a partial-init `AttributeError` (full context-manager conversion declined — would touch every caller for a Nit). `test/test_python_layer.py` swapped the test that had locked in the buggy exit for two asserting the corrected contract; **pytest 9/9**, `validate.sh` **100/100** (default Bash mode). ### HQ fuzzy resolution (1.x) + Phase 3 dispatch (queue/fire) — on a worktree branch → PR (#128)
Built on an isolated worktree (`worktree-hq-gh128-followups`) to avoid congesting `main`.

- **Fuzzy resolution (Phase 1.x)** — `hq_norm` (lowercase + alnum-only) + `hq_candidates` add a fallback when exact resolution misses: "rebalanceOS" now resolves to `rebalance-OS`; a genuinely ambiguous name (e.g. "acme" → acme-app + acme-api) returns rc=2 with `CANDIDATES` rather than guessing. `hq_resolve` now emits `RESOLVED_VIA` (exact|fuzzy); status/park surface the fuzzy note + ambiguous candidates.
- **Phase 3 dispatch** — `queue [--create] [--gh-issue N] <project> <request>` appends a non-destructive `HQ-queued` lane appendix to the target's newest Marathon Plan (previews by default; a human rates + slots it into a wave, GH-45). `fire --gh-issue N [--risk 1-5] <project>` is a **gated prepare-and-hand-off**: resolve + gate (Tier A, `risk<3`) + emit the `swarm-preflight` command — it **never drives the harness itself** (operator decides — GUIDING-PRINCIPLES §8; the relay-xyz guard owns driving). Requires `--gh-issue` (dispatch operates on captured intake).
- **Verification** — `test/hq.sh` 18→24 (fuzzy + ambiguity), new `test/hq-dispatch.sh` 18/18, `test/hq-park.sh` 23/23; all in `validate.sh`. shellcheck clean.

Design stance: HQ prepares and gates; the heaviest action (running a marathon) stays a deliberate operator step via relay-xyz — the same preview-first restraint `park` applies to creating an outward GH issue.

### HQ Phase 2 built — `park` issue-first intake writer, the first runnable end-to-end use case (#128)
Shipped the first *write* path: `hq park [--create] <project> <request>`. It resolves the project (Phase 1 ladder), then either **previews** (default — writes nothing) or, with `--create`, lands the request on the target repo's PDDA rails: `gh issue create` → `PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-<n>-<SLUG>.md` capture doc (PDDA frontmatter) → a `ROADMAP.md` queue pointer inserted under the target's `### Queue` heading → the target's own `pdda.sh frontmatter` check.

- **Preview-first design decision** — creating a GitHub issue is outward-facing and not fully reversible, so `park` previews by default and requires `--create` to write. Reversibility: Easy in preview (no-op), Costly on `--create` (an outward issue). This keeps the prototype fully demonstrable without firing an irreversible action, and makes the exact artifacts reviewable before they exist.
- **Guards** — dup-guard (`gh issue list --search` → abort rather than double-file), Tier C repos get a plain issue only (never a partial doc structure) + a PDDA-install suggestion, files are written but **not committed** (operator reviews then commits). New helpers in `hq-lib.sh`: `hq_slug` (stopword-aware SCREAMING-KEBAB), `hq_render_capture`, `hq_roadmap_insert`, `hq_target_slug`. `HQ_GH_BIN` seam stubs `gh` for tests.
- **Verification** — hermetic `test/hq-park.sh` (19/19, registered in `validate.sh`): preview-writes-nothing, `--create` writing correct frontmatter + a Queue-placed pointer via a stubbed `gh`, stopword slugging, dup-guard abort, Tier C, and unresolved. `test/hq.sh` (Phase 1) still 18/18; shellcheck clean; `pdda.sh run` green. Preview proven live against `sleuth-app` (Tier A).

A **real** `park --create` against a live repo is held for a concrete operator request — the harness is proven end-to-end (stubbed), only the outward `gh issue create` is gated. Phase 3 (dispatch: `queue`/`fire`) is next.

### HQ Phase 0 + Phase 1 built — read-only registry resolver + project card (#128)
Moved HQ from capture to execution: promoted [GH-128-HQ-COMMAND-CENTER.md](PROJECT/2-WORKING/GH-128-HQ-COMMAND-CENTER.md) into `2-WORKING` as a full phased plan and shipped the read-only prototype of the first use case.

- **Phase 0 (discovery)** — enumerated the three live registries and, crucially, which carries a local path: Rebalance `project_registry` (`rebalance.db`, 15 projects) is the semantic name→repo catalog **but has no path**; the XYZ install registry (`~/.config/xyz/registry.tsv`) is the **only** reliable absolute-path source (`coordinated_repo`); the Git Pulse PDDA registry (`registry-<device>.tsv`) is the governance overlay (mode + startup_docs), device-partitioned but read across all devices. Gap → a filesystem `find` fallback is mandatory. Findings written back into the plan doc (PDDA discovery contract).
- **Phase 1 (read-only resolver)** — `skills/hq/SKILL.md` + `utils/hq/hq.sh` (`resolve`/`status`/`registries`) + `utils/hq/hq-lib.sh` (the 4-rung ladder, each rung offline-tolerant, `HQ_*` env seams, SQL-sanitized, avoids the reserved `PATH` name). `park`/`queue`/`fire` are gated not-yet-built notices (rc=3) so the write surface is discoverable without pretending to act.
- **Verification** — hermetic `test/hq.sh` (18/18; builds fixture registries + a repo tree under a temp dir and points the env seams at them, so it never depends on live machine state), registered in `validate.sh`. First use case proven live: `hq status sleuth-app` returns a full **Tier A** card resolving through all three registries; `rebalance-OS` confirms cross-device PDDA resolution; unknown names return rc=1 with an actionable find recipe.

No writes anywhere yet (Rebalance read-only, as designed). Phase 2 (the issue-first intake writer — the first write path) is gated on operator go-ahead.

### deep-research.mjs (GH-87) had shipped un-run against real `agy` — 2 hangs fixed + grounded #111 research
Dogfooding the merged grounded-search adapter to research #111 (Python cutover lessons) revealed it **hung on every real `agy` call** — it had been merged with stub-only tests, so it was never actually run against the real backend. Two independent bugs, both fixed + regression-tested:
- **Missing `--dangerously-skip-permissions`** (`91f17f2`): `agy -p` blocked on a tool-permission prompt for its web-search tool until `--print-timeout`.
- **`execFile` silently ignores the `stdio` option** (`74cd553`): `stdio:['ignore',…]` did nothing, so `agy`'s stdin was left an open pipe it blocked reading — every wrapper call hung. Switched `runAgy` to `spawn` (which honors `stdio`). Measured: `execFile` 75s→timeout/0-bytes vs `spawn` 10s→ok; the stub missed it because it never reads stdin.
Now returns in ~27–52s with real citations; `test/deep-research.sh` **23/23** (added a `--dangerously-skip-permissions` assertion + a stdin-reading `needstdin` stub that hangs unless stdin is EOF'd). **Lesson: a stub can't prove a CLI adapter works against its real backend** — filed **#124** for hardening (opt-in real-`agy` smoke test + a runaway-grounding guard: `--search-context-size high` over-searches and can approach the timeout). First real use: ran 4 grounded searches (reframed for the XYZ→Python port, drawing applicable lessons from #111) and posted them to **#111**.

`relay-automation/deep-research.mjs` is a zero-dep Node adapter wrapping the `agy` CLI as the first grounded-search backend (Agy Gemini Search), normalizing output to `{answer, citations, query, provider, model, raw}` so a second backend (Perplexity) can be added later without reworking the contract. ## 2026-07-03

### GH-30 Phases 3–5 SHIPPED (Plan A lane 5, complete) — Model A off-tree transcript commit
Phase 2 redirected the transcript *writers*; the containment kernel still committed the relay file into the target repo. Phase 3 closes that: when `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` places the relay file in a **separate archive git repo**, the kernel commits the **transcript into the archive** while the **code artifact + the `.tick` token stay anchored to the target**. Built and reviewed operator-direct under Opus 4.8 (kernel lane — not headless).
- **The bet (Costly, reversible — containment-kernel touch):** `rtl_enforce` now performs a second, **isolated** `git -C "$RTL_RELAY_REPO"` pathspec commit for the transcript. Because that commit can never move the target repo's HEAD, it **cannot orphan a concurrent peer commit** in the target tree — the documented `rtl_enforce` reset hazard (GH-13) is guarded on `RTL_ROOT` only, and now holds even when token-tree ≠ transcript-tree. The archive commit is best-effort: a failure **warns** (the transcript file is still written) and never fails the turn, since the file-scoped code commit already stood. Reversibility: unset the var → byte-for-byte the prior single-repo behavior; the archive is a plain committed git repo the operator owns. Revisit trigger: if a turn ever needs the transcript and token in the *same* off-tree repo (not this split), or if worktree isolation must seed the archive relay file (today the agent edits it at its real absolute path, outside the target worktree).
- **`rtl_init` archive detection (one place):** when the relay file's git toplevel differs from `RTL_ROOT`'s, it flags `RTL_ARCHIVE_MODE` + records `RTL_RELAY_REPO`/`RTL_RELAY_ARCHIVE_REL`. An out-of-root relay file survives the repo-root-relative strip as an **absolute** path, so it is already inert to the `RTL_ROOT` status/commit loop; the worktree seed/copyback now **skip absolute allowlist entries** in lockstep (seed + seedsig + copyback) so the seedsig index stays aligned. Same-repo relay file / unset var → `RTL_ARCHIVE_MODE=0`, byte-for-byte every existing path.
- **`.tick` stays on the target:** the token handoff (GH-67) already uses `TICK_REPO_ROOT`; archive mode never touches it. So the coordination token lives with the target repo and the transcript lives in the archive — the two trees differ safely.
- **Phase 4 (telemetry):** `extract-relay-telemetry.sh` reads the resolver — with `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` set it scans the archive across **all** `<repo-slug>/` dirs (one extra level), so a single feed aggregates many source repos without colliding on `<date>/<slug>`. Unset → the local one-level scan, unchanged.
- **`new-relay.sh` wired (Phase 3 usability):** the scaffolder defaults its thread location through the resolver when `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` is set (so a scaffolded thread lands in the archive), preserving the exact relative default when unset.
- **Tests:** new `test/archive-commit.sh` (16 — archive-mode isolated turn: transcript→archive, code→target, target tree free of `relay-system/`, no transcript commit in target history; default-unchanged in-tree turn; **peer-commit preserved when token-tree ≠ transcript-tree**) + `test/archive-telemetry.sh` (3 — cross-repo aggregation with no `<date>/<slug>` collision; unset empty-range → valid `[]`), both wired into `validate.sh`. Docs: `CONSUMING.md` + `relay-automation/README.md` gain the full Phase-3 contract (transcript→archive, `.tick`→target, no orphaned peer commit, configure archive git identity). GH-30 promoted `2-WORKING → 3-COMPLETED`.

### GH-30 Phase 2 SHIPPED — the four transcript writers honor the `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` resolver
Phase 1 built the resolver but wired it to nothing. Phase 2 makes each writer call it, so setting `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` actually redirects transcripts out of a foreign product repo. Done in an isolated git worktree while another agent worked `main`.
- **Four writers wired** — `consult.sh` (`OUT` default), `marathon-drive.sh` (`save_transcript`'s `date_dir`), `relay-drive.sh` (consult-verify `_cv_out_dir`), `swarm-preflight.sh` (`OUT_DIR`, resolved once *before* the dry-run gate so the `Would emit to:` preview matches the real emit). Each sources `relay-turn-lib.sh` by the script's own dir (not `$ROOT`, which may be a foreign `CONSULT_ROOT`).
- **Override wins, fail-loud** — the resolver is invoked **only when no explicit override is set**, so `--out`/`OUT_DIR` fully wins even against an invalid `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT`; each call is `|| exit/return 1` so an invalid archive aborts the writer loudly under both `set -e` and `set -uo` (never a silent fallback into the target repo). `marathon-drive` declares-then-assigns so `local` doesn't mask the resolver's exit code.
- **Scope boundary (honest):** Phase 2 redirects the transcript *writers*. A cross-repo relay *turn* is not fully redirected until **Phase 3** — the turn's own relay-file writes are still governed by the target-repo write-allowlist in `relay-turn-lib.sh` (containment + commit-into-archive is the risk-4 core). `consult.sh` / `swarm-preflight.sh` (no containment) are fully redirected today.
- **Cross-model review (Codex + agy) → 1 Blocker fixed, with an adjudicated split:** `relay-drive.sh`'s consult-verify wiring combined two command substitutions in one assignment — `_cv_out_dir="$(rtl_transcript_root "$ROOT_DIR")/$(date +%F)" || exit 1` — so a resolver failure was **masked by the succeeding `$(date)`** (the assignment's rc is the last substitution's), `|| exit 1` never fired, and an invalid archive silently used a bogus `/<date>` path. Codex graded it **[Blocker]**; agy graded the same line **[Pass]** (it only checked for a `local` prefix, not the double substitution). Adjudicated empirically — `x="$(false)/$(true)"` does NOT trip `||` under `set -e` — Codex was right. Fixed by splitting into the same two-statement form the other three writers use. Added a structural lint that catches this masked-substitution anti-pattern, a swarm behavioral fail-loud test (`T8e`, invalid archive → non-zero, no packet), and broadened the hardcode grep (agy nit).
- `CONSUMING.md` gains an `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` recipe (derive `RELAY_FILE` from the base, not a hardcoded `repoB/relay-system/…`). New `test/archive-writers.sh` (8): consult **end-to-end** through stub advisors (unset → `$ROOT/relay-system`; set → `$archive/relay-system/<slug>`; `--out` wins; invalid-archive → hard fail no leak) + a structural regression lock (source + resolver-called + no hardcode + rc-not-masked) over all four writers; `test/swarm-preflight.sh` +4 dry-run/fail-loud archive assertions. `validate.sh` **90/90**, `hardcoded-paths` clean. Marathon-drive/relay-drive are covered structurally + by the Phase-1 resolver unit test (a full relay loop needs codex/agy + network).

### GH-30 Phase 1 SHIPPED (Plan A lane 5) — single transcript-root resolver (`XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT`, default-off)
The relay/consult/marathon/swarm tooling writes its operational transcript *inside* the target repo (`$ROOT/relay-system/<date>/…`), so running against a foreign product repo B pollutes B with a `relay-system/` tree and lands generic relay commits in B's history. GH-30 adds one optional env var to redirect that; this is **Phase 1 — the safe standalone slice: the resolver only, no writer wiring (Phase 2) and no containment/commit changes (the risk-4 Phase 3).**
- **`rtl_transcript_root <target_root>`** (+ companion `rtl_repo_slug`) in `relay-turn-lib.sh` is now the ONE place the relay-system base is decided. **Unset `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` → `$target_root/relay-system` (byte-for-byte today's path).** Set → `$XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT/relay-system/<repo-slug>`, namespaced per source repo so a central archive holds many repos collision-free. It emits the relay-system *base*; callers append their existing `/<date>/…` tail.
- **Model A (decided 2026-07-02 — separate committed git archive repo) validation:** when set, `XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT` MUST be absolute, exist, **and be a git repo** — any failure is a HARD ERROR (stderr + `return 1`), **never** a silent fallback into the foreign tree (a non-git archive would drop transcripts on the floor at Phase-3 commit time, so it's rejected now at the resolver). Slug = origin remote basename, fallback target dir basename, sanitized to one `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` segment.
- **The bet (Costly, reversible):** GH-30 as a whole touches relay containment + commit semantics (`AGENTS.md` flags containment changes as "broader than they look"), hence risk 4 — but *this phase* changes no runtime behavior (no writer calls the resolver yet; default-unset is byte-for-byte current). Fully reversible: unsetting the var restores today's paths exactly. Revisit trigger: Phase 2 (writer wiring) then Phase 3 (off-tree allowlist + isolated commit-into-archive that never orphans a peer commit).
- **Cross-model review (Codex + agy, both independently) → one Blocker + two Shoulds fixed before merge:** `rtl_repo_slug`'s `tr` sanitizer preserved `.`/`-`, so a target basename of `..` produced slug `..` → `$XYZ_ARCHIVE_ROOT/relay-system/..` **escaped the namespace** (path traversal), and a leading `-` slug was option-shaped for a later `cd`/`git -C`. Also a trailing-slash origin URL (`…/foo.git/`) yielded an empty basename → wrong dir-basename fallback, and a trailing-slash `target_root` → `//relay-system`. Hardened: strip trailing slashes off the remote URL before/after `.git`; strip leading dashes; collapse `.`/`..`/empty to `repo`; normalize a trailing slash off `target_root` (byte-identical on the normalized roots callers actually pass).
- `test/archive-root.sh` (**13 checks**: unset → today's path; set → namespaced; trailing-slash target; relative/missing/non-git → hard error + stderr; slug fallback; scp-style remote; trailing-slash remote; `..`/`.` traversal → `repo`; leading-dash → stripped; space → `_`) wired into `validate.sh`. **89/89 green** — this run also fixed a pre-existing `test/xyz-vendor.sh` typo (`"$(cat …)"= "app-util"` missing the space before `=`) that was reddening the suite after the GH-104 `utils/`-vendor change; verified the vendor itself leaves the target's `utils/` untouched. `pdda hardcoded-paths` clean.

### GH-5 SHIPPED (Plan A lane 4, PR #103) — contract-seam warning for coupled lanes
The one thing that made two lanes *actually* independent in the real run was a coordinator-pinned shared contract (both coded TO the contract, not to each other's source) — but nothing detected coupling, so operators found it only when an agent stalled waiting on another lane. `marathon-plan.sh`'s wave-packer defers only on an **exact** write-set collision, so two lanes writing disjoint files under a common directory (e.g. `src/schema/producer.js` ‖ `src/schema/consumer.js`) co-wave and *look* independent while the consumer stalls on the producer.
- **`sharedSpine`** detects the seam: the deepest directory of ≥2 segments shared by two same-wave lanes' write-sets (top-level-only sharing — both under `src/` — is intentionally not a seam). Every coupled pair becomes a `warn`/`coupled-lanes` finding.
- New **"## Contract seams — pin a contract before launching (GH-5)"** plan section names each pair + the shared dir + the fix (pin a short `CONTRACT.md`, point each lane prompt at it) — making the "pin the contract" step first-class (fix 1) alongside the coupled-lane warning (fix 2). Deferred-coupled-work (fix 3) is the existing Held/flagged section. **Advisory** — never re-waves the lanes (they parallelize fine once a contract is pinned); exit code unchanged.
- Only lanes with a proven write-set (from a contract) are judged. Pairs with GH-4 work-stealing: a stolen task that's actually coupled would stall — this surfaces the seam first. `test/marathon-plan.sh` scenario K (+3: `src/schema` pair flagged; a `utils/` lane not flagged; pin-a-contract stated); `marathon-plan` 39/39. Deferred: auto-scaffolding the CONTRACT.md + source-level import-graph inference.

### GH-4 SHIPPED (Plan A lane 3, PR #101) — lane-count-independent verdict + work-stealing unblocked
The run success bar was `concurrency% AND each agent ≥2 done` — but a clean 2-lane/2-agent split gives 1 done each *by construction*, so a flawless run failed the bar, and the manual `handoff-exclusive` rule *blocked* a fast agent from crossing lanes precisely to protect that (broken) per-agent bar — leaving idle tail-time on the table. Fixed all three ways (events-only):
- **(1) `tick analyze` emits a VERDICT** (`pass`/`fail`/`incomplete`) gating on concurrency% + zero-parked + **all-lanes-done** + **zero-collisions**, and explicitly **not** on per-agent done count (`computeVerdict`; target `TICK_CONCURRENCY_TARGET_PCT`, finite-tunable, default 50). A `circuit_broken` or still-open lane counts as not-done → FAIL.
- **(2) Work-stealing** needed no new mechanism: `src/take.js` already selects the highest-priority `open` task whose paths don't overlap *any* active claim, so a free agent that `take`s after finishing pulls a collision-free item from another lane. Removing the per-agent bar makes the resulting imbalance a *win* — the verdict now PASSES a 2-vs-1 done split.
- **(3) Lane balance** — a per-agent idle-tail line surfaces imbalance; `computeCollisions` reports overlapping concurrent claims (should be 0 — verifies the claim-lock containment invariant held).
- `test/workstealing-verdict.sh` (6, end-to-end: beta steals C1 from a free lane → board drains all-done → **VERDICT PASS on a 2-vs-1 split**, the exact case the old bar failed) + `test/analyze.sh` (+2: 0 collisions, FAIL on an undone/broken lane). Additive JSON fields → `watchdog.sh`/`poll.sh` unaffected. `validate.sh` **87/87**. Deferred: runner auto-`take`-loop + wiring the verdict into a marathon exit gate.

### GH-3 SHIPPED (Plan A lane 2, PR #100) — parked-claim detector no longer false-flags active autonomous agents
`tick analyze`'s parked-claim detector (`src/analyze.js`) disqualified a fully-active 90%-concurrency run: its liveness signal was **`task.heartbeat`-only** and the fixed 10-min threshold is human-paced, but an autonomous subagent in a single long atomic tool call has **no yield point** to emit a `tick ping`, so it looked parked. Fixed events-only (keeps the detector's "no git" invariant):
- **(2) Liveness = any `task.*` event from the agent** for that task inside the window — `scope_changed`, `commented`, a re-claim, `done` — not just `task.heartbeat`. The truest events-only proxy for "work happening."
- **(1) `TICK_PARKED_THRESHOLD_MS`** env override (default unchanged, 10 min; **finite** positive only — a stray `Infinity` can't disable the gate, per the PR #100 cross-model review) so an autonomous-agent marathon can raise it without a code change.
- `parked_suspects` shape/semantics **unchanged** (still "an activity gap over threshold") → safe for the live `watchdog.sh` reap + `poll.sh` count; a new `activity` count is reported alongside `heartbeats`. `test/heartbeat.sh` +4 checks (a `scope_changed` keeps a window live; the env override raises, suppresses, and rejects non-finite); `chaos-midturn-kill` (orphaned claim still parked) + `watchdog-liveness` green. `validate.sh` 85/85. Reviewed cross-model (Codex + agy → merge).

### GH-41 SHIPPED (Plan A lane 1, PR #99) — terminality-seal: a `done` token can't be silently resurrected
Kernel projection fix. In `src/project.js` `foldWithMeta`, a `task.done` was authorized against the **global** highest-epoch `winner`, so a *later* higher-epoch `task.claimed` on the same token made that reclaim the winner and retroactively de-authorized the legitimate `done` — and `isStaleWrite` skipped it too (the done predates the reclaim). Net: **`done → claimed`, 0 rejections, no audit trace.** A terminal state wasn't terminal (GH-40 canary #1).
- **Fix (Option A, [decisions/2026-07-02-terminality-seal.md](decisions/2026-07-02-terminality-seal.md), `/consult` Codex+agy unanimous):** authorize a terminal against `ownerAsOf(terminal.ts)` — the highest-epoch live claim at/before the terminal — not the global winner. Once an authorized terminal exists, **seal** the token: every claim with `ts > terminal.ts` (higher *or* lower epoch) is rejected into `rejections[]` with the new distinct reason **`claim-after-terminal`** (a lifecycle violation, never folded into `stale-epoch`/`non-owner-agent`), never applied as a `done→claimed` flip. Fold stays reorder-deterministic. **No new verb** (Option B deferred).
- **Proof:** canary **inverted** — `test/fixtures/canary-token-reuse/verify-fixture.sh` now asserts `done 1 claim-after-terminal` (was `claimed 0`); control stays `done 0`. All projection/tick/chaos suites green — no epoch-fence regression.
- **⚠️ Parked (pre-existing reds on `main`, not this lane):** `test/xyz-vendor.sh` (`expected 20, got 148` — the full-mirror vendor change ships 148 files but its test still asserts 20) and `path-integrity.sh` (Wave 1 GH-63's `test/signal-triage.sh` uses `test/foo.sh`/`test/some-test.sh` as example classifier inputs, flagged as real path refs).

### Vendor installer now ships a COMPLETE, drift-free XYZ install — aider + consult reach vendored repos
`relay-automation/xyz-vendor.sh` materialized `.xyz/` from a hand-curated manifest derived from `skills/relay-automation/make-pkg.sh`. That manifest silently omitted anything added after it was last touched — the GH-77 **aider ↔ OpenRouter** gateway shipped but was never vendored, and `consult.sh` was absent too, so vendored repos (e.g. RebalanceOS) couldn't run either. Replaced the manifest with a **full directory mirror** (`relay-automation/ bin/ src/ test/ skills/`): every turn shim (codex/agy/**aider**/gemini/claude), `consult.sh`, the marathon runtime, the self-improve loop, `hooks/`, and the skill definitions now travel as one standalone install. No more manifest drift — new scripts are vendored automatically. (`.xyz/` is gitignored, so the larger mirror adds zero git weight to the target repo.)
- **Consult made vendor-aware.** The `consult` skill hardcoded `<repo-toplevel>/relay-automation/consult.sh` and declared itself "not portable." It now resolves the top-level copy **or** the vendored `.xyz/relay-automation/consult.sh`, and — the linchpin — exports `CONSULT_ROOT="$repo_toplevel"` so the vendored script targets the **host** repo (without it, `consult.sh` infers its root as `.xyz/`, the wrong tree). SKILL.md "not portable" language softened accordingly.
- **RebalanceOS re-vendored** to HEAD (`0ab1f32`): 29/29 relay-automation scripts, aider dispatch live in `marathon-agent.sh`, `consult.sh` + `skills/consult/` present, all gateways `bash -n` clean, and the skill's resolver confirmed to land on the vendored copy from inside the (git) repo.
- **Known follow-up:** the *other* install path — `skills/relay-xyz/install.sh` unpacking `relay-pkg.tar.gz` — still uses the stale `make-pkg.sh` manifest and would not ship aider/consult. Out of scope here (the ask was the vendor installer); flagged for a later manifest refresh + tarball rebuild.

## 2026-07-02

### GH-77 live E2E: real Aider↔OpenRouter turn — surfaced + fixed an aux-file containment bug
Ran the first **real** Aider turn through `relay-automation/aider-turn.sh` (installed Aider from the local repo into a throwaway venv; `gpt-4o-mini` via OpenRouter). It failed **exit 6** on the very first turn — a genuine defect the 31 stub tests couldn't catch: Aider writes its own `.aider.chat.history.md` + `.aider.input.history` into CWD, which (with `--no-gitignore`) land as untracked off-allowlist files and trip `rtl_enforce`. **Every real turn would have failed.**
- **Fix** — the shim redirects Aider's chat/input/llm history to a throwaway dir outside the repo via `--chat-history-file`/`--input-history-file`/`--llm-history-file` (`AIDER_AUX_DIR`, default `$TMPDIR/aider-aux-$$`). Re-run confirmed **exit 0**, a file-scoped commit (relay file only), **no `.aider.*` leak**, token handed to the peer.
- **Regression** — the `test/aider-turn.sh` stub now mimics Aider's aux-file write (honors `--chat-history-file`, defaults to CWD), so dropping the redirect flips the good turn to exit 6. New no-leak assertion; **32/32** (verified by a strip-and-restore teeth-check). Doc moved earlier to `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED`; the "live E2E" follow-on is now done.

### GH-77 aider ↔ OpenRouter lane SHIPPED (PR #79) — token-ownership [Blocker] fixed on merge
Took the codex-reviewed PR #79 to merge directly: rebased onto main (churn conflicts + a one-line `validate.sh` test-list merge resolved; `marathon-drive.sh` auto-merged with GH-45's attempt-cap and GH-56's `reconcile_relay_task` both intact), fixed the review's `[Blocker]`, and cleared the two `[Should]`/`[Nit]` items.
- **[Blocker] fixed** — `relay-automation/aider-turn.sh` now **proves token ownership before launching Aider**: after the best-effort `claim`, it asserts `claimer == self` via `tick info` and fails the turn (exit 5, before any mutation) if the claim didn't stick. Previously a missed claim still committed + exited 0, leaving the token open under the old owner and deadlocking the lane. New regression test (unowned token → exit 5, no commit, claimer unchanged).
- **Test hygiene** — the new assertion exposed a latent ordering fragility: tick caps concurrent claims per agent (`MAX_ACTIVE_CLAIMS_PER_AGENT`), and the exit-6/5 failure cases left aider holding un-released tokens, so a later claim silently missed. `test/aider-turn.sh` now releases aider's slot after each failure case (as the real driver reaps a failed lane). **31/31**.
- **[Should]/[Nit]** — moved the shipped `GH-77` doc from `PROJECT/1-INBOX` → `PROJECT/3-COMPLETED` and repointed the ROUTER link; normalized the test-count drift (26/28) to the one observed number, **31**.

### GH-83 — README onboarding-UX rewrite (single operator path; de-brittled a stale test count)
A `/consult` on `README.md` (Codex `gpt-5.4` + agy, run cold as a brand-new operator) flagged the top of the file as **[Blocker]** on two counts both models reached independently, plus a freshness bug. Rewrote the landing page to fix them; docs-only, trim + reorder (no runtime/kernel change). Issue-first: [#83](https://github.com/Claude-AI-Tools-Ventura-County/xyz-3-agents-swarm/issues/83) → [GH-83-README-ONBOARDING-UX.md](PROJECT/1-INBOX/GH-83-README-ONBOARDING-UX.md), parked in ROADMAP.

- **One operator path.** The top used to fire four "first" moves at once (read `ROUTER.md`, run `./validate.sh`, follow a 4-step "Start here", eye `install.sh`). Replaced with: plain-language two-layer value prop → **one** Step 1 (`./validate.sh`, no accounts) → an explicit three-way branch (live relay / kernel / install) → a 4-line glossary (`tick`, relay, Marathon, agy).
- **Scoped the `ROUTER.md` pointer as agent-facing** ("editing this repo as an agent"), not the human first move — ROUTER self-declares it's the AI-agent entry point and README the human overview, so sending a human there first was the wrong surface (both models).
- **Direct auth pointer** — the "per-CLI auth: see Start here below" indirection now links straight to [relay-automation/README.md#headless-bring-up-codex--agy](relay-automation/README.md#headless-bring-up-codex--agy) (agy's catch).
- **De-brittled the test count (freshness fix).** README hard-coded `47/47` twice; the suite is actually `80/80`. Docs disagreeing with reality = the docs are the bug (GUIDING-PRINCIPLES #9). Removed the hard-coded count entirely — the Quickstart now says the suite "prints its own pass count", so it can't rot again.
- **Moved install/kernel mechanics below the concept sections** (was: "Install into another repo" appeared before "What `tick` is").

**Harness finding (not shipped, surfaced for triage):** `test/runner-loop.sh` is **non-hermetic** — it passes `README.md` as the runner's artifact path, and `runner.sh`'s dirty-tree guard (`git -C "$ROOT_DIR" diff --quiet -- README.md`) resolves `$ROOT_DIR` to the **real repo**, so the test fails with `runner: artifact paths have unstaged changes` whenever the real `README.md` has uncommitted edits (as it did mid-rewrite). Same test-isolation-leak class as GH-74/GH-44. Committing README (clean tree) makes it pass; `validate.sh` green post-commit. Candidate follow-up: point the fixture at a synthetic artifact path or bind the guard to `TICK_REPO_ROOT`.

- **GH-56** (`d679fc4`) — `marathon-drive.sh` now runs `reconcile_relay_task()` before seeding a phase token: it **dies on a live claim** (never reaps one), clears a stale `open` handoff routed to the builder/reviewer, else proceeds. The committed file is valid (`bash -n` clean) and the gate passes standalone; the relay had already reached Approved. No effect on the landed fix.
- **GH-59** (`ac6dacb`) — containment-kernel fix: `rtl_in_allow` now allows a git-collapsed untracked-directory entry (`dir/`) when it is a **true ancestor** of a concrete allowlisted **file** entry (`greenfield/` ⊂ `greenfield/output.txt`), never a bare prefix (`green/` still fails for `greenfield/output.txt`) — generalizing the `.relay-artifacts` exemption; case-insensitive variant mirrored, bash-3.2-portable. **Retires the empty-stub-directory workaround** for every greenfield-artifact-in-a-new-dir marathon lane (the GH-61 case). [decision](decisions/2026-07-02-offlane-untracked-dir.md) records the contract + three invariants; `test/worktree-isolation.sh` enforces all three (greenfield-fixed / off-lane-still-rejected / no copy-back widening — 23/0). ### GH-61 Tier 1 CI SHIPPED via marathon dogfood — and reproduced #59 live
Fired the GH-61 Tier 1 lane through `marathon-drive.sh` (codex builder + agy reviewer) on branch `marathon/gh-61-ci-github-actions-2026-07-02`; STATUS **Approved** after 2 turns, pre-advance gate green, merged `--no-ff` to main (`d9b8a14`). `validate.sh` **80/80** (was 79 + the new `ci-workflow` test).

Steps: install+run `shellcheck -S error` on tracked `*.sh`, `bash -n` on tracked `*.sh`, `node --check` on `src/*.js`+`bin/*.js`, `.claude/settings*.json` JSON-validate, and `utils/pdda/pdda.sh run`. **Tier 2 (running `./validate.sh` in CI) intentionally deferred** — it carries an unresolved `macos-latest` vs `ubuntu-latest` runner decision reserved for the operator, so #61 stays open for Tier 2.

### GH-75 SHIPPED — XYZ.json final-completion telemetry at every harness session end
All three harnesses (relay, marathon, swarm) now append a durable, newest-first completion record to a gitignored `XYZ.json` at the harness repo root — the live per-session signal GH-24's on-demand batch extractor never provided. Schema extends GH-24's `{health, title, description, updatedAt}` with `{harness, sessionId}`.

- **Shared health mapping** — `utils/telemetry/health-lib.sh` factors GH-24's STATUS/VERDICT→health (green/orange/red) `case` block out of `extract-relay-telemetry.sh`; the extractor now sources it. Verified **byte-identical** extractor output over the full corpus before/after — no GH-24 regression.
- **Shared writer** — `utils/telemetry/append-xyz-completion.sh <harness> <sessionId> <health> <title> <description>` does a **locked** (GH-72 `mkdir` advisory lock + empty-pid-TOCTOU handling — prevents a lost update when two sessions finish in the same second) + **atomic** (temp-file + `os.replace` — prevents a truncated/partial file if killed mid-write; a corrupt/absent file self-heals to a fresh array) read-modify-write-prepend. Always writes to the harness repo root, never a `--target-root` foreign repo.
- **Relay** — `relay-drive.sh` emits at its terminal exits: `green` on Approved/Closed, `orange` on Escalated handback (both loop-top and post-turn), `red` on the round-cap fallback. Silent when nested (`XYZ_HARNESS_CONTEXT` set).
- **Marathon/swarm** — `marathon-drive.sh` has its own per-run hook (swarm invokes it directly, not via `marathon.sh`): fires for a bare run (`harness:"marathon"`) or a swarm-preflight-originated run (`harness:"swarm"`, self-propagated by prefixing the generated `marathon-invocation.txt` command with `XYZ_HARNESS_CONTEXT=swarm`), and stays silent under `marathon-phase`. `marathon.sh` sets `XYZ_HARNESS_CONTEXT=marathon-phase` per phase and emits a single whole-run `harness:"marathon"` record — so an N-phase run produces exactly one record, never N/N+1. Marathon/swarm health is binary green/red.
- **Tests** — `test/xyz-completion.sh` (prepend order, 25 sequential, 16-way concurrency no-lost-update, corrupt-file self-heal, arg validation, health-lib table), `test/xyz-harness-hooks.sh` (real relay-drive green/orange/red + nested-silent; bare/swarm/marathon-phase marathon-drive; marathon.sh N-phase = 1 record), and a swarm-tag assertion in `test/swarm-preflight.sh`. `XYZ.json` added to `.gitignore`; `ROUTER.md` routing hint added. `relay-pkg.tar.gz` regenerated (make-pkg) so the packaged `relay-drive.sh` carries the hook. `validate.sh` green (pre-existing macOS `._*`-tarball `path-integrity` drift resolved as a side effect of the regen; `registry-lock-concurrency` is an install.sh load-flake, passes in isolation).

**Bet:** the writer's health/title/description are computed by each caller at its terminal exit rather than re-derived from the file, because the exit path already knows the outcome authoritatively — cheaper and avoids a second parse. Reversibility: Easy — additive scripts + best-effort call sites (a telemetry failure never changes a harness's own exit code), no kernel/schema/event-shape change.

**Follow-up (PR review, 3 gaps found by running the code, all fixed + covered):**
- **Blocker — a `marathon.sh`-orchestrated HALT emitted zero records.** Each phase is silenced (`marathon-phase`) and the whole-run record was only written on the success tail, which an early halt skips — so a failed orchestrated run was *silently absent* (worse than a bare `marathon-drive` halt, which emits red). Fixed: `marathon.sh` now emits a single `red` whole-run record on the halt path too (`"halted at phase K of M (id) — <reason>"`, reason mapped from the phase exit code). Test `test/xyz-harness-hooks.sh` M6.
- **Should — `relay-drive.sh --review-once` never emitted** (its three early exits bypass the loop's emit sites), despite being this repo's recommended one-shot review flow. Fixed: approval→green, changes-requested handback→orange, stall→red. Same class of bypass on the multi-round no-progress exit — also now emits red. Tests RO1–RO3 + R5.
- **Should — swarm/bare-marathon records carried a constant `sessionId:"p1"`** (the default `PHASE_ID`; swarm-preflight's generated invocation passed no `--phase-id`), making the field useless for telling swarm runs apart. Fixed telemetry-only (no change to phases-dir / tick-task naming): `marathon-drive` honors an optional `XYZ_SESSION_ID` override, and `swarm-preflight`'s generated command self-propagates the per-run slug via `XYZ_SESSION_ID=<slug>`. Tests M7 + the swarm-preflight invocation assertion.

`validate.sh` 79/79 after the fixes (`test/xyz-harness-hooks.sh` grew 31→47 checks).
